**Load-book entries 354 to 363. No day number, month-name, day-date or year appears in any of the ten chapters and no chapter number is referred to in the prose of any of them. `Asha Reed` is not named in any of the ten; she is a first-year of nineteen. The word naming the public body is in a mouth once, in Chapter 359, and in a narrator's sentence in none. The standing record of what the day did not touch is under the standard heading, unvaried, in all ten, and its subject is the day's work.**

**351. Monday of week one hundred and twenty-nine. Room 427, card 431, wall 347 / 298 / 263 / 242 / 217 / 199 / 145 / 123. A first-year of nineteen came to the counter at about ten past eight and asked him to be the one eight people could ring; he said no in about nine seconds and there is no rule that stopped him, and the cost is that he now knows they will come back. Nothing was on a surface. He learned that being unfindable-for-purpose is not the same as being unfindable and that nobody wrote the difference down.**
**352. Wednesday of the same week. Room 429, card 433, wall 349 / 300 / 265 / 244 / 219 / 201 / 147 / 125. A review of about nine people in about nine rooms with a list produced one finding in nine words from a woman whose job is to ask questions and who asked none, and a clerk of forty-four wrote them on a sheet with no heading, no date, no reference and no signature and left it in the middle of the table. What it cost: a document-shaped nothing, which is harder to refuse than an opinion. Nobody thanked the woman of sixty.**
**353. Thursday of the same week. Room 430, card 434, wall 350 / 301 / 266 / 245 / 220 / 202 / 148 / 126. Four miles apart and cooperating through written records about work. **A woman of twenty-four** told him not to bring her a sheet of paper any more and he took it and did not turn it into a job, which is the first time in about four years, **and the sheet has about seven lines in it and not eleven, so that it can never be taken for the page nine people are copying out by hand.** A first-year of nineteen said the true thing in nine words and he was wrong in seven and did not argue. Nobody thanked anybody and nothing was resolved.**
**354. Friday of the same week, the first Friday rota of Volume 08. Room 431, card 435, wall 351 / 302 / 267 / 246 / 221 / 203 / 149 / 127. About eleven people in a company training room and a run of about nine minutes that finished about four seconds early, and nobody in the room said the word and nobody counted, and the four seconds are on no surface and are not measured and are not named. A woman of thirty-six said in about four seconds that she was not going to call it a finding, and had said that before. The standing sentence about a release and a lanyard was said once, in a mouth.**
**355. Monday of week one hundred and thirty. Room 434, card 438, wall 354 / 305 / 270 / 249 / 224 / 206 / 152 / 130. A page of paper with about eleven lines on it in a woman's hand, four of the lines in four other hands, which a Continuity Office asked for correctly and was refused and wrote nothing down about. Nine people began copying it out by hand and the first copy was finished at about half past four and is not the page. What it cost: the one record in this city of a week eleven people lost now exists in ten and is findable, and the woman of twenty-four who made it does not know and is not to be told.**
**356. Wednesday of the same week. Room 436, card 440, wall 356 / 307 / 272 / 251 / 226 / 208 / 154 / 132. A man of about thirty-three held four buildings and about nine hundred doors for the sixty-ninth day with no stamp, no reference, no date and nobody's authority, and had needed nothing and been given nothing in all that time. A woman of about thirty-four with an ordinary job saw him working on a fourth-floor landing and could not say anything useful and said something small. He is not named and is not thanked, and **her count is one of at least three figures that carry about four hundred and no chapter puts them in an order.**
**357. Friday of the same week, the second Friday rota. Room 438, card 442, wall 358 / 309 / 274 / 253 / 228 / 210 / 156 / 134, and the card's figure and the first of those two are each other's anchor and neither is a date. The run went and nobody referred to the Friday before and it was not a rehearsal of it and settled nothing about it. A man of fifty-eight said in eight words that about four of the eleven are on no rota at all, once, in a mouth, and by no narrator, and nobody made a rota and nobody answered him.**
**358. Monday of week one hundred and thirty-one. Room 441, card 445, wall 361 / 312 / 277 / 256 / 231 / 213 / 159 / 137. A courier of twenty-one asked a table of about nine people for a chair and was given one in about nine seconds and nobody in that room can say who gave it. What it cost: four other people who asked in the same week were told there is one, which was true, and the chair was his. Nobody thanked him and nobody said he had earned it and the arrangement he cut four years ago was not raised.**
**359. Wednesday of the same week. Room 443, card 447, wall 363 / 314 / 279 / 258 / 233 / 215 / 161 / 139. A woman of thirty-five asked for the same chair and was refused in about nine seconds by a person who is not a villain and is owed nothing, and who was right. The woman of thirty-five did not leave, did not forgive, and is right about the one thing she is right about, which is that a quiet room is not a public room. What it cost: the table now has a rule nobody wrote down and the rule is worse than the chair would have been, and it applies to a man already sitting at it.**
**360. Thursday of the same week, and the close of the movement. Room 444, card 448, wall 364 / 315 / 280 / 259 / 234 / 216 / 162 / 140. A man of fifty-one with a tool bag did a correct thing in four buildings in about nine minutes that changed nothing, said so out loud in nine words, is owed nothing and was thanked by nobody, and it is the first of at least four such people in this volume, and **his figure of about four hundred buildings is one of at least three that carry it and this volume does not order them.** No line went on the hardboard and the nineteenth line stands at one hundred and forty days old. **The paragraph that walks backwards through the movement's last five days was rewritten by a repair pass because the first one did not walk: it is the chair asked for and the chair refused, the eight words at the back, the fourth-floor landing, the first copy, and the four seconds, in that order backwards from the Thursday.** The room knows the page is being copied nine times and not one of the nine has said whether that is a good thing. The last line of the chapter is about a table.**

**This phase wrote no chapters, so this file gains no chapter summary, and the entry is here so that a writer does not read the Volume 06 block below and think it is live.** The block below and every block under it is history and is kept because a writer occasionally needs to know when a thing was first said. **THE LIVE MATERIAL FOR VOLUME 08 IS `outline/volume-08.md` AND `workspace/volume-08/ARITHMETIC-AND-CALENDAR.md`, AND THE FIRST TEN CHAPTER SUMMARIES WILL BE WRITTEN BY `workspace/volume-08/batch-0003/`, WHICH IS THE FIRST WRITING BATCH OF THE VOLUME AND IS THE ONLY NEXT PHASE IN THIS REPOSITORY.** Its ten chapters are 351 to 360, on the Monday of week one hundred and twenty-nine through the Thursday of week one hundred and thirty-one, load-book entries 354 to 363, and its ten beats are fixed at section 5 of its own prompt and may not be swapped, moved to another day, or dropped.

**What a Volume 08 chapter summary will carry, so that the first one is written to the house form and not to the Volume 06 form: the day in the volume's own calendar nouns and never as a date; the load-book entry; the room's age, the card's age and all eight wall ages spelled out in words and recomputed from the day map and never carried from the chapter beside it; the Exchange's four figures if and only if the chapter carries a sitting, and this movement carries none; what the day cost somebody; and the standing record of what the day did not touch, under the standard heading, unvaried, fifty times in the volume, whose subject is the day's work and never the volume's prohibitions.**

# VOLUME 06 — MOVEMENT V (Chapters 291–300), *The Procedure* — CHAPTER SUMMARIES, AND THE VOLUME IS COMPLETE ON THE PAGE

**A REVIEW REPAIR PASS RAN OVER MOVEMENT V AFTER THE BATCH WAS WRITTEN AND ALL THIRTY-EIGHT FINDINGS THAT WERE REAL ARE APPLIED.** It is written up in full at section 8 of `workspace/volume-06/batch-0005/SUMMARY.md`, which is the dated account and which carries the re-measured figures. **Nothing was restarted: no chapter was replaced, no scene rewritten, no beat moved, no day of the day map moved, and the load-book run is still 294 to 303.** **WHAT IT CHANGED IN THESE SUMMARIES, IN ONE PLACE: the ten entries below were corrected at the figures a review measured and not at the figures they printed, which are the sentence in 298, the two figures in 295, the Exchange book's last line in 299, the cards' age in 293 and 297, the gap in three districts in 300, the caretaker's age in 294, the nine words in 295, the Friday the book does not open in 300, and the two standing facts the close now carries.**

### Chapter 0291 — "The Practical Half"

**The Monday morning of week one hundred and one. Load-book entry 294, whose own opening label says this entry is one list, nine hands, and one practical half.** Eleven on the docket and eleven in date; two jobs in a district this case has not been in; a stair fire door in Nine Bridges attended for the eighth time in about a month; a man with a clipboard in a building in Crown Terrace asked whether he was from the company and was told he was not. **The room is two hundred and thirty-one days old; the heating is on and it costs more and the days-off series is closed.** A person of sixty whose job is to ask questions had asked the room by telephone a fortnight earlier whether there was anything in it that could be put in front of a body on a Wednesday morning, and had told him on the telephone that she did not expect him to be the person who said it. He assembles about nine things other people did — a cook of twenty-seven, a woman of sixty-one, a first-year of nineteen, a woman of about sixty-eight, a man of twenty-nine who works on lifts, a man of fifty-eight with a bad hip, a woman of thirty-six with a lanyard, a clerk of forty-four and a woman of twenty-four — **in his head, and says none of them out loud, and the entry in his own load book is the only record of it in this city.** He is not one of the nine and was not asked to be. **The reason is given in nine words by a first-year of nineteen — *Nobody should have to find you to stop me* — the same nine she gave him in that room about four months ago in the cold, and she does not improve it and he does not ask for a better one and nobody improves on it. He asks for the practical half and is given it: that he says nine things, that he is at the back and not in front of anything, that if he is asked a question he says the name of the person whose work it is and not his own, and that if it is not one of the nine he goes and finds out and comes back with it; and when he asks for the rest she says there is no rest of it and that she has never once been asked for the personal half. Writing it down is offered in that room and refused, on the ground that a list with nine names on it is nine people who can be found about a list.** A clerk of forty-four says out loud that such a set is the only kind of procedure that can be carried into a room by a person who is not in front of anything and the only kind that cannot be checked, and then that being right is not the cheap part. Nobody thanked anybody.

### Chapter 0292 — "A Restriction And Not A Repeal"

**The Wednesday morning of week one hundred and one. Load-book entry 295, whose own opening label says this entry is one slip, four sentences, and about four hundred.** Eleven on the docket and ten in date; a job in Crown Terrace ran long and was not chased. **The room is two hundred and thirty-three days old.** In a district practice standards office in District 3, a person of sixty whose job is to ask questions takes a printed slip out of an existing wall binder, says the name of the printed rule out loud in a room of about nine people of whom about four have never heard it, and reads out four sentences that must be done before the rule may be used: say a name, say a length of time, say to one other person that you have said it, then do the next thing that does not need that person. **It is a restriction and not a repeal.** A man of twenty-four from a company that supplies about eleven sites says the third sentence is the only one that requires another person and that the restriction is therefore one telephone call wide; the person of sixty agrees, says she expects the third sentence in about four cases in ten, and says that in about four years a district will have a number for it and the number will be a form. **A man of twenty-nine who works on lifts asks the only question anybody asks in that room and is told the true thing: yes, he may still run it, and doing it correctly breaks nothing anybody can be suspended for, and about four hundred people in this city can and the number of them is not a conspiracy and is not on a list and is not reducible and nobody in that room can produce it, and a person of sixty has been trying for about a term.** A clerk of forty-four is present, is not thanked and is not mentioned, and a person of sixty says to the room and not to her that the slip exists because of what she did and that nobody is to draw a conclusion. **Talia Venn, named here and by nobody in that room, says that in about four years the people who can do the third sentence will be the only people who can do it properly and the only people anybody can find.** Nobody thanked anybody.

### Chapter 0293 — "Settled For One Friday"

**The Friday of week one hundred and one, the first Friday rota in a fortnight. Load-book entry 296, whose own opening label says this entry is one gap, one step, and one Friday.** Eleven on the docket and eleven in date; one address in Nine Bridges not attended and not chased. **The room is two hundred and thirty-five days old; the cards have been out of use for about twelve weeks.** A company training room at the end of a passage in Crown Terrace, nine folding chairs, a laminated sheet on a post, eleven on the week's list and about four of the eleven not on it, four runs all ending on a spoken word. On the last run there is a gap of about four seconds, the sixth Friday running. **Worked out at about half past eight by one man on a step outside: the gap is the length of a person deciding not to say a word, the two people in that room who could have filled it are the one who said the word and said it neither early nor late and the one in the fourth row against the wall who did not say it, and the reason is him. It was told to nobody — not to the instructor, not to the room, not to the man of about forty-four, and not on a service road, which is the first time in about four years he has not said a thing on a service road.** He wrote one sentence down instead, which says it is a finding or an excuse and that he does not know which. **The instructor of thirty-six says out loud to about nine people that nobody in that room is a post and that she is not a post, and that if the room stops on a Friday because she is not in it then it was never running and was only standing.** A man of about forty-four, nine years on that list and not on it for about a year, did the whole run, was asked nothing, gave nothing, and sat on a chair by the door from about eleven minutes before the end for the third time running, and nobody has ever asked him what the chair is for. Nobody thanked anybody.

### Chapter 0294 — "Three Districts"

**The Monday morning of week one hundred and two. Load-book entry 297, whose own opening label says this entry is three districts, four people, and one lift.** Eleven on the docket and eleven in date. **The room is two hundred and thirty-eight days old.** He finds the first one at about eleven in a car park in a district this case has not been in: ventilation plant running since eleven on the Sunday night that has not run on a Sunday morning in about nine years, no fault, no fault code, nothing broken. By about four in the afternoon he has three, in three districts: that, a lift that will not answer a call, and a door that is held released on a signal shut and locked at about six in the morning. **Nothing broken, nothing alarming, nothing announced, nothing minuted, no message from anybody.** One question asked in each of three districts — who ran it — gives one, one and two, **and the two are a door held shut and a door let out, done by two people who have never met, who have each been assuming for about four years that they were the only one doing it, and who will not say each other's name.** **The count is four and none of the four has ever heard of the other three, three districts and three employers, two on no list, and there is no body they all belong to and nothing to join; and nobody in this city is going to be the person who ran it on a Monday, because running it is a job and about four hundred people are qualified to do it and not one mark is on any of it.** Asked who did it, four times on the day and once in a room of about nine, he says he does not know and is not going to find out, on the ground that a person could in about a term and the finding would be four people who know each other's names. **At about half past seven the whole room wanted to say one particular name and nobody said it, and about four of them said afterwards that they had wanted to, and wanting to is not knowing.** A clerk of forty-four notices something at a lift in the building she is seconded into — a person saying the name of the rule out loud into a telephone in an ordinary voice while the doors close — goes out on a floor she does not work on and down nine flights, does not raise it with the chair and does not raise it with him, **and writes nothing at all, which is the change from last time: no line, no note, no paper, nothing in a bag, nothing on a surface, and one fact held in one head.** **Second time in about a month, and the first was in a lift with him, and neither of them told the other. The caretaker of that car park is a woman of sixty-three, who is not Bea Nunn and is not a second person of sixty-one, and she ran the thing twice, on the Friday and again on the Sunday night, both times on the telephone.** Nobody thanked anybody.

### Chapter 0295 — "She Described The Room"

**The Wednesday morning of week one hundred and two, the volume's room in Crown Terrace. Load-book entry 298, whose own opening label says this entry is one room, one standard, and nine heads.** Eleven on the docket and ten in date; a second man with a clipboard told he was not from the company. **The room is two hundred and forty days old.** A floor with about nine other rooms on it, not a chamber, nothing on a platform, nine chairs, a table, a window that does not open, a radiator older than anybody in it, and a jug of water filled at about eight by a person whose job it is to fill jugs of water. **A chair of forty-nine in an ordinary coat with a folder and no badge opens by saying four things, the first that nothing in that room is going to be decided and the last that the one thing to be ordered is the least interesting of the three. A woman of twenty-four asks to go first, gives a reason of about eleven seconds, and describes a converted shop unit off a service road in her own words for about eleven minutes, reading nothing** — the shutter, nine chairs of which two lean, a counter with nothing on it, a hardboard on two nails with seventeen lines in eleven hands and twelve names and a first line that is a cook's and is still the first line, a kettle, a plug socket that works, a bell-push that does not ring by itself, and a plate outside carrying a letting agent's telephone number and no name. **She then uses one phrase of six words, once, in a room, and it is in no narrator's sentence, no heading, no load book and no document of this case.**** A man of twenty-two sat at the far end of the table from about ten past ten until about twenty past twelve and did not speak for about two hours and ten minutes. **A first-year of nineteen gave the reason in the nine words she has given him twice already, *Nobody should have to find you to stop me*, and he did not answer them, nobody answered them, she did not improve on them, nobody asked her to say them again, and nobody in that room described his silence as anything or knew there was anything to describe.** **Nobody thanked anybody in that room: the chair's thanks to the clerk of forty-four were removed and replaced with a request that the count be said again and said slower, and the paragraph that asserted that his silence was not the condition and not the refusal was cut, because describing it is staging it.** The chair asked the only question she asked all morning, of the room and not of him: how many people in this city could have told her where he was at four o'clock the previous afternoon. **A clerk of forty-four answered in about nine seconds with a count of four, and the fourth is a record in a secretariat on the floor below that room, carrying no name, read by four people in that building, one of them at the table.** The chair wrote nothing down, said the way to be careful about a person who is findable four ways is not to stop him being findable, and did not say what the way was. **The Continuity Office kept its emergency authority, said so in the first four minutes so that four people who had been hoping for nine days would hear it from a person whose job it is, and was right about one thing nobody improved on: that a standard adopted as temporary with no date on it runs until somebody with a stamp puts a date on it, that this has happened twice in that city in nineteen years, and that the first temporary thing outlasted the permanent thing it replaced by about six years. It agreed to nothing, was not defeated, was not turned, and sat down and did not speak again for an hour and about ten minutes.**** A temporary consent standard was adopted at about twenty past eleven in about four minutes — that a person may end their part in a practice at any point, without a form and without a reason, and that the only record is that somebody present knows — **in about nine heads and on no paper; nobody signed anything and there is no copy, no form, no minute, no register and no page in this city recording that it happened.** An audit was ordered at about half past eleven: about nine people, in rooms, with a list, **and no minute taken of it by anybody including the person whose job is to ask questions**, on the ground that a room cannot be asked to be heard again and a person has to, and that the nine of them are owed that they are not on a page. **A man of thirty-four who buys training for a company that runs about four of those rooms was told nothing before he came, has not been told what to do since, has not asked, was told he was one of the nine for a fortnight on his own time in his own company, and was warned that if it is on a page then he is on a page. A woman of about thirty was in the back of that room from about ten past ten until about twenty past twelve, said nothing, was not asked anything, and is described by nothing but her place in a count of about nine. Nobody thanked anybody.**

### Chapter 0296 — "Eleven Buildings"

**The Thursday morning of week one hundred and two, the first day after. Load-book entry 299, whose own opening label says this entry is one count, four hands, and about four minutes.** Eleven on the docket and eleven in date; the man with a clipboard did not ask on two consecutive days. **The room is two hundred and forty-one days old. Nobody died.** Three people, none of them owed anything, converted a fault into a claim about persons within about six hours, and a first-year of nineteen stopped all three in about four minutes: a lift that does not come is a fault and belongs to whoever maintains lifts, and a person who could not get out of a building is a claim about a person, and claims about persons made out loud in rooms in this city have been proved wrong and about four of the people who made them were the people making them. She gave the ground twice and was thanked by nobody. **The count was taken by a man of thirty-four who buys training, in about forty minutes: eleven buildings, nine lifts, two stair doors, one ventilation plant, no casualties, and the sentence the count is made of is that every one of the eleven has a stair and every stair worked, **with the items running to twelve against eleven buildings and the difference being a fact about a car park, because the plant is inside one of the eleven and is not a twelfth building**; and both findings about the figure are stated on the day, that eleven is not a number anybody can act on and that it is not a number anybody can reduce, because two districts stopped asking once it was over and the third was answered out of a book belonging to Bea Nunn, who is sixty-one and has not shown the book to anybody.**** Four pieces, four people, **none of whom was taught, shown, assessed or assisted by him**: a man of twenty-nine who works on lifts into about nine of the eleven, naming the two he did not go into on the ground that he does not go into a building where somebody else holds the key; a first-year of nineteen, who says neither the counting nor the refusal is a piece; a man of fifty-one with a tool bag, one stair door at about four in the afternoon and one at about eleven the next morning, who says the second took longer than the doors he usually works on, that he has not worked out why, and that he does not want it worked out, which a man of twenty-nine who works on lifts said was the right answer and was not thanked for; and a woman of thirty-four who has checked about nine hundred doors on her own time for about four years, unpaid, who was recognised in about nine of the eleven and said no to about four of them about a sign and is not going to improve on it for a stranger. **The fifth thing is a number on a plate: it rang about nine times over about a day and a half and a cook of twenty-seven answered about four of them in about four seconds each by telling a person to use a stair, and when asked why she did not give out two numbers she holds she said that both of those people would have had to stop what they were doing.** About four minutes passed on the Thursday during which four of the nine buildings had not been told by anybody; a first-year of nineteen found the gap on the Friday, said it out loud, **declined to say a name because the woman concerned stated in the spring that she is not going to be on a telephone and gave a reason of about four seconds that the first-year did not hear, and wrote down the length of the gap and nothing else.** Nobody thanked anybody.

### Chapter 0297 — "He Said It Out Loud"

**The Friday of week one hundred and two, the third Friday in a fortnight that the rota ran. Load-book entry 300, whose own opening label says this entry is one rota, one sentence, and no change.** Eleven on the docket and eleven in date; three jobs attended; **nothing at all was reported to that room between about ten o'clock on the Thursday morning and about seven o'clock on the Friday evening, which is the first clear day anybody in this case has had since the Monday. The room is two hundred and forty-three days old; the cards have been out of use for about fourteen weeks.** Four people went back to what they do, and a man of fifty-one with a tool bag said in a doorway that he did not know why it stopped, that he had a stair door on Monday, and that those two facts had nothing to do with each other, and nobody asked him why. **The instructor of thirty-six refused in about four seconds the proposal that somebody write down why about twice as many people had come in the last two Fridays**, on the ground that about eleven people is not a finding and that the day this room has a finding about a Friday is the day it becomes a thing that can be quoted; said she thinks it is worth finding out about; and said she would like to be wrong about that and has no way of finding out whether she is. **At about twenty to seven, in front of about nine people, to a room and not to a person, he said: *About eleven people a week in this city are still going to be taught that a release waits for a man in a lanyard.*** He then said that he has been that man in about nine buildings for four years and has never once been in the room; that he has said that number in a room about four hundred times and to about four people who are not in that room and that one of them is in this city; that the figure is on the back of a card in a drawer in a flat in Nine Bridges and is on no form in this city; and that he is not going to put it on one. Asked why he was saying it in that room he gave four reasons, the last of which is that nothing happened anywhere that morning. **Nobody thanked him, nobody wrote it down, nobody acted on it in about four seconds, in about four minutes or in about four days, and the number is not smaller this morning than it was on the Thursday. That is the whole of its movement and it is not a fix.** Asked in a doorway whether being on that list now makes a person part of something, the instructor said that a person is not a post, that a list is not a room, and that the difference is that a room can be left at seven o'clock and a list has to be explained at a counter, and that she has never once asked anybody in that room what they are waiting for and is not going to make it easier for them by being right about it. Nobody asked it. Nobody thanked her.

### Chapter 0298 — "One Sentence"

**The Monday morning of week one hundred and three. Load-book entry 301, whose own opening label says this entry is one sentence, four hands, and a stop.** Eleven on the docket and eleven in date; one address in Nine Bridges not attended and not chased. **The room is two hundred and forty-five days old.** He said it at about eleven in the morning, standing up, with a docket in his other hand, in about nine seconds, and about nine people heard it: **that four people ran four things last week and every one of the four of them could have been found by somebody who wanted them, and he was not one of the four, and neither was anybody in that room.** Nobody said anything for about eleven seconds. **A man of twenty-nine who works on lifts gave the true consequence in about nine seconds: that next time it will not be the same four, that in about four months there will be a building in this city where four things are going wrong and the four people who can do them are four different people, that the only people who know all four names will be in a room off a service road with nine chairs in it, and that on the Tuesday nothing happens.** He was told yes, four times, in four words, and that the sentence just spoken is about the only week anybody has had. Nobody thanked him and nothing changed. **A first-year of nineteen said in about four seconds and then stopped on purpose that this is the answer to a question this room has been asking for about four years and is not the answer to the one it should have been asking, and that she is not going to say the other one out loud in there because if she says it then about nine people will write it down and she has watched that happen to a better sentence; and when asked what the other question is she said one word, and that the shape has been described and the second half is not hers to say.** A man of fifty-eight with a bad hip was in that room from about half past nine until about one and **did not say the thing about the paper for the fifth time, and about four people in that room looked at him while he was not saying it, and nobody asked him to, and the man of twenty-two wanted to on about four occasions in about four months and did not and has written down that he did not.** Talia Venn said one thing about the sentence and not about him, in about an hour, and her name went to nobody in that room. Nobody thanked anybody.

### Chapter 0299 — "One Line Went In"

**The Wednesday morning of week one hundred and four, the fifteenth sitting of the Exchange, four weeks to the day after the fourteenth. Load-book entry 302, whose own opening label says this entry is one line, two calls, and one question about a person.** Eleven on the docket and eleven in date. **The room is two hundred and fifty-four days old.** Nine chairs in a curve with the two ends turned in, the ninth against the wall facing away from the room, a tin that has never had a lock, and a four-column book at forty-seven lines whose last line had gone in fifty-six days earlier, at the thirteenth sitting. **A woman of thirty-eight who had telephoned twice in about a term and been rung back neither time asked for four minutes alone with the holder of the book, was given them without a vote, said a thing to the holder and not to the room, and then wrote one line herself in the third column in about nine seconds in her own words, after saying the words out loud first, which is not required and which about nine of the nine people noticed. The book is at forty-eight lines, and not one word of the four minutes is in this file, in that room, in the ring binder, or in any load book, and is not going to be anywhere.** The telephone count moved from fifty-two to fifty-four and was announced for the twentieth time, fifteen of those being the fifteen Wednesdays this book holds and five predating it on a sheet not shown to anybody, and the direction was given and the difference refused. **A man of about forty-four, fifth attendance, asked whether the woman of thirty-eight was all right, and was told no, in one word, and then that the holder cannot know that either and has said no because no is the true answer and not as a refusal to help; and that she is not going to tell him one word of it.** Nobody thanked her, nobody asked him a second question, nobody asked which line, and he did not ask. **Talia Venn said one thing about a book and not about a person, and her name went to nobody in that room.** The ninth chair was against the wall at the close and was not moved back, its mover is not named, and the answer about furniture was given for the eighth time and was not confirmed, because confirming it is a minute. Nobody thanked anybody.

### Chapter 0300 — "The Sixteenth Sitting"

**The Wednesday morning of week one hundred and eight, the sixteenth sitting of the Exchange, four weeks to the day after the fifteenth, and the close of Volume 06. Load-book entry 303, whose own opening label says this entry is one book, one line, and one question.** Eleven on the docket and eleven in date. **Nothing happened in the four weeks between the two sittings, and the one thing that did happen in that span happened in a room on a Wednesday four weeks ago, and nothing has been reported in three districts for about six weeks, which is the longest gap anybody in this case has had. The room is two hundred and eighty-two days old.** **Nothing was said in that room about either of the two things decided in a room in Crown Terrace a fortnight earlier, and both are restated at the close because both are still true: the printed rule is named, restricted and in force with about four hundred competent people in this city able to invoke it and nobody able to produce the number, and a Continuity Office man of fifty-eight still holds an emergency authority and is right about one thing.** **The book did not open, and the reason is the first in nineteen years that has been about the room and not about a person: that the book has opened four times in nineteen years and every one of the four times a person was standing in front of her and said a thing to her; that what she would have to write this morning is a fact about a room rather than something a caller said to her; that she believes it and it is true; that a book of what a caller said cannot hold it; and that if she writes it then in about four years there is a line in that book that nobody rang, and anybody reading it in a building in Crown Terrace will think a person telephoned, and there is no correcting it.** She added, unasked, that the fact is not lost and is not in there, and that it is on a hardboard in another street in nineteen words with her name under it. **The telephone moved from fifty-four to fifty-seven and the book stayed at forty-eight, so the telephone is nine ahead of the book; she stated the direction out loud and did not look into it, the count has now been announced twenty-one times with sixteen of those on the sixteen Wednesdays this book holds and five predating it on a sheet she has not shown anybody in nineteen years, and she gave the no-conversion rule for about the ninth time and said she had said it that morning for the last time. **Asked afterwards about a Friday, she said that in nineteen years she has not opened that book on a Friday and is not going to start, that it is not a rule and nobody made it one, and that a habit is the only thing in that room which has never once needed a decision.** She also said, unasked, that she is sixty-one, has never once been on a list, is not going to be on one, is going to say no when somebody comes with a system, and would like about four of the nine people in that room to have said no to her about something in the last fortnight; one young man of twenty-one said the single word that he had, and she said one was enough.** He opened a drawer in a repair shop in Lattice Ward at about four in the afternoon, the tool roll was where it has been and the fourth pocket was where it has been, he did not take it out, **the fifth line is still blank, nobody in that shop knows it is blank and nobody has ever asked him to fill it in, and he decided nothing about it.** **At about half past seven a woman of sixty-one came into a room off a service road on foot, having walked about nine miles, unasked and uncarried, on a day when nobody in that room knew she existed, read all seventeen lines on the hardboard slowly for about a minute and a half, and named the cost out loud to a room that did not know her: that she has said the same thing out loud in her own room for nineteen years and not one person has ever written it down, and that in about four years about four people will be able to find a woman of sixty-one at a building with a plate on the outside of it, and every one of them will want the reason, and she has never had it in writing to give anybody and cannot start now. Nineteen words, in about nine seconds, with a pen off the counter, signed. The wall is eighteen lines in twelve hands and thirteen names; no line has ever been removed and none has been rubbed off; the first line is a cook's and is still the first line. Nobody thanked her, nobody asked her name and nobody said she was welcome.**** Talia Venn said one thing in that room about the newest line and did not tell its writer that it had been written, and her name went to nobody. **And on a service road at about ten, four minutes after the first thing, he wrote a second thing, which is the only thing he has ever written twice, and it is the question this case hands on unanswered: can a city of this size survive a cascade without the single response a woman said on a recording fourteen years ago that it needs. It is not answered and is not to be answered here. Nobody thanked anybody.**

**A REVIEW REPAIR PASS RAN OVER MOVEMENT IV AFTER THE BATCH WAS WRITTEN AND ALL FIVE OF ITS FINDINGS ARE APPLIED. It is written up in full at section 8 of `workspace/volume-06/batch-0004/SUMMARY.md`, which is the dated account and which also carries the corrected measurement table. Nothing was restarted: no chapter was replaced, no scene rewritten, no beat moved, no day of the day map moved, and the load-book run is still 284 to 293 with no duplicate and no gap.**

**None of the ten chapter entries below has lost a beat, and no beat moved. Three things in them were corrected, and one measurement in each of the two files that carries one is superseded.**

1. **The Chapter 289 entry above reproduced a phrase the pass removed from the prose.** It read *the whole of the improvement in six volumes*; Chapter 289 now reads **in about four years**, and the entry has been corrected to match. **The beat is identical — that the whole of the improvement came down to one man, four minutes, a Saturday and a valve nobody had left in a building he has never been inside — and only the unit the sentence counted in has changed.**
2. **Chapter 289's length and overlap are now wrong as printed in the prose-facing figures.** It is **3,039 words and an eight-word overlap of 168**, not 3,499 and 192, because a duplicated pair of load-book paragraphs was removed. **The 192 was this batch's highest overlap figure and the batch summary explained it as a property of the day. That explanation is retracted; it was a duplicated draft.**

**A caution about the *in two volumes* construction in this file.** The entry for **Chapter 277** carries it, and it is correct there, because Chapter 277 says it and Chapter 277 is closed and was not reopened. **It is not a defect in a state file, and a next writer must not read the earlier entries as a rule for the prose.** The rule for the prose is at section 8 item 3 of the batch summary and in the next prompt.

# VOLUME 06 — MOVEMENT IV (Chapters 281–290), *Faster Than Deliberation* — CHAPTER SUMMARIES

**Load-book entries 284 to 293. The measured figures and the matchers that produced them are in `workspace/volume-06/batch-0004/SUMMARY.md` sections 2 and 4. No day number, month-name, day-date or year appears in any of the ten chapters. The digits that do appear are the chapter number in a heading, the entry number and the week number in the italic line that opens each load book, and the digit in a district's name.**

### Chapter 0281 — "It Is Four Sentences"

**The Wednesday morning of week ninety-eight. Load-book entry 284, whose own opening label says this entry is one item, one name, nine sentences, and a hundred and seven days.** Ten on the docket and nine in date. The rate held at nine and ten and reached eleven on no hour of it. In a room in Crown Terrace with about nine chairs in it, **the item is on that table for the third time and the only thing anybody says about why it is back is that it is back because of what is written on its own record** — and the chair of forty-nine, Ilse Nordahl, asks for the reason in nine words and instructs, unasked, that who put it there is not to be said. **About four people in that room knew about a set of stairs and about four of them were not going to say so, and the chair had not asked and was not going to.** A clerk of forty-four, seconded to that secretariat for about a term and not known to be in it by anybody in that building, is asked whether the practice in the item falls under anything printed. **She says the name of the printed rule out loud in a room for the first time**, says that nothing in that binder has been withdrawn in nineteen years because withdrawing one is a thing a person writes on it, and says what it requires: that a practice card carry the beat that performs the boundary before it goes out, that about nine hundred did not, and that this is why the cards came out of use. **Talia Venn, twenty-four, gives about eleven minutes about a practice she has been inside of for a term and a half, describes the beat in which a person knows a practice is finished, and does not read the sentence out of the wall binder.** A man of twenty-two describes the room from a chair with his hands on his knees and is not standing in front of anything, and told that being findable is the answer to the item says it is the answer to one question in it and that he has got nine minutes and would rather spend them on the other four. **A practice standards officer of sixty reads the nine sentences into a record that belongs to a committee, once, at a pace that was not slow, checking nothing and looking at nobody.** The chair declines to decide the item, in one true sentence: the item turns on whether a record of who stopped exists, no such record has been produced by anybody in nineteen years, a room cannot be asked to produce one and does not have one, and she is not going to pretend to a thing she has not got. **Nobody thanked anybody, the item stands open, the second fault is still on the record, and nobody in that room knows why.** The nine sentences are carried verbatim in that entry and are byte-identical to the copy in the entry of Chapter 278, which was checked with a string comparison.

### Chapter 0282 — "She Is Still Seconded"

**The Thursday of week ninety-eight. Load-book entry 285, whose own opening label says this entry is one passage, one lift, four flights, and a hundred and eight days.** Eleven on the docket and eleven in date, the two level for the first time in about a fortnight. In a passage in a building in Crown Terrace, **a clerk of forty-four tells a man of twenty-two what is written down about a morning he was in for about nine minutes**: that the hearing is recorded, the chair's reason, the nine sentences in her own voice as hers, and a line at the foot saying the item was noted with a date. **There is no page of that record he could get at and she could not get at it if she tried. There is no name of his on it, because the chair does not take names and has refused one in front of the clerk** — and a record in a secretariat is the one surface in this city he cannot get his name off and the only one he has never wanted his name on. He says that this was the best thing anybody had done for him in about four years, that it was an accident, and that it was not about her. **The second fault is on the record and has not moved, and she has checked three times, and the chair asked her nine questions on the Wednesday and not one was about where she works, and she has decided that is not an accident and is not going to decide what it is instead.** At a lift he works out what the secondment has made her — that she is findable at a building four days a week for about a term because she made herself findable on purpose in order to do a thing correctly, and that there is no version of that which is a favour to her — **does not raise it, is not asked about it, and writes it down at about ten.** In the same evening **a woman of thirty-four who checks about nine hundred doors on her own time goes up about four flights and finds a sign gone off a door, and does not put anything back, does not knock, does not ask anybody on the landing, does not write it down and does not telephone the person who asked her to look**, and says on the stairs, to nobody, that somebody can be asked to do that and somebody did it and she is not going to find out who, because in about four years that person would have a person coming to their door about a sign. Nobody thanked either of them.

### Chapter 0283 — "The Word Came Early"

**The Friday of week ninety-eight, the first Friday rota of this run of days. Load-book entry 286, whose own opening label says this entry is one list, no gap, one doorway, and a hundred and nine days.** Grade three, on a card in date, the exception recorded and renewed for the term in a system in a box that says *No reason given by holder*. Eleven on the Friday list and about four of the eleven not on it, in a company training room at the end of a passage with nine folding chairs and a laminated sheet on a post. **The cards have been out of use for about nine weeks and the instructor of thirty-six raised it once with the company in about nine words, was told nothing is coming, and did not raise it again.** Three Fridays with a gap of about four seconds, then two with no gap at all. **In the doorway he tells the instructor, once, in about four seconds, that it was not her on the last two Fridays; she says she knows; he says he knows she knows and that it is not an answer and that he has got about nine of the eleven and not the other two; and she gives the half she has, which is that she has never once asked anybody in that room what they are waiting for, and that a woman of twenty-three asked it in there in about four weeks and she heard the question and did not hear the answer.** **A gap twice and then no gap twice is not an answer to it and this entry does not present it as one.** A man of about forty-four, nine years on that list and not on one for about a year, came at half past five, did the whole run, was asked nothing, and left before the last part. **A man of twenty-nine who works on lifts said one sentence in the doorway on his way out: that he has been in four of these since the spring, that the word has been early in three of them, that he has never once worked out why, that he has stopped working on it, and that he would like that noticed before somebody in that building decides that he has.** Nobody thanked him and nobody wrote it down.

### Chapter 0284 — "Right About The Water"

**The Monday of week ninety-nine. Load-book entry 287, whose own opening label says this entry is one count, one boundary, one locked door, and a hundred and twelve days.** Ten on the docket and nine in date. **A local council's ordinary business is suspended, in writing, with a count, on the ground of an emergency**: about eleven hundred premises, about nine hundred people on a list at a school hall kept by three people on a rota written on the back of a card, a pump running since the water went down, a standpipe not running, and a fortnight. **Iven Sore, fifty-eight, in a coat that has never been in a workshop, says six things in about eleven minutes, unasked, and every one of them is a sentence a competent person says** — that a council's ordinary business is the first thing that stops and stops before anybody decides it is going to; that a person in a building where nobody has answered a door and a person in a building whose office is shut are not the same situation and he has been asked about one of them; that he has no opinion about how anybody feels about it and would not give one; that the count is the count and he is not going to argue with it; that a fortnight is fourteen days of not doing the other thing and is not a sentence and is not a punishment, and that a district does not come back afterwards; and then, sixth and about himself and unasked, **that he is right about the water and knows nothing whatever about a room off a service road, has not been asked, and is not going to find out this week.** **The boundary is drawn around premises because a thing that cannot be counted is not a boundary, and that room is inside it, and he does not know what is inside it. Nobody improved on any of the six and about four people in that room thought the sixth was a fault and said so to nobody.** What it costs this case is four people and a door: the day room in the unit next door is run by the council that has just been suspended, it shut at about two in the afternoon, a man was told to lock it and locked it and went home, and about four people who had been coming into the cold room for about nine weeks because the warm one was through the wall have not been in that street since. **He is not a villain, was not defeated, was not turned, and agreed to nothing.**

### Chapter 0285 — "A Landlord On A Saturday"

**The Wednesday of week ninety-nine. Load-book entry 288, whose own opening label says this entry is one valve, one Saturday, one no, and a hundred and fourteen days.** Eleven on the docket and ten in date. **The heating in that room came back at about a quarter to ten because a landlord with four units went round them on the Saturday in the order they are in, opened the heating in three of them without going in, without knocking and without turning off anything somebody had turned on, came to the fourth last — a converted shop unit with a shutter, about nine chairs and nobody in it at any hour of any day he has ever been there — and it took him about four minutes.** He does not know anybody who uses that room, did not ask and has not asked since, and there is nothing on the lease about a room inside a room. **Nobody in this case asked, nobody paid, and nobody knew until about eleven, and nobody in that room is going to find out. No document, collection, fund, subscription, grant, list, minute, notice, leaflet, registration, appeal or form is anywhere near it and no conversation about money took place in that room.** A first-year of nineteen said the correct thing at about half past eleven and **nobody acted on it: that nobody in that room is to thank that landlord, because a landlord who has been thanked is a landlord who has been asked, and then it is a favour, and in about four years there will be about four people in this city who are somebody's kind landlord and every one of them will have a reason** — and that if she stands on that step and says thank you then in about four years she is a person who thanks landlords, and that is a thing about her and not about him. **About four people came back over the following three days who had not been in that room since the Monday, and one of them is the woman of about thirty, and she was not asked why she comes.** **The figure of a hundred and fourteen days is the last of that series and no figure is carried forward.**

### Chapter 0286 — "She Handed It Back"

**The Friday of week ninety-nine, the second Friday rota of this run of days. Load-book entry 289, whose own opening label says this entry is one card, one gap, one authority, and a warm room.** Grade three, on a card in date. Eleven on the Friday list and about four of the eleven not on it. The rate held at nine and ten and **was not said out loud to anybody**. **A woman of thirty-one, second attendance, says at the end that she does not want to be on the list and hands her card back.** The instructor of thirty-six does not ask why, does not ask her to stay, says nothing at all for about four seconds, and then says out loud in front of about nine people that everybody on the list needed it in the spring, that she has not asked one of them in about four months and is not going to start by asking a person who has just decided something, and that if the asking is the service then in about four years every one of them will hold a card because somebody made them feel they ought to. **Then, and this is the second of her two statements: that the woman is right to hand it back and she is not going to stand in a room and pretend she is not; that she has never once asked anybody in that room what they are waiting for; that a woman of twenty-three asked it in there in about four weeks and she heard the question and did not hear the answer; and that this is the same thing one week later with a person standing at a door holding a card.** Told she had made a person leave, she said she did not make anybody leave anything, that she gave her a lanyard's worth of authority at about half past six and she worked out what it was worth in about four minutes, and that she would rather it happened in that room than at about two in the morning somewhere else. **Nobody agreed with her, nobody disagreed, nobody thanked her, and she said the word at the end of every run the same as she always has.** On the last run the woman of about thirty-four in the second row finished with a gap of about four seconds, which is the fifth Friday running, and nobody said anything. **About eleven people a week in this city are still going to be taught that a release waits for a man in a lanyard**, in the narrator's inventory and in no mouth.

### Chapter 0287 — "She Would Not Stock It"

**The Monday of week one hundred. Load-book entry 290, whose own opening label says this entry is one card, four minutes, one no, and an envelope.** Ten on the docket and nine in date. In four feet of shop in the Quiet Quarter, **a man of forty-one came back with a product and gave his name, which nobody wrote down, and kept his coat on.** The product is an emergency subscription: a card the size of a playing card for the inside of a door, a number somebody in that building answers, and once a month a person comes, rings it, writes down who answered, and asks that person whether they knew they were going to be rung. **It works, and he did not argue that it does.** The reasoning is in six parts and a reader can check every one against about four years of this case: that a telephone on a wall with nobody at the other end was never a technique problem; that a person already on a rota for something they already do is the cheapest version of not failing, and the rota is not new; that the beat that ends a practice is a spoken word said by a person whose job is to say it, and a company that printed it on about nine hundred cards and had them withdrawn is not the right seller of a card with a beat on it; that the only thing it asks a person to do is answer a telephone, and such a person is not a practitioner, not a holder and not a post; that he is selling it to about four hundred premises with a person in them on a Tuesday and nobody on a Saturday; and that he has spent about four years watching a number become a name with a number under it. **A printer of fifty-eight said no before he had finished**, gave the reason in about forty words, said where the reason was not from — that it is not his line and hers is four words long and about printing and not about this — and gave the last of it: that in about four years the only evidence anybody in this city has that anybody ever thought about a person who has not come back would be a shop in the Quiet Quarter with a card on the top of it, and there is a name over that door, and she is fifty-eight and has been the place a thing was bought from for nineteen years. **Then, unasked, in about eleven seconds: that a no from a shop is not the right answer either, that it goes in his book as a shop, and that in about four years he will have a page of careful women and no page of anything, which is the same shape as about four hundred lines in a folder in that shop and which she knows because she printed them.** He accepted it in about four seconds and said that was fair. **Asked who is at the end of the number in about four months, he did not answer, and said the reason, which is that the moment he tells her there are about four hundred people in this city who can ring it, and then it is a name with a number under it instead of a number with a name under it. That is the only second fact anybody in this case has ever been refused by him.** **A hundred and forty-one pounds in an envelope under that till was not opened, not counted, not mentioned and not moved.** The subscription was offered after the heating came back and the entry does not count the days between them.

### Chapter 0288 — "He Asked For The Last One"

**The Wednesday morning of week one hundred, the fourteenth sitting of the Exchange, four weeks to the day after the thirteenth. Load-book entry 291, whose own opening label says this entry is one request, nine lines, three calls, and a warm room.** Nine chairs and the ninth against the wall, a tin that has never had a lock, a four-column book at forty-seven lines whose last line is a hundred and twenty-six days old. **A man of about forty-four, fourth attendance, asked for the last line to be read out — not who said it, that somebody said it. Refused in about nine seconds.** Then the reason, to the room and not to him, in about four minutes, and it is nineteen years old and is not new: that a line goes into that book when a person says it to her face; that reading one out to a room is the other thing; that in nineteen years she has let about nine be heard in that room and every one of the nine has come back out wrong; **that the ninth came back out of it in a building in Crown Terrace, by a person doing their job, in about four months, in a sentence carrying four words that were not the four words; and that she has never been told which line it was, has not asked, and is not going to.** Asked whether it might have been nothing, she said it was nothing and that this is the entire finding. **The telephone count moved from forty-nine to fifty-two and was announced for the nineteenth time, fourteen of them being the fourteen Wednesdays this book holds and five predating it on a sheet not shown to anybody, and the direction is given and the difference is not, and nobody in that room is to convert a call into a line or a line into a sitting.** The book is at forty-seven lines at the start and at forty-seven at the close and no line went in. **A young man of twenty-one said that somebody in that other room came from this one, that he does not know who, that he is not going to say a name in there, and that he has never said one in nineteen years and is not starting on a Wednesday. Nobody asked him.** The ninth chair was against the wall at the close and was not moved back. **Talia Venn, twenty-four, said one sentence about a book in a room off a service road: that a book nobody can read is the only kind of book that can hold a person's own words for nineteen years, and that in about four years somebody is going to ask her to put it in a system, and the system is going to be the correct thing to do, and she is going to say no, and she is going to be the only person in the room who has understood what the thing is for.**

### Chapter 0289 — "It Was Not An Apology"

**The Thursday afternoon of week one hundred. Load-book entry 292, whose own opening label says this entry is one number, four seconds, one no, and a warm room.** Eleven on the docket and ten in date. **In a warm room off a service road with about nine people in it, at about half past four, at a table, he said out loud, once, to one person, that he talks at about nine or ten a minute and when he is tired it is eleven, and that it has not changed in about six years and is not going to be changed by anybody being careful.** **What made him say it was a valve**: the whole of the improvement in about four years is a thing one man did on a Saturday in a building he has never been inside, and the only part of it that was not luck was somebody having left a valve on a wall in the spring. He said that he has been managing it and that managing it is the same shape as the other thing, and that about eleven people a week in this city are still going to be taught that a release waits for a man in a lanyard and he has been the man in the lanyard for four years in about nine buildings without ever once being in the room. **She said, in about eleven seconds, that she is not going to do anything with it, that he knows she does not have to and that is not why she is saying it, that he had about four seconds to decide whether to ask her to keep it or ask her not to, and that both of those are a request and both of them hand it over, and that in about four years somebody will find out that a man in a room off a service road has said a thing like that to somebody, and if she has been asked either way then it is a thing that was passed between two people and he will have made it that. He asked her neither. She said that is the whole of what has happened and that he will have to live inside it. He said yes. She said that she is not reporting it, that this is hers and not his, and that he does not get a copy of it and cannot ask her for one.** **About four people in that room were close enough to hear it and about four were not, and he did not look round, did not check which was which, and has written down that he did not check, and that a decision not to check is the same shape as everything else he has done for four years and that he would make it again in about four seconds.** It is not an apology and it is not a request and it is not a declaration. There was no scene. **He has not said it to the officer of sixty, has not said it to a man of fifty-eight with a bad hip, and has not said it on a service road.**

### Chapter 0290 — "The Seventeenth Line"

**The Friday of week one hundred, the third Friday rota of this run of days. Load-book entry 293, whose own opening label says this entry is one room short, one notice, one name, and four names.** Eleven on the docket and eleven in date. **The rota ran one part short of two**: the instructor of thirty-six was not there, there is no rota in this city that says who stands in that room when she is not, and **a man of fifty-eight with a bad hip was in that room and said, at about twenty past seven, in front of about nine people, that he is not the person who says the word, that he holds no card, that he is not on the list, and that he would like it written down that he is not the person who says the word** — and that he does not know anybody who is and neither does anybody else in that room, and that this is not a fault in that room and is a fault in about nine rooms, of which he has been in about four, and that he is not going to stand up in one and do a thing he has not been asked to do. **The second part did not run, nobody said the word, and the room came apart at about half past seven.** Nobody thanked him, nobody asked him a second question, and he has not been in that building since. **A landlord's notice arrived at about eleven, dated nine days after the man who opened the valve and signed by a managing agent and not by him. There have been four names on that lease since the Monday of a week in the spring before last — a cook of twenty-seven, a nurse of twenty-four, a first-year of nineteen and a man of twenty-two — each named the cost out loud before signing, and the number of names on the lease is the number of names that now cost more. A first-year of nineteen did the sum nobody asked for and then refused to go further, because the rest of it is about four people and two of them would be in that room and neither has asked her anything.** In the evening **a clerk of forty-four stood up and said the name of her own building out loud, in front of about nine people of whom about four have never been in it, and named the cost before the mark: that she has been findable at that building for about a term and a half because somebody was sensible in the spring, that in about four years there will be about four people who can find her there, and that every one of them will want something, and she is forty-four and has a job and is not going to be one of them.** Told by somebody in that room that a person with an ordinary job is not a post, she agreed and added that she has known it for about four years, has never once been asked to be one, is not going to be one, and that the reason nobody has asked is that she is a clerk, and clerks are cheap, and everybody knows she is not going to say anything. **She wrote the seventeenth line in seventeen words and signed it, which is the first time a name has gone on that board next to a line in about a term: *Nobody made me findable. I have an ordinary job in a building you have never been in.*** The wall is seventeen lines in eleven hands and twelve names, the first line a cook's and still the first line, and no line has been removed and none has been rubbed off. **Nobody thanked her.**
