# VOLUME 06 — MOVEMENT III (Chapters 271–280), *Who Wrote It* — CHAPTER SUMMARIES

**Load-book entries 274 to 283. The measured figures and the matchers that produced them are in `workspace/volume-06/batch-0003/SUMMARY.md` sections 2 and 4. No day number, month-name, day-date or year appears in any of the ten chapters. The digits that do appear are the chapter number in a heading, the entry number and the week number in the italic line that opens each load book, and the digit in a district's name.**

### Chapter 0271 — "Four Years Early"

**The Monday morning of week ninety-five. Load-book entry 274, whose own opening label says this entry is one fault, one record, one doorway, and eighty-four days.** Ten on the docket and ten in date; a stair fire door in Nine Bridges came up for the twelfth time in about a month. The rate held at nine and ten and reached eleven on no hour of it. In a district practice standards office in District 3, **a clerk of forty-four put the second fault in the item on a record on the Friday before, and said out loud, once, that if it comes out the note comes off, that a secondment cannot be ended by anybody in this case, and that she checked that twice, in the first week and the last week.** The fault is that a room cannot be asked to prove it can stop itself, because the only evidence of a person stopping is a record of who stopped, that this is a column, and that about eleven thousand premises have no column. **A man of fifty-eight with a bad hip said the finding out loud, unasked, to nobody: that a fault got onto a record because of a set of stairs. It is the second sentence he has said in about a fortnight and nobody asked him a second question.** The officer of sixty said that being right in that building has never once done anything and that she is not going to be thanked and would like nobody to try. **A man of twenty-two was in the outer office about eleven minutes and could have said one sentence of about nine words onto a second sheet and did not, and gave the reason on a service road: that a person who has checked twice is not somebody who is going to be caught by accident — and then that he has been findable since the spring before the one before last, that this is the first time he has been glad of it, and that he does not know what to do with that, so he did not use it.** The chair of forty-nine is named, Ilse Nordahl, and is a person with an ordinary job. In the afternoon, **in a doorway in Nine Bridges, a woman of thirty-four who checks about nine hundred doors on her own time stands in it for about four minutes and asks a woman of about sixty-eight to put a different sign up, having said what she wanted on it before she asked, on the ground that a telephone would give the person at the other end nine hours to decide whether they were the sort of person who takes signs down.** Four words go up in chalk over a glass door in about four minutes: *Nobody answers. Ring again.* The cost is hers and is not improved on and she is not thanked: eight weeks against four years, she is wrong about the four years, and the second half of it is that a woman of thirty-four now knows she can be asked and that in about four years about four of them will find her, and she has wanted that for about eight weeks and did not know it until about four o'clock that afternoon. Nobody thanked anybody for either half of the day.

### Chapter 0272 — "Four Seconds, Again"

**The Friday of week ninety-five, the first Friday rota of this rotation. Load-book entry 275: one gap of about four seconds, three Fridays running, and eighty-eight days.** Grade three, on a card in date, with the exception for the term recorded and renewed in a system in a box that says *No reason given by holder*. Eleven on the list and about four of the eleven not on it. **The cards have been out of use for about five weeks and the room ended four runs on a spoken word, and the word is not on anything because nobody is holding anything.** On the last run the woman of about thirty-four in the second row finished with a gap of about four seconds and nobody said anything. **A man of about forty-four, nine years on that list, had offered the reason twice in three weeks in a doorway; the man of twenty-two asked him for it, having said out loud that he is the man in the lanyard and that asking a stranger for a fact is a thing he does about eleven times a week for money. The answer was that he does not have it either, that he has got as far as the man of twenty-two has, and that he wants that noted because he offered it twice as though he had it.** They are two people, neither is right, and nobody thanked either. A young man of twenty-one near the door is a third person and is neither the man of fifty-one with a bad wrist nor the young woman of twenty-one in the Exchange back room, and the woman of about thirty-four in the second row is not Petula Fenn. The heating in that room is on its eighty-eighth day and the room is a hundred and ninety-three days old.

### Chapter 0273 — "The Thing Nobody Can End"

**The Monday of week ninety-six. Load-book entry 276: two women, one mother, one arrangement, and ninety-one days.** Two sisters of thirty-nine and thirty-six come into a cold room off a service road at about eleven without telephoning, and their mother is seventy-nine, is at home, and cannot manage stairs after dark. **The care-link is not a service and there is no service: it is a telephone answered at night by a person who is not in the house, in place about four years, about nine pounds a month at the start and about eleven now.** The whole of what they came to say, in about four minutes and twice as not a complaint: they pay it, she would not be at home without it, and in the spring before the one before last she was in a ward for eleven weeks and the ward said to expect her home in about three days and the two of them could not manage three days. **The care-link itself can be ended, by their mother, from her own chair, with the number on a card in a drawer. What cannot be ended is what follows: she would then be on her own at night, and the two of them would find out about it about four days later, and they would find out because somebody rings a district, and there is no job in this city whose business that is.** One question was asked all morning, by a first-year of nineteen, and it was *are you two all right*. **Nobody thanked them, nobody said they had been lucky, nobody proposed a second visit, nobody counted other families, and the family is not a case and does not become a resource.** A man of about thirty-four who buys training said one sentence to two strangers and was told, correctly, that if there were nine of them he would have nine of them on a page in about four years. **Talia Venn, twenty-four, said four sentences, all four about her own position**, including that nobody has ever written down what happens on the morning somebody cancels a care-link and that the reason is that the person who cancels it is the only one who could say and the person who would have to be rung is the person who would not have been rung. Nobody thanked anybody.

### Chapter 0274 — "One Line"

**The Wednesday of week ninety-six, the thirteenth sitting of the Exchange, four weeks to the day after the twelfth. Load-book entry 277: one line, two calls, one refusal, and ninety-three days.** Nine chairs and the ninth against the wall, a tin that has never had a lock, and a four-column book at forty-six lines, the last line a hundred and twenty-two days old. **A woman of twenty-six who telephoned eleven days earlier, was not rung back, and came anyway asked to say a thing to the holder of the book and not to the room. It took about four minutes and not one word of it is in this manuscript.** One line went into the third column in about nine seconds, unread since. **The telephone count moved from forty-seven to forty-nine and the book to forty-seven lines, and the count has now been announced eighteen times, thirteen of them on the thirteen Wednesdays this book holds and five predating it on a sheet not shown to anybody, and no person in that room may convert the two figures into one another or into a number of sittings.** A young man of twenty-one said he keeps a count of the times a person telephoned and was not rung back and that he has never told the holder what it is; and then said, because there was a person in the room, that about four of the people who ring the number on a plate are not ringing for a technique and about four are ringing because they are somewhere with nothing and it is dark, and that he has wanted somebody to ask for about two years and has not, because if he asks then he is the one who has decided that matters. **A man of about forty-four asked where the reason for the last sitting came from and was refused before he was told what he had asked.** The reason is nineteen years old, was given to her by somebody when she was twenty-nine, and is not hers to give; **the only person who could say it out loud with any right to is not findable and has not been for nineteen years, which is why it is not going into a building in Crown Terrace, and why on a page it would become a rule about who may be heard, and a rule about who may be heard is a rule about who may be found.** Nobody thanked her, nobody asked a second question, nobody asked who the person was, and the young man of twenty-one opened his mouth and closed it. The ninth chair was against the wall at the close and was not moved back.

### Chapter 0275 — "Eleven Minutes"

**The Thursday of week ninety-six. Load-book entry 278: one door, one machine, eleven minutes, and ninety-four days. THE MIDPOINT, and the only panel and the only `* * *` marker in the volume.** A machine in a basement in Nine Bridges had been stuck for about four years. **He could not open the door, and the reason is not that anybody did anything to him: a basement in Nine Bridges is a building four hundred people in this case could find and he is the only one of them who is findable at it, and being findable at a building and being known to the man with the key to that building are two different things.** He telephoned the number on a plate at about seven in the morning and asked the man of thirty-four who works nights to open it, and said he would say nothing more then or afterwards and would not telephone about it again. **The man opened it. The man who looks after the building was asked whether the machine works, said no, said yes, and was not told what it was for and did not ask. Nobody in that building knows why the door was open.** In the basement are a woman of forty-four who was a typist in that room for about two years, who has never listened to the tape, who gave the reason before she was asked for it in nine words — *I was the typist. I was not the audience* — and did not improve on it, and the man of thirty-four who opened the door and does not know why he opened it. **On the tape, a person doing their job asks a woman of fifty-three for her name and she gives it, and then, asked whose procedure it is, says that it is hers, that she drafted it, that it is called the Crown Clause, that it has never been denied by her and will not be now, and that she had not come to be asked that and answered anyway because she could tell it was about nine minutes away.** She says four things. **She is right about one and it is not the one she says it for: that where nobody has answered a door for two days the correct procedure is the last approved sequence run as a standing response, and that a person who will not run it has decided that a pump runs until a person comes back, is deciding that about a stranger, and will never have to look at them.** Her second thing is wrong, that it has been run nine times in fourteen years. Her third is right and she does not claim it, that nobody who drafted it wanted it used and that she could not have stopped it. Her fourth is about a matter not in front of the reader. **Nobody in this case has an answer to her, nobody is given one, she is not refuted, she is not defeated, and the recording is not destroyed, is not put in a binder and is not read out in a room. It is not a message. She is not met and nobody went looking for her.** The one panel of the volume is in this chapter, it is two lines, it names nobody, it agrees with her, and he looked at it and put it away.

### Chapter 0276 — "Nobody Asked Her For a Number"

**The Friday of week ninety-six, the second Friday rota of this rotation. Load-book entry 279: one question, no answer, one beat, and ninety-five days.** Eleven on the list and about four of the eleven not on it. **A woman of twenty-three, on her first attendance, asked the only question anybody has ever asked in one of these rooms: when somebody on this list finishes and the room does not say anything, what are they waiting for. The answer is the instructor, a woman of thirty-six with a company lanyard, and it is the first time anybody has said it in a room, and she added that nobody has ever asked her whether she would rather it was a card.** The man of twenty-two had the figure in about four seconds and did not say it, and gave the reason on a service road: that saying it would make about eleven people a week into a figure, that the only person in this case who can be found at that door is him, and that the number is on the back of a card in a drawer in a flat in Nine Bridges and on no form in this city, and he is not going to be the reason it stops being that. **The woman of twenty-three said that it is the instructor she is waiting for and the instructor is the one waiting too, and that it is a strange thing to be and she would rather be waiting for a card, and the instructor said yes.** Nobody thanked the woman of twenty-three. **On the last run there was no gap at all, and nobody in that room said anything about it, and afterwards he could not work out whether he had missed about a second and a half and did not ask, and wrote the not knowing down.**

### Chapter 0277 — "Who Is Going To Be Standing There"

**The Monday of week ninety-seven. Load-book entry 280: one call, one landing, one answer, and ninety-eight days. WHAT CHANGES.** A telephone on a plate rang at about half past four and a woman of about thirty-four said there was a man on the bottom step outside a flat on a landing in Nine Bridges who had asked her whether anybody knew where a man who works nights was, had said it was all right, and had said he would rather somebody was in his building. He gave no name and she does not know one. **He came in at about ten past seven and was not made a joke of it.** A man of fifty-one with a tool bag gave the room's answer in about four seconds: a person on a rota with a card in date, the rota is the Friday one, it has been running for years, he does not know anybody on it, neither does anybody else in that room, and that is not a failure of the rota, that is what a rota is. **Talia Venn, twenty-four, said the sentence that is the whole of the change, in one sentence, to the room and not to him: that the question is not whether the rule is right, that the question is who is going to be standing next to a person who has not come back yet, and that everybody in that room has been asking the first question for about four years because the first question has an answer and the second one has a person in it.** A cook of twenty-seven gave the other half in about nine words — it is a rota, then, and not a man — which is the fifth sentence of that shape she has given in two volumes against a promise of four, and she did not notice. **A man of twenty-nine who works on lifts said for the first time in this case that he is on a rota, that it has been a Tuesday for about nine years, that nobody has ever asked him to be on it and nobody has ever rung to say thank you, and that if the person who wrote it is honest with himself then the rota is the answer and has been for about nine years and none of them has ever stood next to it.** A man of fifty-eight with a bad hip said out loud, to be the one who says it, that none of them are going to write it, and that he is not going to say it again. **No rota was made, no post was invented, no list was written, no title was given to anybody, and the speaker of the answer is not a mechanism and holds nothing.** And on a service road at about nine, to nobody, in about nine seconds, the man of twenty-two worked out that a rota is a promise made in advance by a person who cannot be reached, and that he has been right for four years that being findable is the trap and wrong about one thing.

### Chapter 0278 — "No"

**The Wednesday of week ninety-seven. Load-book entry 281: one question, one no, nine sentences, and a hundred days. THE CORRIDOR, THE REQUEST AND THE REFUSAL, and the only corridor and the only request in the volume.** At about half past four, outside a room in a building in Crown Terrace, Aurelia Sopp asked a man of twenty-two to run the procedure once, in a room, with the people in it. **He said no in about nine seconds, standing up, with no apology and no explanation inside the nine seconds.** Asked for the reason he gave the one about a person who finishes a practice on her own and does not know she has done something she should not have been able to do; was told it is not the reason; answered that it is the reason he has; and was told, out loud, that nobody was going to argue with him about it, and why — that in about four years somebody will say she argued and she will not be able to tell them that she did not. **She told him she is going to say it in the room the next morning with her name on it and not his, and that she is not going to let him have the easier half of refusing to be the person who says a printed rule out loud by refusing a different thing to her on a Wednesday afternoon. At about six she read him nine sentences in the corridor with the door shut and about nine people behind it, and they are the version that goes into the record, and they are hers, and they are better than what he had.** He listened to all nine and found nothing in them to argue with and said so, in about four minutes. **She told him, on purpose and unasked, why she took it off him and put her name on it: a thing in a record with a man's name on it can be argued with in about four years by about nine people and a thing with hers on it can only be argued with by whoever is standing in the room at the time, and she is sixty. Then she told him she could have said all of that before she asked and did not, and why, including that she was tired and that she has told a room in about a term that tiredness is not a reason and that she intended to use it.** The nine sentences are set out in full in the load book and are deliberately verbatim. **Nobody thanked him. Nobody was angry.** He taught, demonstrated, assessed and assisted nothing, and the condition is not quoted, not paraphrased and not softened in any of the ten chapters, and nobody in that corridor knows that he did not give the real reason.

### Chapter 0279 — "Did You Want To"

**The Thursday of week ninety-seven. Load-book entry 282: one arithmetic, one smaller question, four minutes, and a hundred and one days.** In a cold room off a service road there were about nine people in it on the Monday and about seven now and one of the seven was asleep, and nobody had gone anywhere, and a woman of twenty-four counted it and said so: that he was not in that room on Monday afternoon, that he was somewhere with a great deal of paper in it, that he has said no word about Wednesday, and that she is not going to ask him what it was — **and then said she was going to ask something smaller, and asked it, and it is four words: did you want to.** He told her, in about four minutes, and it was not a speech: that a person of sixty had asked him to run the procedure once, in a room, with the people in it, and that he had said no in about nine seconds, and that the reason he gave her is the reason he gave the other woman and is not the reason, and that she had not argued with him about it, and that she is going to be the person who says in a room the next morning that a man said no. **He did not tell her the nine sentences and she did not ask for them. He did not say the refusal had cost him something; she worked that out in about two seconds and did not say it either.** She said one thing, about him and not about the two of them: that he has never once wanted to be the person a room can ask, that she has watched him build a whole case out of not wanting it, that she does not think that case is what he thinks it is, and that there has been one person she would have wanted to ask him for something and to keep on asking, and that there is now, and she is sixty and will be sixty-one and does not want him. **He did not answer it and has not answered it to anybody, and about four people in this case have worked out that there was a Thursday and none of them has said so. Nothing was resolved, there was no scene, no declaration and no kiss, and the words *we*, *our* and *us* do not appear in a sentence about the two of them anywhere in this run.** A first-year of nineteen then said to the room that there is no post in this city whose business it is to ring a person, that about four rooms full of people a year could hold that job and none of them do, and that every one of about four of them has said in that room that somebody should. Nobody said who.

### Chapter 0280 — "She Did Not Take It Down"

**The Monday of week ninety-eight. Load-book entry 283: one sign, one refusal, one line, and a hundred and five days. THE CLOSE.** A man of about thirty-eight in a district jacket, six years visiting addresses, owed nothing, a villain to nobody, and not previously in that room, asked in front of about nine people for a sign on a second-floor door in a district this case has not been in to be taken down: it is not true, he has been three times, he has been told three times that nobody lives there, and a report would make it somebody else's problem at about half past four in the afternoon. About four people in that room thought it was the right thing to do and one of them said so. **She asked one question: who is behind that door. He does not know, has been three times, and was told three times by people on that landing and by a shop on the ground floor, one of whom gave him the name on the sign and therefore read it. She said no**, and gave the ground once: that taking a sign down is saying something about a door and leaving one up is saying nothing about it, and that she is not going to say something about a door in a building in a district none of them has been in because a man doing a job on a Friday was told three times by people on a landing that there was nobody there. Asked what happens if somebody in that flat cannot get down the stairs: there is a person on that landing who knows it, it is not her, it is not a sign, all four she has taken down were put up by somebody who could get down the stairs, she has never taken one down for the person it was about, and she is not going to today in front of nine people. **He sat down and did not argue. Nobody said she was wrong and nobody said she was right, and this is the first time anybody in that room has watched a person decline to do a thing they were asked to do.** She then named the cost before the mark, and the cost is the opposite of the fifteenth line's: that the first cost her the last thing she wanted and this one is going to cost her the only thing she has, which is that somebody can ask her and she can say no; and that in about four years about four people in this city will know she can take a sign down, all four of them will be told she did not, and every one of them will decide that she could not. **She wrote the sixteenth line in seventeen words, unsigned, with no space left for a name: *I take signs down. I did not take that one down, because nobody behind it asked me.*** The wall is sixteen lines in ten hands and eleven names, no line has been removed and none has been rubbed off, and the first line is a cook's and is still the first line. Talia Venn said six claims in one sentence about the sign and not about the woman, including that she is not saying the woman is right and that every one of them would have taken it down. **The man of thirty-eight left at about half past six, wrote nothing down, and was not told that the door had been dealt with or that it had not. The sign is still up.**


# VOLUME 06 — MOVEMENT II (Chapters 261–270), *Who Can Ask* — CHAPTER SUMMARIES

**Load-book entries 264 to 273. The measured figures and the matchers that produced them are in `workspace/volume-06/batch-0002/SUMMARY.md` sections 2 and 6. No day number, month-name, day-date or year appears in any of the ten chapters.**

### Chapter 0261 — "A Room Cannot Come Back"

**The Monday morning of week ninety-two, a room in Crown Terrace, and a service road in the wet. Load-book entry 264, whose own opening label says this entry is one item, six smaller questions, and sixty-three days.** Eleven on the docket and eleven in date; a stair fire door not reported and not chased. Room a hundred and sixty-eight days old, card a hundred and seventy-two, heating on its sixty-third day, twelve lines eighty-eight, thirteen thirty-nine, fourteen four. **The item was heard: whether a practice can be required to show that any person in it may stop at any time, and what evidence would satisfy that.** A chair of forty-nine whose office signs about eleven of a kind a year asked six questions, each smaller than the one before — three of the clerk of forty-four, one of the officer of sixty and two of him — and the word budget came out loud before the fourth of them was finished. Aurelia Sopp, sixty, gave the finding she had given a fortnight earlier in a corridor — the only evidence is a record of who stopped, a record of who stopped is a column, about eleven thousand premises have no column — was met with the smaller question *who would keep the column*, and the item went ahead anyway. **Talia Venn gave about eleven minutes on a practice she has been inside of for a term and a half and did not read the sentence on the wall binder.** He described the room from a chair in nine sentences and was not standing in front of anything. **The one thing produced was twelve words at about eleven twenty-five: a room cannot ask to be heard again and a person has to.** Nobody thanked her. The clerk of forty-four is still seconded to the secretariat and the chair was not told; he saw the shape of it at a lift and did not raise it.

### Chapter 0262 — "A Hearing Is A Book"

**The Wednesday morning of week ninety-two, the twelfth sitting of the Exchange in a room above the line in Saltmarket, and a cold room off a service road in the evening. Load-book entry 265, one unopened book, one sign that changed, and sixty-five days.** Stair fire door reported Monday, attended, reset for the eighth time in about a fortnight, no fault that will go in a box. Room a hundred and seventy days old, card a hundred and seventy-four, heating sixty-five, twelve lines ninety, thirteen forty-one, fourteen six. **A man of about forty-four asked that the Monday hearing be entered in the book and was refused, and the reason given is the first in nineteen years that is about the room: a hearing is a book, and a book of what a caller said cannot be written by the people who did not call, and not one person in that hearing had telephoned that desk or attended that room.** The book did not open and is at forty-six lines. **The telephone count moved from forty-four to forty-seven; she did the arithmetic in her head and refused to do it again; the sign changed from the book two lines ahead to the telephone one line ahead; she said the direction out loud once and did not look into it; and she asked that no person present convert the two figures into one another or into a number of sittings.** The count is now announced seventeen times, twelve of them on the twelve Wednesdays. **She did not ring the one caller who is not coming**, and gave the reason, and he did not come. The ninth chair is against the wall, not moved back, its mover not named, its holder a young woman of twenty-one in attendance since fifteen who was not asked anything. The young man of twenty-one keeps a count and was given the answer about furniture for the fifth time and was not to be confirmed and was not to be stopped.

### Chapter 0263 — "Four Times Over"

**The Thursday of week ninety-two, a delivery stoppage, and a sink. Load-book entry 266, one stoppage, four visits, and sixty-six days.** Eleven on the docket and ten in date; the door attended at about two, found exactly as described on eleven previous occasions; the basement water back to four inches with nothing done to it. Room a hundred and seventy-one days old, card a hundred and seventy-five, heating sixty-six, twelve lines ninety-one, thirteen forty-two, fourteen seven. **About nine drivers are stood down over a hand-held that records every handover; two of the nine say it is the most sensible thing their employer has ever asked of them, and one of those two is a woman of about forty-four with six years of driving for that company.** She came in at about half past six, asked whether she could sit, **was answered in four words, and sat for about forty minutes and was asked nothing.** She said one thing before leaving: that she came because it is the only room in this city where nobody has ever asked her to justify being in it. **At about half past seven a first-year of nineteen named the cost at a sink: four visits, four Thursdays, all of them in that room, and if they are friends then anybody who wants to know what she is doing on a Thursday afternoon has to find one person, and the one person is on about eleven hundred pieces of paper with her own name at the bottom by her own choice. She is going to stop being her friend for about a term.** Nobody improved on it, nobody thanked her, and about four people wanted to say it had not been her choice and none of them said it. The stoppage was settled in about four minutes nine days later by the company and its drivers, and nobody in this case was in that room.

### Chapter 0264 — "Cards Go In Pockets"

**The Friday of week ninety-two, a company training room off a corridor in Crown Terrace, and a cold room off a service road. Load-book entry 267, one question asked four times, one refusal of four words, and sixty-seven days.** Grade three, twenty to eight, on a card in date; eleven on the list and about four not on it; four jobs, the last a stair fire door reset for the ninth time in about a fortnight. Room a hundred and seventy-two days old, card a hundred and seventy-six, heating sixty-seven, twelve lines ninety-two, thirteen forty-three, fourteen eight. Nothing to hand out, four runs faster, all four ending on a spoken word that is on no card and never has been. **A woman of about thirty-four asked for the fourth time in a term to have the ending written down and was refused in four words — cards go in pockets — and the long form of the reason was given in about thirty words on the Friday before last, was given a second time on being asked whether it had changed, and will not be given a third.** She said she would ask a fifth and is not asking because she thinks the refusal is wrong; she has finished a run by waiting about eleven seconds for four years and would like to stop. **On the last run nobody waited, in about four seconds, and whether that is the cards or the chair is not established. The cards have been out of use about two weeks, which is correct: the withdrawal was the Wednesday of week ninety.** A man of twenty-one near the door did not speak. The man of twenty-nine who works on lifts was not in that room and was not asked whether he was coming. Nobody thanked anybody.

### Chapter 0265 — "I Am Not In The Book"

**The Monday morning of week ninety-three, a room above the line in Saltmarket with no sitting in it. Load-book entry 268, one refusal of ten words, and seventy days.** Ten on the docket and ten in date; the basement reported for the tenth time inside about four weeks. Room a hundred and seventy-five days old, card a hundred and seventy-nine, heating seventy, twelve lines ninety-five, thirteen forty-six, fourteen eleven. **A hearing produced one thing and the item therefore came back on the Friday to the officer of sixty who found its fault and did nothing about it. She went to Saltmarket at about half past ten with a folder and asked, once, for the woman of sixty-one to come to a room in Crown Terrace and say what happens on the other side of her table.** The refusal was ten words, given in nine seconds and twice more in the same words: **she keeps the book and is not in the book. Those ten words are not the sentence she has been refusing in for nineteen years, and she made sure of that before she said them; one man in that room heard the difference and has never been told what the other sentence is.** The rest of the reason was given once and withdrawn — in about four years a description of nineteen years would make every one of those people a thing that was described, and not one of them can object, and she is not going to carry it into a building. **Nobody thanked her. Nobody asked a second question, nobody proposed a smaller thing, nobody offered a car, nobody offered to come to her, nothing was written down, and about four people noticed that nobody had said that somebody ought to go and get her.** She is not carried anywhere, is not put on a stage, is not described as a resource, and is not asked again. **He did not ask her to change her mind** and was asked why by a first-year of nineteen. Aurelia Sopp said once that she has been right every time she opens her mouth for nineteen years, that this morning the right thing was to be right, and that she held it back, and that she is not going to be thanked for it. **This was the second time in nineteen years she has had to hold being right back on purpose; the first was in a corridor in Crown Terrace the previous Monday, where being right would have cost the clerk of forty-four something, and the second was the harder of the two.**

### Chapter 0266 — "Six Weeks Of Somebody"

**The Wednesday of week ninety-three, the object of the volume, and a cold room. Load-book entry 269, one figure, six weeks, and seventy-two days.** Eleven on the docket; the stair fire door back and reset; the basement down to two inches on its own. Room a hundred and seventy-seven days old, card a hundred and eighty-one, heating seventy-two, twelve lines ninety-seven, thirteen forty-eight, fourteen thirteen. **A man of fifty-one with a tool bag said nine hundred doors in four buildings carrying a number off a different line of the same list, the same figure for about four years, said in about four rooms, never thanked, and was not thanked.** **A woman of thirty-four, Petula Fenn, said the checking has to be done by a person because a number is on a wall and the list is on paper, that it is about four minutes a door and about nine where there is no lift, two of the four buildings having none and about nine of the doors being in them, that the list is reissued about four times a year and never corrected and arrives in a bag with nobody ringing anybody, and that there is no money for it and she is the person and it is her own time.** A first-year of nineteen did the arithmetic out loud three times: nine hundred at four minutes is sixty hours, four issues a year is two hundred and forty hours, **about six weeks of a working year, every year, for one person.** She has asked twice in writing and the second reply calls the list a guide. **Nobody asked to see the card. She did not produce it, on the ground that then it would be in the room.** The man of twenty-nine who works on lifts was not there, was not told and was not asked. Talia Venn said that she has never been asked to show anybody the card and that the only person who ever asked her about it asked in a corridor in about nine seconds, and that those two facts are one sentence.

### Chapter 0267 — "Nothing Is Wrong With It"

**The Friday of week ninety-three, a telephone at ten past five in the morning, a company training room in the evening, and a service road in the dark. Load-book entry 270, one plate, one refusal, and seventy-four days.** Grade three, twenty to eight; eleven on the list, about four not on it; the stair fire door attended on the Thursday and reset for the tenth time in about a fortnight. Room a hundred and seventy-nine days old, card a hundred and eighty-three, heating seventy-four, twelve lines ninety-nine, thirteen fifty, fourteen fifteen. **A man of thirty-four who works nights found a plate in a service cupboard on a fourth landing in Nine Bridges, did not touch it, did not open the box behind it, did not photograph it, wrote nothing beside it and did not report it, and rang the number on a plate in the Lower Wards to say so.** It carries the block's own number, four things in a box on its face and no fifth, and it was not there in the spring and he was in that cupboard in the spring. **Nothing is wrong with it, and that is the whole of the finding.** He was asked what the fifth thing is and was not told, and the ground is that explaining it would make him the second person in this case who has been told. **He said in advance he would not say it twice and would not put it on paper, and that if it were written down he would know; he was told it would not be; he said it would be, and that he would rather be written down than asked twice.** Nobody has ever asked him anything. On the rota, **the woman of about thirty-four was in the second row for a second consecutive Friday, four years on that list, asked nothing, and finished a run in about four seconds, and nobody in that room said anything and nobody was thanked. The question the man of twenty-two carried out of the room a fortnight earlier was not put in it by anybody on this Friday, and the not putting is the whole of what is different about those four seconds.** **He wrote nothing down himself, and the only record of the plate is the man of twenty-two's book on a service road; the chapter is not called after a hand and no hand is in it.**

### Chapter 0268 — "Four Hundred Hands"

**The Monday of week ninety-four, Crown Terrace, and a cold room. Load-book entry 271, one sheet, about two hundred and forty hands, and seventy-seven days.** Ten on the docket and ten in date; a call in Nine Bridges closed by somebody else before he arrived. Room a hundred and eighty-two days old, card a hundred and eighty-six, heating seventy-seven, twelve lines a hundred and two, thirteen fifty-three, fourteen eighteen. **A man of fifty-one, on a job and not looking for it, found a sheet about the size of a piece of kitchen paper taped to a service corridor wall with about two hundred and forty lines on it in about two hundred and forty different hands, all the same four words, of which about four hundred exist over four years in the nine buildings he has worked in, two hundred and forty of them in that one corridor.** He did not photograph it. **The four words are a cook's and are the first line of the hardboard in that room, and she was at a sink in that room while it was all said, and nobody told her, and a first-year of nineteen gave ten words — they are hers, four hundred lines is a bigger surface — and then gave the whole ground when asked for the rest of it, and about nine people said nothing at the same time.** **The oldest hand is in the second column about nine lines down; two people in that room had seen it and neither said so; the man who wrote it was given an answer by a stranger about four years ago, was sixty-one, and has gone quiet; a room of about forty people in Crown Terrace knows he still has nine words of his own; and nobody in this case can hand him four words, because he cannot be found.** He is not named, nothing was sent to him, and nothing will be.

### Chapter 0269 — "Forty-One Names"

**The Wednesday of week ninety-four, a counter in Bower Street and a back room, and a cold room in the evening. Load-book entry 272, one page, forty-one names, nine minutes, and seventy-nine days.** Eleven on the docket; the stair fire door attended and reset for the eleventh time in about a fortnight. Room a hundred and eighty-four days old, card a hundred and eighty-eight, heating seventy-nine, twelve lines a hundred and four, thirteen fifty-five, fourteen twenty. **A clerk of fifty-three with nineteen years behind that counter read a page of about forty-one names out loud once, in about nine minutes, to about nine people, because a page nobody has read is a page nobody can close.** She said before she began that it was not going to do anything; she worked that out halfway down the list and read the other half anyway; nobody spoke between the names; and she put the sheet on the table writing side up, which is the only time in about four years it has been on a table. About nine of the forty-one were telephoned before whatever happened, one who was not was safe, and thirty-one have not been heard from. **A district officer asked whether it could be recorded properly with a reference and was refused: a page of forty-one cannot go into a system because it would work, and if it did not work she would be doing it.** The pencil box on that counter was not emptied, took no second name, was not given a reference, was not moved onto a system and was adopted by no district. She was thanked by nobody. A first-year of nineteen said one sentence in the evening and asked that nobody make it into a thing.

### Chapter 0270 — "The Last Thing He Wanted"

**The small hours of the Thursday of week ninety-four, a cold room, a hardboard, and a service road in the wet. Load-book entry 273, one line in fourteen words, one cost, and eighty days.** Eleven on the docket and ten in date; one job not attended, not reassigned and not reported. Room a hundred and eighty-five days old, card a hundred and eighty-nine, heating on its eightieth day and unpaid, twelve lines a hundred and five, thirteen fifty-six, fourteen twenty-one, **and the fifteenth zero, which is a difference and not an inclusive count.** About five people at half past one in the morning. **A man of thirty-four who works nights named the cost before he made the mark: that he is the only person in this case who can now be found at a door, that this is the last thing he has wanted for about four years, and that he knows that is a stupid thing to want.** He gave the mechanism himself — the call at ten past five, the refusal to have it written down, and that he would rather be written down than asked twice — and he gave the reason the cost is real, which is a plate in a service cupboard that is still there, that in about four years somebody will go into it, and that the only person who knows it exists is findable at a door. **He wrote fourteen words, unsigned, with a space left where a name would go, and two people looked at it. The wall is fifteen lines in nine hands and ten names. Nobody signed it, nobody was thanked, nobody argued, and nobody asked him whether he was sure.** A first-year of nineteen said again that a line is not a rule and was told by the man who had written it that the difference has been said on that board four times and by about nine people and that she is none of them; the count was agreed as four. **On his way out he said that he is not going back into that cupboard, that nobody is going, that this is not a plan but four words at ten to two in the morning, and that somebody ought to write down before then that not going is a decision and not a thing that lasts — and was told that nobody is going to write that down, and said he knows, and that this is why he said it to a room and not to a man.** The woman of about thirty came in at about two and was not asked anything and nobody apologised to her.

### Chapter 0251 — "Folded Once"

**The Monday morning of week eighty-nine, from about half past nine to about ten at night, in a room off a service road in the Lower Wards and on a service road in the dark. Load-book entry 254, and its own opening label says this entry is one folded page, one number, and forty-two days.** Ten on the docket and nine in date, the tenth a stair fire door in Nine Bridges that has come up seven times in about a month with a stamped number in the description box, reset four times and checked twice and found as described every time. **The eighth thing went into a ring binder at about half past two without anybody asking for it:** a woman of about thirty, who has been in that room on every working day since the Friday of week sixty-nine and has been asked nothing in nineteen weeks, took a page folded once out of a bag on her lap, carried it to a shelf, opened the binder, put it in at the back, shut it, put it back and sat down, and said nine words addressed to the room and not to a person. Nobody asked her a question. **A first-year of nineteen told a man of twenty-two, in nine words and from the end chair with her boots off, that reading it makes you the person who read it, and then gave the reason unprompted, and the reason is not manners: four of us were watching you, and if you do it then you are a person who reads other people's pages, and that is a thing you can do again.** He asked what he was to do with it and was told nothing. The binder holds eight items, cannot be emptied, has been read standing up by one man of about thirty-one in about nine minutes, and no page has ever been taken out. **A cook of twenty-seven, at a sink, said she has asked about the heating three times, will not ask a fourth, that this is not a refusal but a Tuesday she is not going to spend again, and that this was the fourth time she had said a thing of that shape against a promise not to say a fifth, and she would rather she was the one wrong.** Nobody wrote it down. A man of fifty-eight with a bad hip came for an hour and a half, read the board and the unremovable grey ghost above it, declined tea and offered no comment. A man of thirty-four who works nights declined to produce his sheet of about nine addresses and said once that he was not going to talk about the door list again, and that the last time he had, he had talked for about four minutes and come away with the sense that he had done something, and that he had not.

### Chapter 0252 — "The Box In Pencil"

**The Wednesday morning of week eighty-nine, a ground-floor district practice standards office in District 3, and a service road in the wet. Load-book entry 255, and its own opening label says this entry is four fields, one date, and a binder on a wall.** Eleven on the docket and eleven in date, the first time in about a term that the two have been equal, and a basement's standing water reported for the fifth time at about twenty-two inches, into whose description box he wrote that the fault is not the pump and declined to state where the water is thought to come from. **A practice standards officer of District 3 of sixty, whose job it is to ask questions, asked a woman of twenty-four whether a person can take a share back without a form, and was told yes, in this city, in about four buildings, one of them by hand, by a clerk of fifty-three with nineteen years behind her, in a box ruled in pencil holding one name, with no reference number, not on a system, not a form, and which may not take a second name and which nobody in this city has ever asked her for.** And was told the four figures: a page of about forty-one people that cannot be closed from the outside, about nine of them telephoned before anything happened and one who was not and was safe; about four hundred and one replies in a company's general enquiries box of which about nine of the first forty-one got the company answer; about nine hundred doors in four buildings carrying a number off a different line of the same list; and a wall plate that says two-fourteen over a room that is two-oh-seven, with a fault ticket closed with the correct number in the building's own typeface by a man doing the job properly, **which changed nothing and is on the page as a correct action that changed nothing.** And was told that the standard on a practice card is real, numbered, dated eleven years, **not withdrawn**, that nothing in that binder has been withdrawn in nineteen years because withdrawing one is a thing a person writes on it, that she would be worried if it were not right, and that there is no conspirator in it and the question of who may invoke it is a question about about four hundred people and not about one. A standards officer named in this case since the eighth week of a term two years ago read a letter for a man in a car park and telephoned the woman who was to read it on the Friday in advance, and the man has not forgotten it and did not use it. **The man of twenty-two was in the corridor for the last four minutes and did not enter the room, said he had never seen one of those standards and would not know if one was put in front of him, was told that this was the most useful thing anybody in that building had said that morning and that he did not know he was saying it, described the hardboard, was told there was no use for it, and said that somebody had already photographed a thing in a room for a prospectus read by about four hundred people and he was not going to do it again.** The smaller question, asked in about nine seconds: are you going to say this again. Yes. Why. Because I want it, and wanting it is not a reason, and I would rather be told than have it noticed later — the first such statement in nineteen years in that room, against about eleven refusals of which about nine gave a reason and two of those were better.

### Chapter 0253 — "Three Buildings"

**The Thursday morning of week eighty-nine, in three buildings and then in the room off the service road. Load-book entry 256, and its own opening label says this entry is one sentence in three buildings, one card nobody read out, and a refusal.** A man of thirty-four who buys training gave his name at the door, which nobody in that room had ever done before and which about four people noticed, and said beforehand that nobody had asked him to repeat anything and that he had decided in about nine days that not repeating it was worse, and that the decision was about him and not about the room. He then said the first line on that hardboard, a cook's, without altering it, in three buildings, to about thirty people. **Thanked in the first, by a woman who meant it, who wrote it on a whiteboard uncredited and said out loud that she had done that. Ignored in the second, where about eleven people were all waiting for somebody else to move, and he named that as the difficult one. Asked to leave in the third, by a man who used the word *scope* twice, and he named the first as the occasion he was worst at.** **A first-year of nineteen said a whiteboard is worse than a photograph and smaller, because a photograph makes a picture and a whiteboard makes a thing that is in the building, and in about four years somebody will write a sentence under it and the two will be read together and neither will be about a cook.** He answered that a woman on about eleven hundred leaflets is a different problem from a photograph of a counter and that he had not known about the leaflets until that day, and she said that it was not the same and it was worse and it was smaller. He then said that he had carried it out of the room, that about nine people in three buildings now know a man of twenty-two holds a view about names, that none of them know which of them said it, and that the whole of the sentence is a cook's who would not want it out of that room and cannot stop it and has never once been asked — and he said he had done that to a woman he had never met because he did not want to be the person saying it, asked to be told he was wrong, said he did not think he would be, and was not told. **A company card is described and then stopped:** a starting thing, steps, a place where you let go, a printed box about what the practice must not touch, and nothing about how a person decides they are finished — and a man of twenty-nine who works on lifts instructed him to stop and gave his standing reason for the second time in this city, that if he goes and looks then in about four years there is a list of buildings and a list of buildings is a list of people, and added that the eleven are a different sentence, that he has no reason for the eleven, and that he will not make one up in a room on a Thursday. **A woman of twenty-four put the whole argument in about thirty words: a sentence in a room is nine people, a sentence outside a room is nobody's, those are the two things there are, and you cannot build a third thing out of the two.** Nobody thanked anybody.

### Chapter 0254 — "The Word He Did Not Say"

**The Friday of week eighty-nine, a company training room off a corridor in Crown Terrace with about nine other rooms on it, the corridor, and a service road with the shutter behind him. Load-book entry 257, and its own opening label says this entry is one release, one word, and forty-six days.** Grade three, twenty to eight, on a card in date, with the term's exception recorded and renewed in a system in a box that says *No reason given by holder*. Eleven on the Friday list for the week and about four of the eleven not on it, and the city's standing weekly figure is unaltered and is not a rate and is on the back of a woman's own card. Nine folding chairs, nine people, a laminated sheet on a post by the door carrying nine words with no name, no date, no company and no version, and a man of fifty-one with a bad wrist who came on a Friday and is not on the list. **The practice is a load-hold with a boundary-release at the end, and the beat is printed on all nine cards in a box in the same typeface as the box about what the practice must not touch, and the wording is correct. The instructor ran it through four times in about twenty minutes, ending each with one spoken word that is not *finished*, not *release*, and is not any word on the card.** Asked in a corridor what the word was for, she said to confirm the release, and that the card is what somebody wrote down and the room is what people actually do, and that in about four years a person will be sitting in a chair waiting and she is the person they wait for and she would like to be able to say why that is a good idea. Asked whether he thought it was a good idea: very good at not going wrong, which is not the same, and she is thirty-six and has done it for nine years. **On the last run a woman of about twenty-two completed the hold, reached the release, stopped, consulted a card that carries the beat, and waited; two counts of about eleven seconds were taken, one by him and one at the back; the instructor said the word and the practitioner finished and the room restarted. He was in the fourth row, had the word available, could have produced it at any point in those eleven seconds, and did not. Nobody in the room or the corridor remarked on it and nobody thanked him for it.** A man of fifty-one with a bad wrist said, to nobody, that she will go home and practise and wait about four times and the fourth will be in a stairwell on her own with nobody there and will then work without her noticing, and that this is the good outcome and the bad outcome and is the same thing, and that he has written a version of it down for a company and been told it is not a field, and that the field is blank and the person who licensed it is not named, and that he has been trying for about two years to find out who they were and has stopped asking and has not stopped noticing. **At about half past four on a service road he said the reason out loud, standing up, to nobody, and said it twice to check it, and the reason is that he is the man in the lanyard, and that in about nine minutes in a room in Crown Terrace he was the only other person in that building who could have made a woman of twenty-two finish and did not, because he does not know what happens to a person who finishes it alone and does not know she has done something she should not have been able to do.**

### Chapter 0255 — "Four Parts And Not Five"

**The Monday morning of week ninety, the same district office, and a service road in the wet. Load-book entry 258, and its own opening label says this entry is one card, one typeface, and forty-nine days.** Ten on the docket and nine in date, and a basement's standing water at about sixteen inches, down from about twenty-two, reported for the seventh time in about four weeks, with no stopcock, valve or inlet fitted by anybody. **A clerk of forty-four, who is the person who answers the officer's questions and not her instrument, was named at the counter in the ordinary way and about four people heard it.** She brought a plastic wallet with about nine cards and a folded sheet listing printings, and said the cards are the company's and not hers, that they come in a pack of about four hundred and go out to about eleven sites, and that the one on the top is not the company's. **The card names all five parts of a practice — a starting hold, ordered steps, a point of release, a printed box stating what the practice must not touch, and a statement that an aftertaste is normal — and contains nothing whatever about how a practitioner knows they are finished.** She said she has carried it since about a term and a half and brought it because a person of sixty asked her a question in that building. He warned her not to say it too quickly, so that nobody starts looking for who put it there, and she said she has not said it quickly. **He then turned five copies of the same card over: the hold, the steps and the release are one typeface in all five; the boundary box is the same typeface in four and a different one at half a point larger in the fifth, in circulation about eleven years; the revision column dates that change eleven years and the four earlier printings are older, and no line anywhere on the sheet carries a note of withdrawal, amendment, reason or reviewer.** The officer said out loud that she wanted it noted that she did not ask whether a company was hiding something, because in about four weeks somebody will say she did. **A man of twenty-nine who works on lifts, told the finding in four sentences, asked nothing, and restated his standing refusal before the question was asked. A first-year of nineteen stated the room's position: we are not going to write that down anywhere, and if somebody writes it down it is a record of a card, and a record of a card says who read the card. A man of thirty-four who buys training answered that he would rather be findable than be the ninth in a row who is not.** And the clerk of forty-four then said, before leaving and unprompted, that she worked in Crown Terrace for about two years a long time ago in an office with nothing to do with this one, that she would remember the name of a room if it were said to her and nothing else, that there were about nine people in it and she typed for all of them, and that she was not important and neither was the typing — and gave the reason for saying it in the room rather than a corridor: that in about four years somebody will find out that a clerk in this district used to type in a building about two hundred metres from where she works, and she would rather it came from her on a Monday. No name was asked for and none was given, and no room, no floor and no date appears in the chapter.

### Chapter 0256 — "Nine Hundred Cards"

**The Wednesday morning of week ninety, a district office, about eleven sites over four days, and a cold room off a service road at about half past six. Load-book entry 259, and its own opening label says this entry is one rule, nine seconds, and about nine hundred.** Ten on the docket and ten in date, and a stair fire door in Nine Bridges for the eighth report in about a month, attended at about three, reset for the sixth time in about a fortnight, expected back. **It took about nine seconds and there is no part of the account anybody would argue with. PS-3-11(c), dated eleven years, not withdrawn, a practice card is not to be issued unless it carries a beat that performs the boundary — and the clerk who said it said before saying it that she did not want to be the one who said it out loud, that this was exactly why she had to, and that in about four years it would be in a newspaper with a photograph of the person who did it on it, and that she is the person who did it. Anybody in that office can invoke it on a Monday without asking anybody. It is not hers. Nobody thanked her. She has never been thanked.** About four days, about nine hundred cards, about eleven sites, and the figure is a figure of objects and not of people and she said so aloud in about nine buildings before she said it anywhere else. **The checking was done by a man of thirty-four who buys training, on his own time, on four days his employer does not know he took and has not been told, driving between about eleven sites against a printed column, thanked at nine and asked at two by a man with a clipboard whether he was from a company, and at the eleventh he said that it is the company that supplies them.** The four days found the two-typeface card in nine of about eleven sites; four of the nine locally reprinted by a printer told he was correcting a misprint, in one of which a man of fifty-eight with a bad hip printed two hundred himself above a fish market, for nothing, out of a habit he cannot stop; about nine people on no rota across four of the sites, two of whom have never been asked whether they want to be there, one being a man of fifty-one with a bad wrist who attends on a Friday and is not on the list; **and, in about nine minutes of a Friday afternoon, a card that had already been withdrawn still on a lanyard round a woman's neck while a person who had not read the list was about to tell her that her release was held and current.** Nobody was hurt, the release was held and current, the woman said the outcome was correct and the hour was wrong, and she was thanked by nobody. **A man of twenty-nine who works on lifts said, unprompted, in about nine words, that the four days were the right four days, that not one of those cards remains in about eleven buildings, and that what is actually happening in about nine of those buildings has not changed and cannot be changed by taking a piece of paper out of a drawer.** He was answered yes and said nothing else. **The clerk of forty-four named the cost of the day nobody is paying for, in a corridor, on the second morning, to one person, in about nine seconds and once: the pulling is one day a week, it is not in her job, nobody is paying for the day, nobody has asked her to stop, and if she stops then in about four years the standard will still be on that wall and nobody will be pulling cards.**

### Chapter 0257 — "Two Sets Of Nine Hundred"

**The Friday of week ninety, the second Friday rota, a company training room in Crown Terrace with eleven people in nine chairs, and a cold room off a service road. Load-book entry 260, and its own opening label says this entry is two figures of nine hundred, one question nobody had asked in nine years, and fifty-three days.** Grade three from twenty to eight on a card in date, eleven on the Friday list and about four of the eleven not on it, the city's standing weekly figure unaltered, and a stair fire door in Nine Bridges for the ninth report in about a month, attended at about half past three, reset for the seventh time in about a fortnight, and found as described. **The cards were gone and the room had nothing to hand out.** The instructor handed in the last copy on the Thursday, unobjected to, and said twice that she wanted it on the record that she did not object, and that a card is a note and you take the note, and that she has been able to name the five parts aloud for nine years and has never once had to. **The four runs went faster, and the reason is hers and is correct: nine people were not looking at a piece of paper. The runs ended on the same spoken word they have ended on for nine years, and that word is the beat which ends a practice and hands the choice back to the practitioner, and it is on no card, has never been on a card, and is not in the box on the post by the door.** A woman of about thirty-four in the second row asked whether the ending could be written down for her to check herself against, and was refused, on one reason used three times rather than three reasons: a sheet becomes a card, a card becomes something in a pocket, and a pocket becomes a session somebody runs for a person who has not got the pocket. **A man of twenty-nine who works on lifts then said the day's actual finding, to the floor: there were nine of us last Friday and there are eleven this week, nobody was told, nobody came because of a card, he does not know one of the eleven faces from any other Friday, he is not going to ask, and if nine hundred people stop being handed a piece of paper and eleven of them turn up instead then that is not an improvement and it is not nothing, and we will not find out which for about four years.** Nobody thanked him. In the cold room a man of fifty-one with a tool bag put the company's own list of the eleven on the counter and established that four of those eleven sites are buildings he has put a number on, and that **the same figure — about nine hundred — has now come out twice in this case for two different sets of objects and nobody in this city can tell them apart in under about four minutes, and that he has wanted that written down for about four years and has never once wanted it written more than he wanted it not to be true.** Nobody wrote it down. A man of twenty-nine who works on lifts was asked, before he had sat down, whether he would go to two of the eleven sites and stand in them for one session each, and said: **No. I said why once and that is all.** Nine words, and not the reason. He is not to be asked again and is not to be thanked. **The rate reached eleven for about four minutes at about ten past four, the second time in about a term and a half, the first having been while about eleven buildings were cleared in standing water. He told nobody, and at about ten on a service road he said the number out loud once to an empty road, and he records that he does not know why a fortnight of water and a room that got two people through the door are the same size, and that the not knowing is the part of it he has.**

### Chapter 0258 — "Nine Seconds, And Then A Letter"

**The Monday morning of week ninety-one, a district office, a cold room off a service road, and a service road in the wet. Load-book entry 261, and its own opening label says this entry is one refusal, four minutes at a counter, and fifty-six days.** Ten on the docket and nine in date, and a basement's standing water reported for the eighth time at about nine inches, down from about sixteen, with nothing done to it by anybody, and he recorded the fall and attributed it to nothing. **A company telephoned the number on the plate at about half past nine**, asked for about forty minutes and said it would bring two people, and gave its name and its company before its reason, which is a thing a person does when the reason is going to sound bad. **A woman of about forty-three with a company lanyard said that her company supplies about eleven of the sites in question, that on Wednesday somebody in a district office pulled about nine hundred of its cards out of use in about four days on the authority of a piece of paper on a wall, that this was the correct thing to do and she is not here about that, and that her employer asked her on Friday whether there is anything in that room that is a problem and she has to write an answer and cannot write an answer about a thing she has not been in — and then said that the request is entirely reasonable, that she would make it, and that this is why she is nervous.** She came at about half past two with one other person, stood in the gap between the counter and the hardboard, looked at the four words in chalk and the unremovable grey ghost for about nine seconds and did not ask what either said, and said unprompted that she has a photograph of that counter in a bag in a car and has looked at it more than her own and that this is a filthy thing to say to a room — and a cook of twenty-seven said it was a filthy thing to say and the first true thing anybody had said at that door in about four months. **A first-year of nineteen refused in about nine seconds: a room which lets a company in is a room that gets quoted, a room that gets quoted is a room that gets asked about, and in about four years somebody will stand in that doorway and say the room agreed to something, and there is no way in this city to make them take it back out of the sentence — and that a company is a company and is not a problem and did nothing wrong, which is exactly why it cannot be in here, and that both readings of the request come to the same answer.** The visitor said it was the cleanest refusal she had been given and that she did not think it was right, and was told to say the rest. **Then nobody could work out who was going to answer the letter.** There is no name over the door, no committee, no minute, no officer and no post, and a written refusal requires a person at the bottom of it, and there were about eleven people in the room and one of them had a name anybody outside that building could use. The cook declined in one sentence. The man of twenty-nine declined in four words and gave no reason and was not asked. The first-year gave the standing position — whoever writes it owns it and is findable and the room will neither stop them nor help them — and then declined to write it, because she is the one person in that room who knows exactly what her own handwriting on about eleven hundred pieces of paper does to a person. **So he wrote it: about four sentences on the company's own notepaper, standing up at the counter, in about four minutes, signed, and read back to the room before it was sealed, which nobody had asked him to do, because a thing you are going to be asked about for four years ought to have been said in front of the people it is about.** The letter says no, thanks you for asking, we are not able to say why, and that if the company wants to be useful in any of this it can go and find the about nine people in those sites who are not on anybody's rota and put somebody in with them. A cook of twenty-seven said that was a good letter and the worst thing that has happened in that room in about four months, and that both of those are about the signature. **The visitor was given four figures out loud once in about nine minutes by a woman of twenty-four standing with her hands behind her back, wrote them on the back of her own company's card, and left no copy because the room does not keep things. She said it does not help her, that what she has to write is that she has seen it, and named her own cost: a file note recording a declined approach, which inside her own employer is a thing she can be found about, and a stated preference for writing it herself rather than have it written about her by the person above her. Nobody thanked her. The company sends nothing further this term.**

### Chapter 0259 — "A Question With One Answer"

**The Wednesday morning of week ninety-one, a district office in District 3, and a cold room off a service road at about half past seven with about nine people and then about eleven. Load-book entry 262, and its own opening label says this entry is one item, two faults in it, and fifty-eight days.** Ten on the docket and ten in date, a stair fire door for the tenth time in about a month attended at about eleven and reset, and a basement not reported since the Monday and not chased, and the entry records that it has not been chased. **The item is four paragraphs, handed to the officer on the Friday, and the question beneath them is whether a practice can be required to show that any person in it may stop at any time, and what evidence would satisfy that.** The hearing is set for the Monday of week ninety-two, one morning, in a room in Crown Terrace with about nine chairs. **She read the item twice, read the standard off the wall binder, and then said it out loud once, standing up, in about nine seconds and not in a corridor, and it was two faults.** One: the woman who pulled about nine hundred cards out of use on Wednesday is now seconded to the secretariat of the committee that is going to hear this, because a committee with an item needs a person to prepare it, and she is the only person in either building who knows what it cost to say it out loud, and the chair has not been told. Two: a practice cannot show that a person may stop at any time, because the only way to show it is to keep a record of who stopped, and a record of who stopped is a column, and a column is the one thing a room like that does not have, and a hearing where the answer is always no is not a hearing, it is a way of producing a finding. She was asked what she wanted to do about it and said nothing, and gave the reason: she is not the person who can do anything, has done nothing, and is speaking so that in about four years nobody can say an officer of that district sat on it. **And then she said the thing she has said once in nineteen years: that she has been right every time she has opened her mouth in that building, that this is not a qualification, that this is the difficulty, and that if a person is ever in a room with her and she is right then that is information about her and none of it is about the matter in front of them.** Nobody wrote it down and the clerk of forty-four, who is in the room, did not look up. **In the evening a woman of twenty-four, who had been asked on the Tuesday in a corridor and had asked for a day, refused before anybody asked her anything to be the person who says it out loud, named the sentence she will not say, and said she knows it is not hers and that not knowing whose it is is a different problem.** Reason given the room, in about nine seconds: if I am the person who says it then the only thing anybody can argue with becomes me; the rule is printed, numbered, dated, hangs on a wall and can be invoked by any competent person on a Monday without asking anybody, and none of that is me; the minute I say it in a room where it matters it becomes a thing a nurse of twenty-four said, and in about four years the sentence anybody reads will be mine and will be true and will also be the only sentence, and then the rule will have got a face on it — and a number on a wall cannot be cross-examined and a person can. **Then she gave the reason under the reason, unprompted, and said so before she said it: she is tired; she has been asked to be findable three times in a term and a half and has said no three times and this is the fourth; tiredness is not a reason and she is going to use it anyway; she would like to be somewhere that is not the answer to the question; and she has been pretending for about a day that a better reason was going to arrive.** **He argued for about four minutes and was wrong:** a clerk reading a number into a record is the only account of the standard that will reach about four hundred competent people on the Monday of week ninety-two, she is the only person he has met who has been inside one of these and come out of it, and if she does not describe what a card does to a person then the description will be a sheet with two typefaces on it and the sheet will be found sufficient. She answered that the card will be read by whoever is clerk of that hearing and read correctly, because it is written down, which is the whole reason it is written down, and that this is the only answer that does not put a person in the middle of a thing no person is required to stand in the middle of. **A first-year of nineteen said it from the end chair with her boots off in nine words: he is wrong, you are right, leave it there — and that he has been wrong about this for a term, said in the same breath as the other six words and not as a separate finding. He accepted it in about four seconds, did not say he would not improve on it, did not repeat it, and did not argue. Nobody thanked him for not arguing.** And she said the last of it to the room and not to him: the evidence she would have given was worth a lot, it is not going to be given, the item will go ahead on a worse record, that is the correct outcome, she is the one who has cost this room the better record, and nobody is to tell her it was not her choice.

### Chapter 0260 — "A Line Is Not A Rule"

**The Thursday evening of week ninety-one, a cold room off a service road from about half past seven until the shutter came down at about ten, and a service road in the wet. Load-book entry 263, and its own opening label says this entry is one line in fourteen words, one cost, and fifty-nine days.** Eleven on the docket and ten in date, a stair fire door for the eleventh time in about a month, attended and reset, and a basement reported for the ninth time at about four inches and found to have fallen again with nothing done to it, and he recorded the fall and attributed it to nothing. **The board over the door carried four words in chalk in the hand of a woman of about sixty-eight and, above them, a grey ghost of four in a sign-writer's hand that has been there since a Friday in week eighty-seven and cannot be got off it, and nobody in that room has ever tried twice. Underneath, on two nails, a hardboard the size of a tea tray carried thirteen lines in seven hands and eight names, the first a cook's and still the first, and the thirteenth a first-year of nineteen's and about five weeks old and an opt-out — and the hardboard is not the board over the door and is not the wall list in its plastic sleeve in a repair shop four districts away, and there are three objects and none has ever been merged with either of the others.** A woman of twenty-four stood up at about nine without asking and named the cost out loud before the mark: that her name is then on a surface in a building with a plate on the outside of it carrying a telephone number and no name, that such a plate is how about eleven things have got to that door in about a term, that in about four years somebody may stand in a room and photograph that hardboard and her handwriting will be on it and she will not be able to take it off, and that she has said no to being findable three times in a term and a half and this is not one of the three.
