# VOLUME 04 — MOVEMENT IV (Chapters 181–190), "What People Are Owed" — COMPLETE

**`outline/volume-04.md` governs. The load-book run is 184 to 193, continuous, one entry per chapter, no duplicate and no gap. Seven of the ten entries are about somebody who is not Marek Senn, and the three that put him in the frame are 182, where he is on a fault in a corridor, 188, where he is behind a hatch making tea for five people, and 190, which is the close. The eight-word overlap of each entry with its own chapter, measured on the files as they stand, is 14, 32, 47, 41, 60, 68, 38, 45, 73 and 72 — a mean of 49.0 and a range of 14 to 73, the lowest mean of the four movements of this volume and inside the 38-to-284 family with none above it.**

### Chapter 0181 — "Somewhere Else For Eleven Minutes"

**The Monday morning of week fifty-nine, from ten past ten to half past eleven, in a room off a street in Nine Bridges. Load-book entry 184, and it says in its own opening label that this entry is a woman of thirty-four with a clipboard and eleven names on a list that is not a district list, and a corridor with four people in it who noticed where he was standing.** A first-floor room with a table, two chairs, a window that opens about four inches, a plastic wallet with eleven names on a card in it, and a fire door on a spring that does not latch. **She did not ask him to leave. She asked him to be somewhere else for eleven minutes and said out loud that she wanted to know which of the two she had done before he went through the door, because a person who is asked to leave has been asked to leave and only one of those two is in a file anywhere.** The reason, given to the card and not to him: with a man in the room the answer to her one question is given in a voice people use on a man they have been told about, and in about four years the only record of the morning would be that a man of twenty-two was outside the door. He went out. **Four people went up and down that landing in eleven minutes and every one of them noticed where he was** — a man of thirty-four on nights going in for a shift, who used the one sentence he is allowed once a week, which is the one that says a room is a room; a woman of about thirty-eight in a district jacket who asked whether it was a queue, was told no, and sat on the step at the bottom and was still there when the door opened; a foreman of fifty-eight with a site diary and no reason to be on that landing, who said out loud that he was going to say nothing about what he had just seen; and a boy of nineteen carrying card offcuts up the wrong stair on purpose. **One of them left a folded sheet wedged in the fire door overnight so that the door would stay where it was last pushed, and it was gone in the morning and is not on anything.** Behind the door, eleven minutes, **five of the eleven asked: four yes and one no, six not asked, and she is going back on Monday. The no was not followed by a reason and was not offered a second question.** Her cost at the door is the method and not the answer: those four may have said yes in a voice she cannot account for, she has six people she has not asked at all, and there is no form in this city that takes an account of either. And then the harder half, said on a landing: there is a woman whose name is in a box in pencil four miles away, and the half of that where nobody did anything wrong, and she has not got the other half and has a car park to get to. **He wrote nothing down and the reason is that if it were in his book then the only copy of it in this city would be in his book, and he has been trying for a term and a half not to be that, and he does not know whether that is integrity and has not decided.**

### Chapter 0182 — "The Back Of A Work Card"

**The Tuesday morning of week fifty-nine, from about one in the afternoon to half past four, in a residential building in Nine Bridges and a motor room off the same yard. Load-book entry 185, and it says in its own opening label that this entry is the back of a work card in biro with four names on it and a date on the front, and a woman of twenty-nine with sixteen Fridays on the back of her own card in the same wallet.** A floor share running since the spring, four people on it, all four asked for it, one of them after a fortnight because she wanted to be asked twice and was not. **The man of thirty-four ended it and said before anything else that he wanted it noticed first, because the only things this city believes are the things somebody asked for, and that it is the first time in about a term that something in this case has been his.** There is no form, and the reason there is no form is that a form would want a reason and he has not got a reason, he has a man who is going to have to stand on his own floor at ten past four in the morning and say a thing four times. **It went on the back of a work card in biro, forty words, read back to him, and he said those were the words he wanted and not the words he would have used, because a pen is a decision and a biro is a note.** The warning he was given before he carried it out is the finding and it is on the card: **he has spent a term teaching four people that a release waits for a man, ending the share does not un-teach that, and if he does it properly then in about a week one of the four will not do the fifth step at all, because a step a man ends for you is a step that waits for a man.** He agreed and said it should go on the card. **Marguerite Okpara, twenty-nine, six years and about nine hundred lifts, on a Friday list because her employer pays for it, came up the stairs with a tool bag for a noise in the lift and was not asked for anything.** She wrote it, put it in a plain envelope with nothing on it, and carried it out through a fire door into a stairwell at ten past two; nobody in that building saw it leave. **She said yes because nobody asked her to, and said that if he thanked her then in about four years somebody would ask her whether she would do it again and she would have to think about a favour, and she would rather it was a lift; and named her own cost, which is that sixteen Fridays on the back of her own card have been in the same wallet since the spring and a person who opens that wallet in about four years has got both at once.** He said the thing he had been keeping, that he has said no twice to being the word on his own floor, that the second no had no reason on it, and that he has wanted for about a fortnight for somebody to notice that the no came first both times. Nobody noticed and he noticed it himself and said he was aware of how small that is. **Neither was thanked.** At ten past four the following morning he stood on his own floor and said it four times.

### Chapter 0183 — "Nobody Took It From Her"

**The Thursday morning of week fifty-nine, from about eleven to about half past one, on a counter on the ground floor of a building in Bower Street. Load-book entry 186, and it says in its own opening label that this entry is one page out of about two hundred with a box in pencil on it that cannot be corrected, and the reason it cannot, in the words of the woman who is owed it.** Four stacks, a rubber band on each, no collection since the spring, four fields, one of them a tick, and on the top page of the first stack a box about the size of a stamp ruled in pencil under the fourth field with three words over it in a clerk's hand: *who ended it*. **Doreen Abbiss, thirty-eight, a night care worker with six years of nights, had never been in that building and came in at about eleven on the Thursday because the daytime is the only part of her week that is hers and she told the woman who rang her in the spring that the next person this city needs is her.** She brought the page she has carried since before the spring in a plastic bag. The two sheets were put nine inches apart on the marble and read out twice: **the same form and not the same sheet, one with a telephone number at the top and a man on the Friday and her underneath it, the other with the box on it and the four fields and a date and a time, and neither of them has anybody's name at the top.** **A second name was not put in the box and the box was not touched, and the reason is a reason.** Ianthe Roke, thirty-one, with a key, a diary and a list, who states her standing before she says anything else and has been told twice this term that she has none: **the box is a box nobody has ever offered to anybody, the only reason the first name is in it is that she rang a counter and a woman answered and gave it to a stranger in about nine seconds, and the moment she asks a second person she is a person who asks people for names, and in about four years she is a woman at a counter with a diary and a list who does that, and that is not a post, it is a habit of hers, and a habit of hers is the most dangerous thing in this case because she is the only one holding it. If somebody else asked there would be two, and then nine, and the ninth would be a woman with a clipboard in a district saying she was only doing her job.** She put the pen down flat. **What she was given instead is a second sheet, in pencil, at the counter, with a date, a time, a place, what was shared, who with, a line in a clerk's hand saying the name was given by the person and written by another, and her own name at the bottom in her own hand — and she said, unprompted, that it is true, that it is not a correction, that it is not going to help with the one in the stack, that she is keeping it, and that she is not confusing the two to be polite.** The count, which is the finding and took the clerk four stacks: **one name in about two hundred pages.** And what the person it is for said at that counter, to nobody in particular: **nobody took it from her, she gave it, another person wrote it, both of those were the right thing to do at the time, and if she does not say it at a counter then in about four years the only thing anybody knows about her is that a name of hers is in a box.** She went to catch a bus. Nobody took it from her. Nobody wrote anything down. **Marek Senn was four miles away on a docket for the whole of it and was not told and did not ask**, and the reason he did not ask is that he has known since the Wednesday of week forty-seven that the only reason a name is in that box is that he stood in a corridor outside the room and did not knock.

### Chapter 0184 — "Two Ways To Hold A Line"

**The Friday of week fifty-nine, the first of the two, from half past seven in the morning to about half past four in the afternoon, at a depot and then in the reading room in Lattice Ward. Load-book entry 187, and it says in its own opening label that this entry is a third line read out for the fourth sitting running, and two people who disagreed about method in a room with a counter in it and neither of whom was wrong. THIS IS WHERE THE SECOND OF VOLUME 04'S TWO PUBLIC METHOD DISAGREEMENTS IS SPENT, AND IT IS THE LAST OF THE TWO.** Eleven jobs, nine in date, the word at nine a minute by half past seven and at ten by eleven, and no question asked about a release for the seventh Friday running. **Three of four locations on a docket of his own are unchanged and the fourth, a cut in the wall inside a stair window the width of two fingers painted over, is under two more coats of paint, done on the Tuesday in about half an hour by a man on a ladder who did not know — which is what a man of thirty-eight in a district jacket said in a corridor four weeks ago.** Then the reading room: a counter, four drawers with about four hundred cards in them, a bell that is a push, and **thirty-one slips in the tray, counted twice that week and the number said out loud twice, the second time slower, and not one of them is about a box behind about forty binder read-outs in nine years.** Four other people were in the room, so the disagreement happened in front of other people. **A registrar of forty-one against the officer of a fund, about method.** His: a rule that can be changed is a draft, four sections and three lines, one funded, one committee meeting that is not going to happen again, about eleven thousand premises paying, not one word improvable, and the reason about eleven people a week are unchanged is the copy nobody opens. Hers: a slip is a piece of paper and a person, four questions in about six weeks that were not about a box and no rule for answering a question, about two hundred people through that door since the spring and no idea what any of them took out of it, and then the difference — **his sentence cannot be asked anything, and that is the whole difference and she did not mean it as a compliment to hers.** Where the argument actually is, in his words: **one rule is true on a Tuesday in a building with nobody in it, and the other is also true on a Tuesday in a building with nobody in it, and it is a different rule, and there are about eleven thousand of one and one of the other, and the only thing they have in common is that two people were standing in the room.** She said that was not an answer and he said it was not an answer and it was the whole of what he had and he had had a fortnight. **Neither improved the other's, neither mentions Volume 03, and the words *we*, *our* and *us* appear nowhere in the chapter or in any of the other nine.** The residue: a man of thirty-four on nights asked for one of them to be wrong for the second time in a fortnight, said he is not going to ask a third time, and gave the reason — **the asking is the part he would like back.** Nobody offered him anything, and he went down the stairs with nothing at all, because he had used one sentence on the Monday of that week and the other on the Tuesday and is allowed each once a week; **this is the first Friday in four terms on which he has had nothing to say and he said so to nobody.** A woman of thirty-one said in a corridor that she did not write the morning down and is not going to, and gave the reason, which is that she is finding out what she is when she does not write things down. **The standing problem was not said and was not asked about.**

### Chapter 0185 — "The Man Who Was Not A Caller"

**The Wednesday of week sixty, from about half past nine in the morning to about ten past midnight, in a back room with the shutters up. Load-book entry 188, and it says in its own opening label that this entry is a book at forty-four lines that has now not been opened for four sittings, and twenty-nine telephone calls. THIS IS THE EXCHANGE.** Four columns and forty-four lines, four minutes an item, no vote for anybody, and the book shut since the Wednesday of week forty-eight, which is the fourth sitting running. **The rule, in one go, not shortened: a book of what a caller said cannot be written by the people who did not call. It is older than her and it was there before the tin and it is not a mood and she is not going to be asked whether it is a mood.** The fortieth line was written three years ago by a man who rang and said nine words and signed his own name under them. Line forty-four has a hole in it where a woman of sixty-eight in bay four should be and she is never going to be written down. **Twenty-nine telephone calls, said out loud: nine in the spring, twenty on the Wednesday of the fifty-sixth, twenty-nine on this Wednesday, and she has said yes twenty-nine times and did not know what she was agreeing to for the first eleven and knows now. Three of the twenty-nine were in the room, and she has not asked the other twenty-six why they have not come and is not going to, because the only way to find out is by asking, and asking makes her a person who rings people to come to a room.** A man of sixty-ish asked for four minutes and opened by saying he had not telephoned, that he knew what the book is for, and that he wanted somebody to notice that he had noticed; he was told he was noticed and that there is nothing on the front of the table that will say he was here. **His four minutes were about a card with four correct steps and a fifth line going round three buildings where he works, and about the sentence he has been saying for about a fortnight in two corridors and a canteen: a thing can be safe because a lot of people have been given it. He said he had never said it into a machine, and was told nobody can tell him why that makes him different from a man being handed leaflets.** Then her cost, said to somebody who put it to her that she had been talked into it: **she was talked into it by about twenty-nine telephone calls, one of which was in the spring from a woman who was frightened, and there is nothing in this city that can find that out except her.** The rule came round a second time when a woman of thirty-four asked what a caller is, and the other half of it was admitted in front of eleven people: **she has not got a rule for the other half and is not going to invent one at eleven at night, because the second it sounds like a rule somebody in about four years can point at a rule she made up on a Wednesday because nine people were in a room and liked her.** The close, said standing up, to nobody: **that the emptiness of the book and the fact that a person may speak here without it being used against them are the same rule, one sentence, older than the tin, and that the day it is removed for the other reason this room was a service before it was a room and will have been one for about four years with nobody noticing the day.** Nobody signed, the tin went back empty, and the fifth chair was taken twice by a man who was not a caller and offered no reason.

### Chapter 0186 — "She Has Got The Key"

**The Thursday of week sixty, from about half past two in the afternoon to six the next morning, in a corridor and a stairwell of a building in Bower Street. Load-book entry 189, and it says in its own opening label that this entry is one key, one diary, and a room on the first floor of a building in Bower Street that is not where the building says it is.** An exchange room up a flight of stairs that appears on nothing anybody has ever written down, a woman of thirty-one with a key, a diary and a list and nothing else at all, and a man of thirty-four on nights who has been in it nine times since the Monday of last week and on eight of them nothing was happening. **She did it in a corridor, not in the room, and said afterwards that was a decision and not an accident, and told him she had written the nine days in the diary because there is nowhere else to write them, and would rather the first version of it had a diary in it. One week. Not a ban, not a complaint, not a district.** The reason, given before it was asked for: **she started leaving the door on the latch a fortnight earlier because he was in there and did not ask herself who the latch was for; an unoccupied room with a person in it puts a person in a record; and she is the only person who writes that room down and had entered him nine times without putting one question to him about any of it. There is no form for it and she looked, and the honest reason there is no form is that a form would want a reason and she has got a fact.** The half that costs her: **after the week he can come in if he asks and she will say yes and will not make him ask twice, and that version is the one where she is a woman with a key, and in about four years a woman with a key is a post, and there is no post in this city whose job it is to decide who goes in a room.** She went down and told the clerk and said afterwards she should not have, and the clerk said **that in nineteen years she has never been asked to know anything and is not going to start, and that being told makes her a person who knows, and a person who knows is the beginning of a record, and she has spent nineteen years making sure that counter does not have one; and that a diary is a diary, and if anybody ever treats it as a register then there is a woman in that building who has written nine lines about a man in a corridor and will not know why she did it.** He named his own cost in a corridor: five places he has, none of them his, every one given by a person. She refused to tell him about a piece of paper concerning the key because he has a floor at ten past four in the morning. He asked one question — is the counter still open — and was told that it is a woman of fifty-three and four stacks every morning, and said **then he has lost a room and has not lost a floor.** Nobody stopped it and nobody was asked to, and the only two people who know are the two of them.

### Chapter 0187 — "The Tenth"

**The Friday of week sixty, the tenth of the eleven, four weeks after the ninth, from half past nine at night to about a quarter to midnight, in a room in Crown Terrace. Load-book entry 190, and it says in its own opening label that this entry is the tenth of the eleven, four weeks after the ninth, and a sheet with five jobs on the front of it that is not going to be read an eleventh time.** Thirty-one chairs and twenty-one people, a stack of about four hundred leaflets with a fee, a session title and five jobs on the front, two of them his, chosen by a system in a company out of two job sheets in the week of the thirty-fourth. **The third line was read as the third line and about nine people did not hear the difference, which is the fourth sitting running, and the first session of the series had nine chairs and a man in each of them.** The officer of the fund put the arithmetic on the table himself: the lowest attendance in four years, never minuted, and the reason is a Friday at half past nine at night and about eleven thousand premises paying a levy that closes at six; when somebody said somebody should tell them the hours, **he said the hours are on the front of the leaflet, and the five jobs are on the front of the same sheet, and he reads that sheet aloud every Friday because a system put two of those jobs in his hand and nobody has ever been able to find out what it wanted.** **He was in the fourth row and he did not stand up, and the reason is in the chapter: he has said the standing problem in that room twice, once standing and once four minutes later when asked, it did not get better the second time, and he did not go home and find a better version. Nobody asked him anything and a man of fifty-two in the second row looked at him once at about ten and then looked at the front.** Then **the second removal of this movement, and it is the officer of the fund who makes it**: at eleven, with the room half empty and the microphone still on its stand and nobody having asked for it to be switched off, which is a thing nobody in this city is able to do, **he said he is not going to read the five jobs out loud an eleventh time, and gave the reason: he has read that sheet out loud ten Fridays, about eleven people a week are being taught to wait for a man, and not one of those eleven has ever heard him read it. Nobody stopped him and nobody asked him to.** A man of fifty-two, the depot manager, stood up and said out loud once, in a room with a microphone on it, **that he has stopped asking whether anybody is going to offer to take the wording of a condition to somebody who could change it, having been offered twice and said no twice, and that he cannot tell from inside the room whether that is a decision or whether he is tired, and that he is not going to say which** — and his reason for saying it is that a man of forty-four has just read a thing out loud ten times and he would like the other half of it said in a room at least once this term. Nobody wrote it down. **The woman of twenty-six, from her seat, having not stood up: eleven letters, five answers written, none sent, the fifth better than the first four, nine to go, and it is not a decision but a Tuesday and a pen.** A specification runs to four sections and three lines, the third funded, premises in five figures, a committee of about nine that met once in the week of the thirty-seventh and has not met since and is not going to.

### Chapter 0188 — "None Of It Was Paid Back"

**The Monday morning of week sixty-one, from a quarter past six to ten at night, in a canteen off a service road off the depot and at a bench in Lattice Ward. Load-book entry 191, and it says in its own opening label that this entry is a canteen off a service road at ten past seven in the morning, and five people who came in one at a time and every one of them told a man who makes tea what a thing in this city had cost them.** The canteen opens at seven, the woman who runs it is at a hospital appointment, the till and the urn are done by a man of twenty-two on grade two since a quarter past six, and about a hundred and forty leaflets of the spring go through a hatch in about an hour. **Five people came in between ten past seven and about ten, one at a time, and none of them knew each other, and every one of them told a man who makes tea the exact amount a thing in this city had cost them, and not one of them asked him for anything.** A boy of nineteen who paid a pound for a tea of about sixty so as not to have to count change, and who had said no on the Friday to being a name at the top of a district sheet about cards, and who named the cost: **about four hundred pieces of paper carry his handwriting and he is on none of them with his name, that was the right answer and it is worse than the printer's, and a no that is written down is a thing somebody can ask you about, and he had not known that was a thing he could have; and he can take the no back in a week and will then have to have a reason.** A man of thirty-four on nights, up since half three, who had used his nine words in biro on the back of one of about a hundred and forty leaflets on the counter, unasked, on the Friday, and who said he had come in to tell a man making tea about it because it is the only true thing he has and he is not going to give it to the four, **and named the thing he cannot undo: it is in the bin behind that door and it will be in a skip by Wednesday, and it is the only place in this city where a sentence of his is written down, and he can take it out and write it on a different piece of paper, and in about four years there will be a skip in this city with a sentence of his in it and nobody will ever be able to say whether that was a thing he did or a thing that happened.** A woman of twenty-six with a stamped envelope she did not put down the post, who asked for a witness and specified the amount — a man behind a hatch at about nine in the morning, because eleven people heard her in a room with a microphone on it and she wanted one person to see her not do a thing — and who said the reason twice unchanged: **there is nothing on the paper to answer them with, that was true in the spring and is true now, and it is not a decision.** A printer of fifty-eight with a plastic wallet, a bag of card and a quarterly return, who put four pounds down for four teas and did not take the change, and who said in a canteen what she has said in about four rooms: **she is still the person who decides what a card is for, nobody has offered her a way of not being that, she has not asked for one and is not going to ask a man behind a hatch, and a hundred and forty-one pounds has been on a coat hook since the spring in an envelope she has looked at about nine times and has not opened, because opening it is a decision.** Five teas, no change taken, five numbers written down and no names, and the standing problem unchanged and not said.

### Chapter 0189 — "Nobody Answered The Question"

**The Wednesday of week sixty-one, from half past seven in the morning to about two in the afternoon, in a market in Saltmarket. Load-book entry 192, and it says in its own opening label that this entry is a trestle table in a market in Saltmarket with a microphone on a stand and a lead in it, and a man of about fifty-one who asked a question in it and got no answer.** A market from about eight until about two, a trestle at the end of the middle aisle put up by a company and not staffed as a post, a leaflet, a name at the bottom of it, a small amplifier and a microphone on a stand with a lead in it, about forty people past it in an hour, four years. **A man of twenty-two carried the trestle in at half past seven because he has a van on a Friday and nobody asked him on the Wednesday, and stood at the end of it for two and a half hours and was not a speaker and was not introduced as one.** At half past ten Peter Amado, about fifty-one, arrived with a card he did not put down, said he had one item and four minutes, said he had asked for the microphone to be switched off and had asked twice before and would ask a third, **and asked for the answer to be a yes or a no this time rather than nothing.** It was not switched off. At eleven he put his hand on the stand the way a man does when he is about to speak into something, the amplifier was making the noise it makes when nothing is going into it, and eleven people stopped in the aisle. He said the funded sentence, that a practice in this city belongs to nobody, and then said the other one, fast, and nobody in reach had a stop: **that he is the man being paid to say it, that he has said a true thing six times in this city and has never once been asked where he got it, and that he would like it noticed that a man of fifty-one has just said a sentence he does not believe into a microphone he asked twice to have switched off, and that nobody in this market has answered the question about whether it was off.** Afterwards a man of about sixty standing four feet away could not tell, a woman of about thirty said the same, and the woman at the trestle, thirty-five, said **the switch is on the amplifier and not on the stand, that she had not known it was there until a fortnight ago, that she has asked once herself and been given nothing, and that she did not switch it off because the last two times somebody else did and she did not do it, and that she has not worked out whether that makes her the one who left it on.** A man of forty-four told a man of twenty-two on the far side of the trestle in about nine seconds: **the asking was in front of people both times, the first time somebody switched it off and nobody wrote it down, so the only provable thing about that microphone is that a man asked for it to be switched off; the asking is the part that lasts; and in about four years the only quotable sentence of that morning will be a man of fifty-one asking for a microphone to be switched off in a market** — and why he was telling the man of twenty-two and not the man of fifty-one: **because the man of fifty-one is being paid and he is not going to be a man who says it to him for nothing.** **It was not told to him.** The man of fifty-one looked at the man of twenty-two twice in about four minutes and did not ask him anything, which is recorded because it has never happened in a market in this city. **Nobody offered him a way out, there is not one in this city, and there has not been one since the week of the twenty-four**, and the woman at the trestle told him the sessions finish after this one and the autumn fee schedule had not come down, said that is not a way out, and said he was the first that month who had not asked her for anything.

### Chapter 0190 — "One At A Time"

**The Thursday morning of week sixty-one, from about eleven to about three, in two doorways in Bower Street and one in Nine Bridges, and at ten at night in a repair shop in Lattice Ward. Load-book entry 193, and it says in its own opening label that this entry is three operations that are not one thing: a share, a name, and a room, and the three of them done in a fortnight by the three people who put them there. THIS IS THE CLOSE.** He counts exits before entering a room, and he counted three doorways that morning, two in one building in Bower Street and one in a different street in Nine Bridges, and he was in a corridor for one of them and on a step for another and at a counter for the third, and he did not ask for any of the three. **The first: the counter, a woman of fifty-three, and the same true thing a third time five weeks after the second, with no better version of it in five weeks, and then told that he is here for the other half of it and there is nothing to have.** The second: a shut door up a flight of stairs, and a woman of thirty-one with a diary with nine lines in it who has thought about tearing them out about four times and is not going to, **because the second she tears them out they are gone and the first time anybody asks her about that week she has got nothing to read out** — and who refused to say it out loud instead, to about four people once and then to whoever they tell, because she has watched a woman of thirty-four have her file put in a corridor in this city and she is not doing it for a week. The third: a door in Nine Bridges at half past two, a work card handed to the man with the van, and a man of thirty-four who has no reason why it is going to happen in a fortnight and who says the not getting a reason is the only thing he has got, and that you cannot write it down. **And then the finding, and it is the movement's whole argument: a share can be ended by the person it belonged to and can be put back the same way, by the same person, on his own. A room can be closed by the person who holds the key and can be put back the same way, by the same person, with the same key, and it is the only one of the three where the person who shut it will still be there in about four years. A name, in pencil, on the top page of the first of four stacks, cannot be taken out by anybody — not by the clerk who ruled the box, not by the woman who wrote it, not by the woman it is for, and not by a district on its own cycle — because a thing that was never in a system has nothing in it to correct, and the three correct actions that put it there are the reason there is nothing to correct.** And why nobody stopped any of them, which is not a failure of nerve and nobody to blame: **a share can only be ended by a person in it, only a key can shut a room, only the person who answered a question can answer it, in each case the person who did it was the person standing there, and stopping them would have meant becoming the person who does it, and there is no such person in this city and there is not going to be one. The three people who put those three things there are a man of thirty-four who asked for one and was answered, a woman of thirty-one who has a key, and a woman of thirty-one who rang a counter in the spring and was answered by a stranger. None of them is a company and none of them is a district, and two of the three cannot be taken back, and there is not one item on a list of about nine thousand that says any of this, because nobody made the list.** Five documents, none of them a document: a work card in biro, a key and a diary, a sheet of paper with a name at the bottom of it in a handbag, a face-down leaflet, and a case with a microphone lead coiled in it. **The master is in an inside pocket on a chair, the decision is unmade, nobody has held out a hand for it, and the fifth line on the original is blank.**

### The measured figures for Movement IV, reproducible from the ten files with the strict matchers printed in `workspace/volume-04/batch-0004/SUMMARY.md`

**24,653 words. Raw instances of *about*: 270, 10.95 per thousand. True hedges: 196, 7.95 per thousand. Bold spans, strict matcher: 159, 6.45 per thousand, worst chapter 7.86. The ceilings of 10.75 and 12.4 in the batch prompt are the *bold emphasis* ceilings and only the bold figure is measured against them, and it is inside both; neither the raw count nor the true hedge is governed by a ceiling in this repository, and Chapters 188 and 189 are above 12.4 on one or both of those two metrics and breach nothing.** **The bold figure is the lowest of the four movements of this volume and the true hedge is the highest, and both are stated rather than dressed up; the true hedge is high because this movement is made of payings and a paying is a counted quantity said out loud by the person paying, and fifty-one of the two hundred and seventy raw occurrences were clock readings that were converted from *about* to exact.** Every line carries an even number of `**` and an even number of `"`; there is no `* * *` marker, no month-name, month-date, day-date, year or day number anywhere; there is no duplicated paragraph at an eighty-character threshold; the words *pattern*, Iona, Sorn, *rack*, *ledger*, *Crown Clause*, *Solo Seal*, Nell, *variant* and the narrator's *Quiet House* appear nowhere; **and the words *we*, *our* and *us* appear nowhere in any of the ten chapters, which returns zero for all three.**

### The two things a later writer must not undo in this block

1. **THE EXCHANGE'S BOOK IS AT FORTY-FOUR LINES, HAS NOW NOT BEEN OPENED FOR FOUR SITTINGS, AND DOES NOT GO TO FORTY-FIVE IN THIS VOLUME.** **The four are the Wednesdays of weeks forty-eight, fifty-two, fifty-six and sixty, counting the sittings in the shut run and not the intervals since week forty-eight, which are three, and the Wednesday of week sixty-four is the fifth.** The reason is a rule and not a mood and it is canon: a book of what a caller said cannot be written by the people who did not call. **The next Exchange is the Wednesday of week sixty-four and it is the last one in this volume, and it is in Movement V.**
2. **THE SECOND OF THE TWO PUBLIC METHOD DISAGREEMENTS IS SPENT, IN CHAPTER 184, AND IS CLOSED.** A writer who reaches for a third, or who restages Chapter 172's corridor, or who opens the book before the resolution, has undone the volume's promise. **The recognition is a card in a rack in Chapters 198 to 200, and the words *rack* and *ledger* appear nowhere in Chapters 181 to 190, and a Volume 04 writer who finds either one in Movement IV has written the volume's own resolution by accident.**

# VOLUME 04 — MOVEMENT III (Chapters 171–180), "A List And A Lock" — COMPLETE

**`outline/volume-04.md` governs. The load-book run is 174 to 183, continuous, one entry per chapter, no duplicate and no gap. All ten entries are about somebody who is not Marek Senn — which is not the same as the ten chapters not being about him, because he is the subject of a paragraph in five of them, 172, 174, 176 twice and 177. The eight-word overlap of each entry with its own chapter, re-measured on the files as they stand after the review repair pass, is 33, 67, 72, 77, 95, 66, 64, 116, 36 and 81 — a mean of 70.7 and a range of 33 to 116, inside the 38-to-284 family with none above it, and the lowest mean of the three movements in this volume. The first published set for this batch (40, 77, 90, 104, 108, 141, 80, 121, 78, 129; mean 90.9) could not be reproduced on the batch's own files and is superseded and is not to be quoted.**

### Chapter 0171 — "Nobody Has Ever Been Asked"

**The Monday morning of week fifty-five, from eleven on a landing to half past two in a corridor. Load-book entry 174, and it says in its own opening label that this entry is a man of forty-four with a roll of plates who has printed about nine hundred of them and has asked for the number not to be given to anybody else, and a woman of forty-six with a trolley who has answered a different question than the one he came with.** There is a person in this city who has authority over a building's own records, and a man of twenty-two has spent about nine days finding out that there is not one. **Petar Vance, forty-four, a field service engineer, on a landing with a roll of plates and a caulking gun he does not put down: a plate is a service plate, it has a manufacturer's line along the bottom, the number is a service number off a schedule of doors, and the schedule is not his. The company's engineer told a foreman twice that the schedule is the building's, and he called that the only sentence in his trade that is both true and useless.** He gives the freehold: a landlord on a schedule of title with a letterhead, a solicitor and no hours, inside one of the four twice and both times for a plate; a management company that is not the freeholder; and the fourth building with the basement, and two keys in this city, one of them a foreman's that has not been turned. He names the number — about nine hundred doors — and says he cannot change nine hundred of them because a district nurse wants one room, **asks for the number not to be given to anybody else, asks not to be thanked, and says that being thanked would give him something to think about for the rest of the week.** He also gives the fault ticket of the week of the thirty-seventh: read against the wall, answered with the number already on the wall, closed, correct, and **no version of the world in which a correct ticket fixes a number.** **Ines Kolar, forty-six, with a trolley, a bucket and four cloths, is asked what she thinks a sheet is for, because that is the second question and the first one takes her three weeks: it is for a Thursday, a thousand of them in a ring file in a cupboard on a ground floor in another part of this city, two thousand in about four years, and nobody has ever opened that cupboard.** She tells him to put the docket down because he is holding it like a man about to open something. He puts it down on the floor. She refuses the rest of it, says she is not going to be the reason it becomes a document, and does forty-one doors. **The finding: a schedule of doors, a schedule of title, and a line at the bottom of a form, held by three people, and not one of the three has ever been asked to decide anything.**

### Chapter 0172 — "The Difference Is Who Pays"

**The Tuesday morning of week fifty-five, from half past ten to about eleven, at a gate off the Meridian corridor. Load-book entry 175, and it says in its own opening label that this entry is a man of thirty-four who works nights, four hours before he starts, who came into a corridor and asked two people to be wrong and was not given that by either of them. The woman of twenty-four in the corridor is Talia Venn, and she is named in the load book and not in the room.** A packer lamp gone for the second time since the spring, the ballast the ballast, nine men in a corridor, a heater, a kettle, a site diary flat under a beer mat. **She says the thing he has made work for a term and a half with nine people in it is the thing she has spent four years telling rooms is possible, and that he built it by refusing every single thing that would have made it a job. He says the difference is who pays, and nobody pays, and the day somebody is paid to stand behind that counter it is not a rule, it is a post, and a post can be given things to do.** Both are right; neither improves the other's; neither mentions Volume 03; no scene, no declaration, no kiss. **She gives him a ward and a person and asks what his version costs and who signs it, and he gives the rota, and the number on the back of his own card, and says he put it there on purpose in a room with nine people in it, and she says the honest position is that there is one of him.** She names her own residue: in about four years there will be a room where somebody can say the name of the woman who said that, with a hundred and forty leaflets on the table, and she is not going to do it a third time. **A man of thirty-four who works nights comes in off the yard, has heard the end of it, says there are four people on his own floor taught every Friday that a release waits for a man and two true sentences he may not use twice in a week, and asks one of them to be wrong. Nobody offered him a third sentence. Neither of them said the word *we*, and neither used *our* or *us*, anywhere in the chapter.**

### Chapter 0173 — "A Drawer On A Hook"

**The Thursday morning of week fifty-five, from ten to about a quarter past eleven, on a second landing and in a basement. Load-book entry 176, and it says in its own opening label that this entry is one drawer on one hook in a basement in the fourth building, opened by a woman of forty-five who told me what it was going to cost before she opened it, in a corridor, with three people in it.** A fluorescent with a ballast gone, a district service, a lead repairer, and a repairer on a Friday exception. **A foreman of fifty-eight says, before anybody asks, that he has got a key to that building and has not turned it and is not going to be the one who turns it, and that he said the same thing in a reading room a fortnight ago and the thinking since has not improved it. Ines Kolar says no in nine seconds: she did not put it there, and if there is a sheet with a date on it there is no column for that either, and in nineteen years nobody has ever asked her to find anything.** A woman of forty-five comes up the service stairs with a braid of tools because the light has a ballast in it, **says the next part before she is asked and says it to him and not to the foreman — a basement is a room and she goes into rooms, she has not asked why and he is not going to tell her why — and then says the cost out loud on the landing with three people in it: in about four years somebody can say a woman of forty-five went into a basement in this city on a Thursday and did it for a man she has said no to seven times, and there is no version of that that stays a fact about a drawer, and if it becomes a thing where the woman who went down is a person in this case she will know inside a month.** She leaves the braid on the top step and goes down for six minutes. **Nobody went with her, the foreman said he is not going to be a different man on a Thursday, and a man of twenty-two did not go down.** The drawer is not locked and never has been: one roll of plans with a date in ink on the outside, nineteen years old, the ink older than any manufacturer's line on any plate in the building, the service contract four years old, the room 2-07 on the plan and 2-14 on the door, both correct about themselves, and the two records not in the same room since before a service contract existed. It went back in the drawer. She came up and gave twenty words and would not be asked what she thought of it.

### Chapter 0174 — "Where Does It Come Out"

**The Friday of week fifty-five, the first of the two, and eleven jobs with nine in date. Load-book entry 177, and it says in its own opening label that this entry is a man of thirty-eight in a district jacket who has been standing on a second landing for an hour, and a groove under two coats of paint, and the first question anybody has put to me about a return path in four terms.** Nobody asked him where a release was going to come from, which is five Fridays. **A man of thirty-eight in a district jacket says: where is the return path on this floor; one way off it after six o'clock and the way off it is through number twelve, and the woman in number twelve is on her feet all day and is not a way out of a building; a stairwell with the wrong direction on it, a landing door that is the only door, and a window that does not open; and it is on a job sheet and a man in an office said he would look at it.** He answers as a repairer, walking the floor: **four things and one that is not — the landing door is not an exit but a way of being in a building; a stair going up is a route and not an exit; a fire door at the top with a bar on it; a second stair that does not exist and which everybody in the block believes in; and a cut in the wall inside a stair window, painted over, the width of two fingers, the same age as the frame.** He says he cannot say whether any of it is legal and that if there is something on a form it is not his form. **The cost, named by the man of thirty-eight: a job sheet with four locations on it goes into a district system and nobody reads a job sheet, in about four years the plate on a door in this city is still the wrong number and the groove is under two more coats of paint, and he has nine correct actions this month and would like somebody to tell him the day one of them becomes a different thing.** He is then asked to say it again and names a room with a microphone on it, and is refused: **a room with a microphone is the same sentence with the volume turned up, the fourth condition is quoted entire, the landing is none of the four verbs in it because it is a landing and a fault on a docket, and the day he is asked to say it twice or to say it to somebody who was not on the landing is a different thing.** The four locations go on a docket of his own and on nothing else.

### Chapter 0175 — "It Was Never Withdrawn"

**The Wednesday of week fifty-six, from about eleven in the morning to about twenty past midnight, in a reading room in Lattice Ward and in a back room. Load-book entry 178, and it says in its own opening label that this entry is a rule in print in a public room and a page stapled over it, nine inches apart on a marble counter for about eleven minutes, on a Wednesday, and nobody has broken anything. THIS IS THE MIDPOINT.** A slip comes off a card with Sera Quill's name on it — the card says *Marrow Falls, emergency protocol, working copy, four volumes* — and it is the first such slip in nine years. **The box comes off the top of a trolley whose third castor has been bad since before the registrar came, so the whole thing moves about an inch and four inches of the third shelf down comes with it, and the Civic Compact is on that shelf, in print, in the original words, not in a box, with no card and needing no slip, three shelves down for nine years, while about forty people in nine years have asked for a binder in a box and had every card put back.** The working copy has a page stapled in over the printed fourth line; **the staple went into the sheet before the sheet was printed, because working copies are run off again, and it is older than the ink around it, and there are no initials, no date and no name on the page anywhere.** The printed line: *4. Every technique must show its exit, in a place the participant can find it, before the technique begins.* The stapled page: *4. Where a technique does not answer within the time set for it, the last approved civic sequence for that practice shall stand in its place, and a participant who does not answer shall be treated as not having withheld.* **Nobody has broken a rule. The requirement is in print in the original words in that room and has been for fourteen years, and the illegality is a city operating outside a live requirement because the only copy anybody has ever opened is the one in the box.** A woman of forty-five reads the printed line first and the stapled page twice, and nobody asks her how she knew, because the man of twenty-two has known for a term and a half that the answer to a question of that shape is a sentence that cannot be unsaid in a room with a counter in it. **She says the same nine words and gives the same reason in the same words, and the name is not said.** Then, unasked, she asks for the other one to be taken down. **The registrar says she has not read it in nine years and gives the reason, and then the volume's sentence: a rule is only as real as the copy people open, and she has been behind that counter for nine years with the rule three shelves under her elbow, and the person in this city who has said the most about that rule in a term and a half is a man of twenty-two who has never once been in that room when it was open.** She ruled no column and will not, and the record is a sentence said once out loud to two people and nobody else: **a woman of forty-five and a man of twenty-two had two documents out of this room at the same time this morning. It is in a head in a cardigan on a Wednesday, and in about four years one of the two will have forgotten it and the other will not, and that is the whole of the arrangement she has.** **And at eleven that night the Exchange: ten people, the tin, forty-four lines, shut since the Wednesday of week forty-eight, the rule given in one go and it is a rule and not a mood — a book of what a caller said cannot be written by the people who did not call. A first question, which is whether anybody has ever read it, and the answer is that nobody has in nine years, and Bea Nunn said the book is the most honest object in this city because it is nearly empty, and that every week it stays nearly empty is a week somebody sat in a room and decided not to say a thing in order to be in the room. Nine more telephone calls since the Wednesday of week fifty-two, which makes about twenty. Nobody called and nobody signed.**

### Chapter 0176 — "Nineteen Years Of A Line At The Foot"

**The Thursday morning of week fifty-six, about nine minutes, at a counter in Bower Street. Load-book entry 179, and it says in its own opening label that this entry is a line printed at the foot of a district instruction that says no local additions, and a woman of fifty-three who has read it every day for nineteen years and broke it once in the week of forty-five and told nobody until a man asked her a different question.** About two hundred district withdrawal pages in four stacks with a rubber band on each, four fields and a tick, and a clerk of fifty-three who has been there nineteen years and has never once been asked to make a decision. **He asks the second question — what do you think of that line — and nobody has ever asked her that, and the answer is that the line is there for the day somebody wants to be able to say that nothing was added, and nobody has wanted that once in nineteen years, and about four hundred people a week come past that counter, and the reason is that the line is at the foot and the foot of a page is not read by anybody who has read the top. She calls it a door that has not been locked since the year she started, and says she is the only person standing next to it and has never once been asked whether it is a door.** Then she says she broke it: **in the week of week forty-five a woman of thirty-one with a diary came down and stated her standing twice and asked for a box, and there was no box, and the clerk ruled one in pencil under the fourth field at the size of a stamp, wrote three words over the top of it in her own hand, and pushed the page four inches along the counter away from herself, and that is a local addition, and she has told nobody since, and had about eleven weeks to work out what to do about it and there is nothing to do about it.** She names the cost in one go, at greater length than she means to: **in about four years nobody will be able to tell that the line at the foot of that instruction and the box on that page are the same document, and she is the only person alive who knows and he is the only other one and he came in with a docket.** She says the name in the box is not hers to take out, the name at the bottom of the page belongs to the woman of thirty-one who is on the first floor and comes down on Thursdays and did not come, the page goes back in the stack, and **there is no way in this city for a thing that was never in a system to be put right and she is not going to invent one.** He says he has not written it down and is not going to and is not going to call that integrity. **She says: that is a man who has been standing next to a door since the spring and has decided that standing next to it is the same as having to open it, and I have been standing next to the same kind of door for nineteen years, and the difference between the two of us is not the door and not the character and is that nobody has ever asked either of us which one we are.** **A form obeyed every day for nineteen years by one woman and disobeyed for fourteen years by a city, and the difference is not the form.**

### Chapter 0177 — "What He Cannot Hear"

**The Friday of week fifty-six, the ninth of the eleven sessions, four weeks after the eighth. Load-book entry 180, and it says in its own opening label that this entry is the ninth of the eleven, four weeks after the eighth, and the seventh time in public that anybody has said the standing problem, and the first time it was said with the other half attached to it.** The third line is read and nine people do not hear the difference, for the third sitting running. **He stands at half past nine and puts the fourth condition into the first person for the second time in four weeks, and the six reasons, and says he is not going to say them a third time in a better sentence because there is not one, and the reason there is no rule about who may be asked is that a rule about who may be asked is a rule about who is findable.** Then the second condition, which is on no sheet: **he is the only person in this city who can say what he heard in a corridor and cannot hear a corridor, nothing is wrong with him that a doctor has found and it is not going to be fixed this term, in a fortnight he talked over three people in three rooms and failed to hear one, and the one he failed to hear is the reason a thing in this case was not noticed.** About four people in the room had no shape for it. **A district nurse of thirty-four stands up and gives her name and where she works into a room with a microphone on a stand, and says there are four people in this city with a file at the top of it that they put there themselves and she has met three of them and not the fourth, and the condition on his share is the only reason the four of them are not a group, and names her own cost — a file that was already hers, a man in a district jacket who heard her in the spring and put it in a corridor in Nine Bridges and did not do it on purpose — and asks for it to be noticed that she said the price and nobody wrote it down.** A man of thirty-four who works nights says from the sixth row that he has a version of it in nine words in biro and will not say it into a microphone. **The woman of twenty-six says from her seat that there are eleven letters in the folder, that she has written the answers to four of them over three evenings, that they are in the bottom drawer of her table and are not going out, that both of those are true at once, and that she is saying it in a room of thirty people because otherwise in about four years there are four answers in a drawer and no record of why.** No rule about who may be asked was invented. **There is now also no mechanism in this city for telling the difference between a man hearing a room and a man hearing a room and getting it wrong, and the man in question said so himself first.**

### Chapter 0178 — "Four Feet Of Shop"

**The Monday morning of week fifty-seven, from about half past nine to about midday, in four feet of shop in the Quiet Quarter. Load-book entry 181, and it says in its own opening label that this entry is a printer of fifty-eight and a quarterly return and about four hundred and thirty-two cards that cannot be taken back off anybody, and the cost of both, which is not going to arrive until about four years.** A sign over the door a woman of fifty-eight paid for out of her own money in her first year, a till, a folder of work, a boy of nineteen who has run the machine once. **A woman of about thirty brings a quarterly district return with a field for what a practice is currently in use and a field for whoever set it in type. The first is filled in with a refusal to put a number in it, because a number on a return is a number somebody can quote; the second is filled in with the shop's name, because a blank on a return is a question and she gets nine a quarter.** The cost does not arrive that morning. **She says it at eleven to a man of about sixty who came in with one of the four hundred and thirty-two and has still not given his name: in about four years somebody will walk into a building with a return in their hand and say the shop on that street printed about four hundred and thirty-two, and the shop has a name over the door that a person paid for out of their own money, and there is no version of that she can put in a drawer.** Three things she says to him: **the fifth line is the same five words as the company's and not the same sentence, she set it in type in the spring, and she has been told the difference is who the technique is waiting for and does not know whether that is true; the older cards are safer by the company's own argument, which is why people use them to come off a link a ward needs, and there is no way in this city to take them back off anybody; and she is the person who decides what that card is for, nobody has offered her a way of not being that, she has not asked for one and she is not going to ask him for one.** The boy of nineteen raises it himself: about four hundred pieces of paper carry his handwriting, he is on none of them with his name, and in about four years somebody going round shops will find this shop, and he cannot un-ring the bell. **She also says, unasked, that the hundred and forty-one pounds is in a coat on a hook in the back of that shop, that a form wants a name at the top of it, that the only name she has is a postcode and a wet coat, and that in about four years she will be sixty-two and somebody else will be behind that counter and will not know whose coat it is, and that this is the correct outcome and she worked it out herself.** Nobody thanked her. The man of about sixty put the card back in his pocket.

### Chapter 0179 — "Not One Of Them Wanted A Box"

**The Wednesday morning of week fifty-seven, from about half past ten to twenty past twelve, in the reading room in Lattice Ward. Load-book entry 182, and it says in its own opening label that this entry is a tray with nineteen slips in it about a month after it had eleven, and a woman of forty-one who has counted it twice and said the number out loud twice, and three people who came in that morning and not one of them wanted a box.** **A foreman of fifty-eight came into that room once, in the week of week fifty-three, with his own name at the bottom of a slip, and has not been back, and he is in the chapter as something that has happened to the room rather than as a person who was in it on the Wednesday.** **A man of thirty-four on nights fills nothing in, says he rang a woman of sixty-one to be told by somebody who has never heard of him, and asks what he does with nine words in biro he cannot hand over, and is told: then it is a piece of paper; and says he has stood in a corridor at ten past four that month with two sentences from two people who are both right and neither of whom will give way, that the rule is the same in both places, and that he would like it written down that he knew nothing would happen.** A woman of forty-three gives her name and where she works before she asks, and asks whether a room like this is a place a person goes when they are frightened, and is told no in one go: **three mornings a week and the hours are staying, the door is open on the other four days with nobody behind the counter, a frightened person at ten o'clock on a Tuesday would find a bell and a tray, and that is worse than not coming because they would have been somewhere.** She is told she is not going to be the reason a frightened person comes to a counter with a bell on it, because then in about four years she is a post, and there is no post in this city whose job it is to be here. **She gives the rest of it in six minutes: she does the stairs at three addresses, one of them a house she goes into four days a week whose address is on the district leaflet from the spring, there is a hall table in that house with a card on it and a fifth line that is the reason she can end something at work, she has used it twice, and what she has worked out since is that the leaflet did not take the table and the microphone took the table.** A boy of nineteen from the shop asks for the second time whether anybody has ever written down what a person said in a room like this and been believed, and is told two, in nine years, both wrong. **Then she says what she came to say: she has decided to keep answering questions in that room that are not about a box; she has four reasons written at home for not answering them and the fourth is that the argument went round this city in a corridor off a service road on the Tuesday of the week before last and she was not in it; a version of it was said to her in about nine words, which means her fourth reason is not hers, and if she refuses a question out of fear of what the room becomes then two people have already had that argument and have not let go of it either. They are not the only one, and she is not going to find out whether they have been told, and she will answer the next question that is not about a box on the Friday.**

### Chapter 0180 — "A Column Heading"

**The Thursday morning of week fifty-seven, and ten at night in a repair shop in Lattice Ward. Load-book entry 183, and it says in its own opening label that this entry is a bound register in four columns in a public building on a Thursday, with nobody behind the counter, and a woman of forty-one who came on her day off because it is the only table in this city where an authority is written down. THIS IS THE CLOSE.** A bound register in a canvas cover, four columns, about nine hundred lines, fourteen years back to the week of week thirty-eight, and **on a Thursday there is nobody behind it, because the woman of thirty-four who reads it out is there on Mondays, Wednesdays and Fridays, and about four hundred people in fourteen years have had a line read to them and not one of them has ever been there on a Thursday.** A woman of forty-one has come on her day off and said why before she was asked: **her room has about four hundred cards with a box number, a date, a place and a hand on them, and not one has an authority on it anywhere, and this is the only table in this city where an authority is written down.** The finding: **this is a register of removals, a book of what was taken out of a room; a binder went down a corridor to a room with a register in it in the week of week thirty-eight and six lines in this book are about it, and the four volumes in a box on a counter in Lattice Ward are a working copy that has never been in that room; the Compact was never taken out of a room, so it is not in this book, and no line anywhere in this city says who may change it.** He says there is a man on the floor above; **she says he is a person and a person can be asked, he has been asked six times in fourteen years and has agreed to nothing, if you asked him whether the requirement stands he would say it stands and say why, and that is not the same as anybody being able to take it away, and he is not the authority, he is the only person on that floor, that is not a post, there is no post on that floor and there is not going to be one, and nobody has ever asked him to be the authority over anything.** The six lines are read because there is nothing else to do with a Thursday: two have a hand, one of those two has an author still in this city, and the fourth column on all six and on all about nine hundred is a job title. **And the close sentence, which the load book records as a finding rather than a sentence, because the sentence is in the room and the finding is what the narrator has: the requirement is in print in the original words and cannot be withdrawn, the only thing that would have to be asked for it is a heading, and a heading is not a person.** She adds that she has read all four hundred of her own cards in about four years and has never once wanted to know who was allowed to take one out, and has wanted to for a fortnight, and is not going to put it in a tray because that would give the room a column. At ten at night: the shutter, the lamp, the docket, the five things under the bench, the jacket, the corner of card in the inside pocket, and the fifth line in the fourth pocket of the tool roll that is blank. **Nothing was settled by a form, a minute, a notice, a report, a committee or a leaflet, and nobody was asked to.**

### The measured figures for Movement III, reproducible from the ten files with the strict matchers printed in `workspace/volume-04/batch-0003/SUMMARY.md`

**28,201 words. Raw instances of *about*: 281, 9.96 per thousand. True hedges: 184, 6.52 per thousand. Bold spans, strict matcher: 178, 6.31 per thousand, worst chapter 6.86**, against ceilings of 10.75 and 12.4, and the count excludes the `---` rule markers. **The true hedge is the best figure any movement of this volume has finished on and the raw figure is the first in this volume under ten. Chapter 178 is the batch's outlier on the true hedge at 12.96 and the reason is structural: it is a chapter about four hundred and thirty-two cards and that canonical figure appears eighteen times across the chapter and its load book. Chapter 173 is the low at 3.46, and it is four people on a landing and a drawer and almost every figure in it is an age or a count the scene has already fixed.** Every line carries an even number of `**` and an even number of `"`; there is no `* * *` marker, no month-name, month-date, day-date, year or day number anywhere; there is no duplicated paragraph at an eighty-character threshold; and the word *pattern* appears nowhere, and so do the Crown Clause, Iona, Sorn, the Solo Seal, Nell, the Quiet House in the narrator's judgement, and *variant*. **The ten files carry a review repair pass and the gate set holds after it; the pass fixed six blockers and seven should-fixes without rewriting a scene, and its record is in `workspace/volume-04/batch-0003/SUMMARY.md`.**

### The two things a later writer must not undo in this block

1. **The Exchange's book is at forty-four lines, has not been opened for three sittings, and does not go to forty-five in this volume.** The reason is a rule and not a mood and it is canon: a book of what a caller said cannot be written by the people who did not call. **A Volume 04 writer may not open that book before the resolution and may not have a caller sign. The next Exchange is the Wednesday of week sixty.**
2. **THE SECOND OF THE TWO PUBLIC METHOD DISAGREEMENTS IS OWED TO MOVEMENT IV AND IS NOT SPENT.** Chapter 172 spent the first and only the first. **A Chapter 181 writer must find the second unwritten, must not restage Chapter 172's corridor, and must not mistake the standing problem for it.**
