# VOLUME 07 — MOVEMENT I (Chapters 301–310), *Four Hours Of Warning* — CHAPTER SUMMARIES

**`outline/volume-07.md` governs. Load-book entries 304 to 313, continuous with the 303 at Chapter 300. The day of Chapter 310 is day 666, the Thursday of week one hundred and eleven. `workspace/volume-07/batch-0001/SUMMARY.md` is the dated account and carries the measured figures, the nine deviations and the eleven things this movement reports and does not settle. No day number, month-name, month-date or year appears in any of the ten chapters; the digits in them are the chapter number in a heading, the entry number and the week number in the italic line that opens each load book, the entry number again in the closer, and the digit in a district's own name, which occurs twice in Chapter 307.** **A REVIEW REPAIR PASS HAS SINCE RUN OVER THOSE TEN CHAPTERS AND CORRECTED TWENTY-NINE FACTS IN THEM, AND EVERY ENTRY ABOVE THAT NAMED ONE HAS BEEN CORRECTED IN PLACE: a bench of nine and not fourteen, a line immediately above the newest and not eight lines up, an interval of just over three weeks and not a fortnight, two words in a van and not four, three words on a pad and not two, about an hour on a landing and not an hour and a half, a hardboard and not a plate, and the single point of failure named by him and not by her. The full log is section 8 of `workspace/volume-07/batch-0001/SUMMARY.md` and the continuity record is the block at the foot of `state/continuity.md`. No chapter was added, no beat moved, and no name was introduced.**

### Chapter 0301 — "Two Floors"

**The Monday of week one hundred and nine, day 649, entry 304.** A stair fire door in Nine Bridges at its ninth occasion in about a month, the same fault number on a company's list the company has never been inside the building with, adjusted for the fourth time, gone to the frame twice, no fault findable. **On a stair above the lobby, a lift in service about twenty-two years stopped between five and six, which has no door in that shaft, and came back on the press, and did it four times in about an hour, and the fourth time he stood in the car for about nine seconds waiting for it to and it did not.** He wrote it in the back of his own book and not on the docket, on the ground that a docket is a company's and a company is quotable, **and said out loud in a room off a service road at about eight in the evening that the only thing he is frightened of is a piece of paper and has been for about four years — which is the first time in six volumes he has taken a thing to a room instead of a service road, and it is the first act of this volume.** A cook of twenty-seven said the gas has smelled different since Saturday and that it is not the extract, and a person in the room said it was the extract, and nobody connected the two observations and about four people heard both. **The room did one thing about the lift and the one thing was that about four people stopped talking and about four went home early, and neither of those is a procedure.** The shutter came down at about ten, the room was warmer than the week before and nobody had turned anything down, about four people in this city came in this month for the temperature, and the man who put the heating on went round four units on a Saturday and nobody asked him and nobody thanked him. The tool roll was not taken out of its drawer and the fifth line on it is blank. **Changed:** a true thing is now in a room instead of on a service road, and cannot be got back out of it.

### Chapter 0302 — "Nine In One Morning"

**The Wednesday of week one hundred and nine, day 651, entry 305.** A mobile clinic in the Lower Wards, about nine chairs, a window that faces a yard. **Talia Venn counted nine people in one morning who each described a practice that has run the same way for years ending before they were ready: a woman of sixty-one and a bias binding, a man of thirty-one and a corner he will not discuss, a girl of nine who said it went faster than the song, three women at one table who do not know each other, a man of forty-four who asked whether it was new, a woman who said nothing and used her hand, and a man of about seventy who said stopped and meant the opposite and corrected himself in about four seconds.** She wrote all nine in their own words, in seat order, with the times, in about eleven minutes, and rang none of them, and told nobody. A woman of about forty-three who has been in that room for nine years said it was tiredness, and was not refuted, and said that nine is not a finding, which is true and is not a reason. **At about a quarter to twelve, alone, Talia put her thumb on her own wrist and the shape arrived about four seconds early, and she sat on the edge of the trolley for about a minute and did not write it down, and the reason she would give is that a number a practitioner writes about herself in her own building is a number that is in her building.** A second copy written out at about half past three did not survive being written twice — four had changed tense and one had lost a clause about wanting to know whether it was her eyes — and the clause was restored by hand. Both copies are in a locked clinical drawer. The board on the wall says eleven appointments, nine people, and that is about a quarter of the day and is the only record of it anybody in this city can see without asking. **Changed:** nine people are on paper that one person holds, and the tenth is not on paper at all, and she told nobody.

### Chapter 0303 — "The Standing Instruction"

**The Thursday of week one hundred and nine, day 652, entry 306.** A workshop with four bays and a yard and about eleven crews, and a lead repairer of forty-five with a metal braid of tools under her shirt. **She said the standing instruction was wrong for this week and then, four minutes later, said she was not changing it, and both halves were true.** The instruction: hold if a release does not come, telephone, hold until a person answers, hold until one arrives on site, the person who arrives owns the release, and you do not finish somebody else's practice for them. **Her reason, in about nine words: a person standing in a stairwell at eleven at night deciding whether he is allowed to finish is the whole cost, and she would rather be wrong about a person than right about a pump.** A woman of thirty-eight said the phones are off and are not phones; a person in the room said the shape of the problem is the problem and it is not the problem, and that the standing instruction has been fought over for four years and is not being stopped on a Thursday. **About four of the eleven crews sent nobody out; there was no vote and no list, and about four of the eleven are crews whose people have stayed with them more than about four years, which no document records. She let them go on one condition — bring back what you did not find — which was correct and was thanked for by nobody.** The man of twenty-two offered the lift twice and was refused twice in about four seconds each: a woman of thirty-four told him a piece of paper in a bag is worth nothing; **Sera read his four lines standing up and said a lift is not a practice, that she has no place to put it, and that the nothing would be on her, and then said that about four of her people had gone out against her and that if he wanted to be useful he could be useful to them and not to her, and he did not go.** The two crews came back at about half past four: **nine people on a bench in a stairwell in a four-tenant building in the Lower Wards, and no fault, and that is the finding, and the crew lead of about thirty-four said so in about nine seconds on a yard.** Sera said do not write it down, twice, and the second time she was improved on by the person it was about and was thanked for that and not thanked. **Changed:** about nine people are on a bench in a city where nothing is wrong, and the competent action of the day was to do nothing, and about four crews went out against the best instruction in this case and were right.

### Chapter 0304 — "Four Seconds Early"

**The Friday of week one hundred and nine, day 653, entry 307. The first Friday rota.** A company training room at the end of a passage in Crown Terrace, nine folding chairs, a laminated sheet on a post, about eleven people, four of them on no list, a man of fifty-eight with a bad hip holding a card in date and not sitting in the fourth row. **The last run of the evening ended about four seconds before anybody expected and nobody said the word, and about four people had hands in the position they had put them in.** The release word is not on the laminated sheet and has not been for about four years and has been asked for four times and refused four times. **He noticed it, in the fourth row against the wall, and did not write it down — on a service road, in his own book, or anywhere — and this is the second occasion in about four years on which he has had something in his hands and not written it, and he cannot tell whether that is discipline or that he does not want to be a man who has a book.** **A man of twenty-nine who works on lifts then did the same run with the count started at the other end, without a word at any point, and it finished, and the release came at the end of the shape and about four seconds later than the supervised one, and he said the true thing in about nine seconds: it is not the room and it is not the week, he has been doing it the other way for nine years, and the only thing he changed is that he stopped waiting to be told where the end was.** The instructor said that was never a technique, and that he, a card in a box and about eleven people a week, and then gave a new reason for not writing it down: **the moment a sheet exists with two columns on it, one saying supervised and one not, about four hundred people in this city will be handed that sheet by somebody who has never done a run in his life.** At the door, a man of thirty-four who buys training said eleven of eleven are on the list where there were eleven four years ago, one of the eleven was not there tonight, he will not be the man in the lanyard and he will not stop buying the training, **and there is nobody in this city whose job it is to ring a person and find out, and about four people have said that out loud in about four years and nobody has been given anything for saying it.** He put his card back in the box in about nine seconds and nobody stopped him. **Changed:** a run can be done backwards, and the four seconds happened again and are not a reopening, and one absence has no mechanism behind it.

### Chapter 0305 — "People In The Middle Of A Morning"

**The Monday of week one hundred and ten, day 656, entry 308.** A depot and four bays and a yard where somebody starts counting before anybody has said anything, which is on no form. **About nine people were in a bus shelter at about half past ten and they were not lost: they knew where they were. Their shifts had ended early, they had been standing where they had been since six, they were due back at four, and there were about eleven hours in front of them and nothing in them.** A courier of twenty-one, who is not a social worker and has not been trained as one, moved them: four vans, four directions, about four telephone calls to four houses where somebody was at home, eleven hot drinks. The only one who could not be moved was a woman of about eighty-four on the fourth floor of a block in Nine Bridges, whose lift had been stopping between floors since about half past eight and who had decided on a stair that she was finished with it. **He said he would not carry her, that he would walk her at her speed, that she could sit on every landing, and that it would take about forty minutes; he was told no; and he then told the woman of about thirty-one at the top of the stair, and gave as his reason for telling her and not the old woman, that he was not going to tell her anything she could use against him.** He sat on that landing for about an hour and came down with her at about twenty past twelve at her speed, with her own bag and a folded blanket he had taken off a chair without asking and returned before the ground floor. **He was telephoned at about eleven and the number is a letting agent's on a plate with no name on it and the letting agent knew a voice, and the call took about three and a half hours to become a man on a pavement, and that is the fourth surface on which he can be found.** On arrival he said, in about four seconds, that the courier is the only person in this city he can ring and has known that for about four years and it is not a compliment; **and then, in about nine seconds, that the day there is one of us is a day somebody is going to use it, and he is not asking him not to be it and is not going to be the one who thanks him for it, because that is how you end up being it.** On a wall he said out loud that he is not the man for this work and is also the man for it, and that if that makes him a post he will say so himself and will not sign anything. Nobody wrote anything down. **Changed:** a city found a man by accident, through a letting agent, because he said a true thing in a room nine days ago, and one person is now findable as the one who can be telephoned and has said out loud that he does not want that and will do it anyway.

### Chapter 0306 — "It Is Not A Fault"

**The Wednesday of week one hundred and ten, day 658, entry 309.** An extract plant on the roof of about eleven flats in a second district, running since ten past six, dead at half past six, refusing nine presses, and the building's own maintenance line returning a recorded notice that the service was operating normally to at least four separate callers. **The record says the work was done four months ago, one set of initials and no surname, and it is not a lie, and both people in that plant room read it first for about a minute in silence and one of them asked for that to be written down.** The part is a switch the size of a fist from a different manufacturer, which the man of fifty-one had in his bag since the spring, and it is the same wrong line of the same company's list that puts about nine hundred plates on about four hundred doors. Replaced at about eleven, running at about ten past twelve, **and it will go again in about nine hours or it will not, and that is a correct action performed by two people who are owed nothing and it reaches nothing.** In a van at about half past three he said the sentence this movement is built on, to nobody: **the building was in a fault for four months and the fault was doing nothing at all, and it began to matter on a Tuesday, and the thing that changed was not the building. Nobody wrote it down and it is carried into a later entry and is to be attributed to him when he is next on the page and he has not agreed to that.** The woman of thirty-four said about four hundred of her nine hundred doors are in buildings like this one and she has never thought it connected to anything because a number is not a thing that does anything; he said about four hundred lines; **she said that was not the same four hundred as nine hundred doors, and he said no, and she said then there is a third one somewhere and she has not met it, and he said that is the first time anybody has said anything to him about that number in about four years and that she is not going to be thanked for it.** Asked whether it is going to be four hundred lines everywhere: he has no idea, and a man of fifty-one with a tool bag was asked the same question on Thursday and said do not write it down, and she said, so the two of us know two different halves of it and there is nobody whose job it is to hold the halves. **Changed:** two of the three referents of about four hundred are separated on the page by two people who do not know a third exists, and the cook has still not been told what her own number is.

### Chapter 0307 — "It Is On The Counter"

**The Friday of week one hundred and ten, day 660, entry 310. The second Friday rota, which ran in Crown Terrace and is not in this chapter.** A plain envelope delivered by hand in a day where the post takes four. One large sheet, a district office's mark, and **a ruled line under the mark carrying no characters, which about four people noticed before reading a word of the body: a notice is not a rule, a rule has a number and a date and a reference, and this has none of the three, and the person who sent it did that on purpose.** About nine lines describing a condition in the plainest words any document in this case has been written in, naming no cause and no person, and asking for nothing except that the reader note it. **Asha Reed read it twice and asked who sent it and was given a name, an age and a job with no judgement attached to any of the three, and then said in about nine seconds, to the room: we can refuse this, and we are not going to, and the not refusing is the whole of the cost and it is mine, because nobody has ever told her that a piece of paper can be a cost — and the four words that carry it are *nobody chose us*.** The address is not a mystery and not a conspiracy: a converted shop unit is a letting, a letting has premises records held by a district, there are about four hundred and eleven of them, the room is one of them because it has a door and a lease and a landlord, and a forwarding address on a form is the likely route. A man of fifty-eight with a bad hip asked in about nine words whether it would be on the table on Monday, was answered with where it was, said that was not what he asked, and was not answered again. **A cook of twenty-seven said the word Friday before she said anything else about it, and would like it noted that she did.** It was still face up on the counter at about half past nine and about nine people had read it and about four had read it twice, and no copy is on anything and no record of it exists that anybody can require. **Changed:** a document is now in that room and cannot be got out of it, and a converted shop unit with no name on its plate is on a district list, and the first time in this manuscript that the room was located by a record rather than by a person who gave the address to nine people.

### Chapter 0308 — "Every Difficult Share"

**The Monday of week one hundred and eleven, day 663, entry 311.** Talia Venn's workroom, one floor above a corridor with a plate on the outside of the building, about four desks, a consent clipboard on the wall that no institution has asked for. **He asked how many there were and she told him all nine in about four minutes, flat, in order, and the order was the whole of the argument and she did not know that while she was telling it.** She said they were hers, they had said them to her, and that this was both a fact and a reason. **Then she said the real thing: that in about four days he would have arranged them, and found the thing they had in common, and the thing would be true, and the list would be better than hers and would not be theirs — and, in about eleven seconds, *you do it because a list is the only shape you have ever trusted* — and then the smaller reason, marked as smaller: that it is about four o'clock, that she has not eaten, that she has been in that building since half past seven, and that she does not want the nine shared, and part of that is a preference and part of it is that they are the only thing in this city that are only hers.** He was wrong in nine words: **you have decided and you have not told me.** She has not decided anything, and the reason he said it is that a person with a closed door at four o'clock has decided, and a sentence that works in a stairwell does not work at a desk. He did not argue and has not improved on them. He then told her what he had actually come with, which was not the nine accounts at all: **since Wednesday he has had four things he could not hand to anybody — a lift, nine people on a bench, a switch, a plant — and she is the person he hands things to, and it took him four miles and nine words.** He gave up, unasked for, that he will not ask her for the nine again and will not say in a room off a service road that a woman of twenty-four has nine of something. She said that was not what she had asked for either. He agreed, and said it was what he had instead of what she asked for, and that it was not the same as obeying her. **HE identified the single point of failure — one locked drawer in one building with a plate on the outside of it and no copy anywhere in this city — and she did not answer it, and he said he had not expected her to, and that he was not asking her to do anything about it and was asking what the difficult share was. He said she had said no because the nine were hers, and that he was asking about the part that was not hers.** **It was a Monday. They do this on a Thursday. He came on a Monday because a Thursday was a week away and he could not carry it for a week, and he had not written it down and had not said it on a service road, and he told her those two facts instead of telling her he was all right. She said: if you come on Thursday then this is a Thursday thing, and if it is a Thursday thing then it is a practice, and a practice is two people agreeing to do a thing and then doing it, and she has spent about four years not doing that with him because the last time neither of them could get out of it. He said then he will not come on Thursday. She said that was the first thing he had got right this week.** He then said the rest of it: that he does not want to be the reason she is alone with the nine, and is going to keep being the person she hands things to, and cannot make those two compatible, and she has known that for about four years and has never once asked him to fix it. She classified it in four words. It is restraint. He said: good. She wrote three words on a pad, did not read them back, and did not give the pad to anybody. **Changed:** he will not come on a Thursday, which is the first limit he has accepted from her in this case and the first one he has not tried to renegotiate, and the single point of failure is still a single point of failure and both of them know it.

### Chapter 0309 — "The Fifth Part"

**The Wednesday of week one hundred and eleven, day 665, entry 312.** Four ordinary buildings in two districts, four of the four hundred and eleven premises a district keeps. **Every plate between them carries a number belonging to a different line of the same company's list, and the correction, given in about nine seconds, is that they are not wrong numbers, they are right numbers for a different building.** A man of twenty-nine who works on lifts came out of the third building on his own lunch and stayed. That building has a barrier and a gate and a handover of about nine seconds, done by a man of about forty in the morning and a man of about thirty-five in the afternoon, both eleven years. **The older did it four times and it was fine. The younger stopped, twice, in the same place, about four seconds short of the end of the shape, with his hand where it was, since Tuesday, and had done it on Monday. Nobody in that building was frightened and nothing was broken.** The man of twenty-nine said, having said first that he had not got the words for it and only the shape, that the thing needs a person to tell them where it stops, that the telling has stopped, that they cannot find the end of it on their own, that they are not frightened, not ill and not stupid — and then, to the man of thirty-five and to nobody else, that he has been the man in the lanyard in about nine buildings a week for four years and has never once thought about what happens on the day he is not there. **Waiting is not a fault, and a person can be damaged by the absence of something they were never supposed to learn to do without.** At the third landing, in about four seconds and with no preamble, a woman of thirty-four said do not say anything, and then said one thing once: that she has stood on a step four times in about four years and could not move, has told nobody, and told him instead, and asked him not to write it down. He agreed in about one second and she observed the speed of it aloud. **It is on no surface in this case, he is the only witness, and it is in the load book because a refusal that is not written down anywhere does not exist. He did not tell Talia Venn and has not been asked why and there is a question he has not answered and is not going to ask her.** In a car park at about four, four hundred was said four times and came out as four numbers: the man of twenty-two's competent people who can invoke a printed rule, said out loud for the first time outside a room in Crown Terrace; the man of twenty-nine's buildings; the woman of thirty-four's doors; **and the man of fifty-one's lines, at which point he said *that is not mine*, and was right, and said that he, and the man of twenty-nine, and about nine people in a room have all been saying the same number for different things and have never been in a room together.** None of the three can be produced. She then refused, unprompted, to be a list. **Changed:** three referents are separated on the page and none is closed, and two men at a barrier cannot finish because the week took away a cue they were never supposed to be able to do without, and a woman of thirty-four has told one person a thing about herself that is on nothing.

### Chapter 0310 — "The Nineteenth"

**The Thursday of week one hundred and eleven, day 666, entry 313. The movement's close.** About eleven people in the room by half past six, which is more than on any previous Thursday. **A man of twenty-nine who works on lifts said the cost out loud, to the room and not to a person, in four items and about nine seconds: he has said the true thing twice in about four years and nobody improved on either and nobody thanked him; he has read the eighteenth line about four times; in about four years about four people will be able to find a person because of a piece of paper; and the person they will find is the only man in this city who has said in public that he will not take the middle, in a week when everybody is looking for one.** Asked by about four people at once why, he said he was in the fourth row for four years and nobody came and got him, and that he would like it on a wall and not in a head. He wrote it in about nine seconds with a pen off the counter and signed it. **I was fourth. I am not going to be the middle.** Eleven words, no space left where a name would go. **The wall is nineteen lines in thirteen hands and fourteen names; no line has been removed and none has been rubbed off; the first line is a cook's and is still the first line; and this is the first line in nineteen that is a promise rather than a record of something that had already happened.** Nobody thanked him and nobody said it was welcome. **A cook of twenty-seven then said, unasked, that a woman wrote the line immediately above the one about to be written and walked about nine miles to be in that room and signed it in about nine seconds, and that neither of them has been thanked, and that the interval between the two is just over three weeks, and that about four people in that room had noticed and not one had said so — and then said so.** Nobody said anything. **A first-year of nineteen read all nineteen lines between about half past six and a quarter to eight, said nothing for about half an hour, looked at the board and then at the man who had written the line, and said nothing at that either. The reason is not recorded in her mouth and is not to be asked for. **The chapter does not name her, and the load book of this chapter does not name her, and the editorial justification for naming her at all is not a sentence in this manuscript and is not printed in either.** At about a quarter to eight, from the corner, without a cup of tea and without a preamble, a man of fifty-eight with a bad hip said in about nine seconds that three people in this city can be asked and it is not one of them — a hardboard on a wall with a woman's name on it one line below the newest, a district office four miles away with a woman of sixty in it who has never once lied to him, and the man of twenty-two, who is the only one of the three who can be in a stairwell at eleven at night and decide whether a stranger is allowed to finish what they started. With four facts he is carrying: about four buildings in a district he has never worked in; a man of fifty-one who will not take his telephone calls; a woman of thirty-four who has told about four hundred people to go away; a cook who will not be on a telephone. **The question, whole: are you going to be the one who is asked. The answer, in about four seconds, seated, with a docket in the other hand: yes.** Nobody repeated it, nobody improved on it, nobody said he did not have to, nobody thanked him. **There is no form, no list, no minute, no notice of a notice, no permission, no appointment, no authority and no stamp, and nobody in that room can point at the thing that made him say yes, and in about three weeks a Continuity Office will write that name on a piece of paper in a building he has never been inside, and the piece of paper will not be wrong.** The asker said he had told a stranger where to find a man for the first time in about four years and would not be thanked for that either, and that it would be all right. He was not thanked. **Changed:** the wall carries its first promise, a refuser is findable, and a man has said yes to the only thing this case has spent seven volumes arguing he is not allowed to do, in a room, on a Thursday, with no authority behind it — and the only person in the room who noticed what the two lines together mean said nothing and is nineteen and has not been asked.**

---

# VOLUME 07 — MOVEMENT II (Chapters 311–320), *The Emergency Order* — CHAPTER SUMMARIES

**Load-book entries 314 to 323, continuous with the 313 at Chapter 310. The measured figures and the matchers that produced them are in `workspace/volume-07/batch-0002/SUMMARY.md`; every interval figure below was walked from the anchors in `workspace/volume-07/ARITHMETIC-AND-CALENDAR.md` and not from the chapter beside it, and every one of the one hundred in the ten dockets reproduces to the digit.**

**311 — Monday of week 112 — entry 314 — the room is three hundred and eight days old, a card in a rail is three hundred and twelve, the hardboard's twelve lines are two hundred and twenty-eight, its thirteen one hundred and seventy-nine, its fourteen one hundred and forty-four, its fifteen one hundred and twenty-three, its sixteen ninety-eight, its seventeen eighty, its eighteen twenty-six, its nineteen four.**
A Continuity Office instrument, two sides, five parts and no more: a mark; a reference in a form only that building can read aloud, which a woman of sixty copied in block capitals without understanding; a date on the letter; a count of about nine sites in four districts with a reason that is itself a count; and one name in a body at the foot of the second side as the point of coordination for a resonance event. Not on it: a question at the foot of the page, a date on which the appointment ends, any statement that he has been asked, any signature of his, any reference to who does the work at that premises. **A woman of about forty-one typed it in about eleven minutes because typing it is her job, and about four hundred of these have gone through her hands in about eleven years, and there has never once been a question at the foot of one asking a person whether they are the one, and the reason is not that nobody thought of it.** The name was found in three steps and nobody's job involved looking: the Office had no candidate because there is no list of anybody in this city; it asked a district to confirm premises because a district keeps a count of buildings, about four hundred and eleven of them, which is a count of buildings and not of anybody; and on the confirmation form a line said confirm premises and provide the tenant, with an instruction attached in about nine words. The premises is a converted shop unit off a service road with four names on the lease. The woman of sixty answered correctly — yes, it is in use — and nobody asked her to read the name and she read it. The managing agent gave the tenant's name in about nine seconds off his own file and was not thanked and is not a villain. **The typist named the cost out loud first and unasked: if a page can be refused then the count on the page is a count of people who said yes, and it is going to be about nine sites, and nobody is going to have said yes to anything; and in about four years somebody is going to ask her how she knew that man was the one.** The man of twenty-two read about nine lines and one line of a second side on a service road at about two in the afternoon, standing up, in about nine minutes, and did not see the sheet, and stood there about four seconds longer than the reading took. A first-year of nineteen put the difference in about nine words: one of those is a thing somebody sent us and this one is a thing somebody made about us. A woman of about thirty was in the chair nearest the door and was not asked anything.

**312 — Wednesday of week 112 — entry 315 — the room is three hundred and ten, the card three hundred and fourteen, the twelve lines two hundred and thirty, the thirteen one hundred and eighty-one, the fourteen one hundred and forty-six, the fifteen one hundred and twenty-five, the sixteen one hundred, the seventeen eighty-two, the eighteen twenty-eight, the nineteen six.**
The seventeenth sitting, four weeks to the day after the sixteenth, in a room above a line in a building in Saltmarket, shutters up, nine chairs in a curve, the ninth against the wall and not moved back and its mover not named, a tin that has never had a lock on it, and a book with four columns and forty-eight lines in it. A man of about forty-four, seventh attendance, said a thing to the holder of the book to her face and not to the room, in about four minutes, and it went in in about nine seconds in the third column, and she did not read it back, did not summarise it and did not thank him. **What it was about, in the room's hearing in about eleven words: he has been the person people go to for about four years, he has said yes to all of it, and last week he said no to one, and the one he said no to was to somebody who had nowhere else to go.** Book forty-eight to forty-nine. Telephone fifty-seven to sixty, eleven in four weeks. Announced count twenty-one to twenty-two, seventeen of which are the seventeen Wednesdays this book holds and five predate it on a sheet not shown to anybody in nineteen years. The four figures, not converted: forty-nine, sixty, seventeen, nineteen years. The direction given and the difference refused and she said she was not going to look into it in front of nine people. **About four minutes later the same man asked her for the no-conversion rule, having first said he had thought about it for a week and was asking so that he could tell somebody he had asked, and she gave him nine words and they were not it: *you already know it, you are asking for company.*** It is not the rule and not a refusal of the rule and not the rule a tenth time. Nobody improved on it. Nobody thanked either of them. **She kept a promise made out loud in front of nine people about four weeks earlier and about four of the nine noticed at the same moment and not one of them said so and she did not mention it.** At about half past eleven she volunteered, unasked, that she is sixty-one, has run that room for nineteen years, has never once been on a list and is not going to be on one, and that about four of them noticed and not one of them has said so and she has noticed that all of them noticed. A young man of twenty-one said one word and was told that was not the same thing. Nothing was said in that room about the printed rule or about the name that went onto a page on the Monday, and the two facts are placed side by side in the record and joined in no way. Two pieces of paper are on a counter in a room off a service road and they are not the same kind of object.

**313 — Thursday of week 112 — entry 316 — the room is three hundred and eleven, the card three hundred and fifteen, the twelve lines two hundred and thirty-one, the thirteen one hundred and eighty-two, the fourteen one hundred and forty-seven, the fifteen one hundred and twenty-six, the sixteen one hundred and one, the seventeen eighty-three, the eighteen twenty-nine, the nineteen seven.**
A copy of the instrument was on the middle bench of a workshop behind four bays from about nine the previous evening, put there by nobody anybody in that yard can identify, and no form in this case says who. A lead repairer of forty-five read both sides out in about four minutes including a reference she could not pronounce. **About nine crews went out and about four did not, and the four that did not are the four that did not go out on the previous Thursday, and they are the four whose people have been with them more than about four years, and no document in this city records that and none was created and there was no vote on either Thursday.** The division is not about the weather and it was not about the weather last week, and the same four did the same thing under a spoken instruction and under a printed one. **The sentence about a release and the person who owns it was said out loud once in this volume, in that bay, a week earlier, and is not said again by anybody in these ten days, and the record does not carry its wording because a record that carries it is a second saying**; a crew lead of about thirty-four carried the argument in about nine seconds instead — that a page puts nobody in the room at the end of a practice — and the standing instruction of the previous Thursday was that the person who owns the ending does the ending, and a page has not got a person on it. **The lead repairer named her own cost out loud, unasked: two of the four crews sitting it out have been carrying her own sentence since the previous Thursday as a permission and she has not given it and is not going to, and if one of them is hurt this week her name is on that too, and that is the first time in nine years that anything done in that bay has carried her name that she did not choose.** She was right that a piece of paper would come in about four days and wrong that it would be about ninety, and gave both out loud in that order. Two crews attended nine sites in four districts with job numbers and with the second half of every report and found the count on the page to be right and no person, no mechanism, no cause and no connection between the nine sites and anybody, **and could not say which of the nine sites the person named has been inside, and he has been inside none of them and does not know he is on the page.** A first-year of nineteen said in about nine seconds that four crews did not go out and about nine people are not going to be told they did not, and that about four people in that bay have just done a correct thing for the second time in a fortnight.

**314 — Friday of week 112 — entry 317 — the room is three hundred and twelve, the card three hundred and sixteen, the twelve lines two hundred and thirty-two, the thirteen one hundred and eighty-three, the fourteen one hundred and forty-eight, the fifteen one hundred and twenty-seven, the sixteen one hundred and two, the seventeen eighty-four, the eighteen thirty, the nineteen eight.**
The third Friday rota of this term, about eleven people in a company training room, seven on a card in a box and about four on no list. The last run is a hold with a count and an end that is supposed to arrive when somebody says so. **The instructor went to a counter at the back for a tally sheet, the bell-push is by that counter, a hand went near it and did not press it, and the absence was about four seconds, and it was an ordinary human absence and is not a fault and is not connected to the weather and no character has connected it to anything.** In that gap a woman of about forty-one, on no list, four years, no speech in that room in the whole of that time, said the word out loud once, and about four other people said it with her, and nobody looked at anybody. She said afterwards, to the chairs and to nobody, in about four words, that she had not known she was doing that. Nobody asked her why. **The instructor said nothing about it at any point in the rest of the evening, was not asked for a reason, and the reason she gave the last time she was asked for one is not repeated and has not been improved on; there is no sheet in that room with two columns on it and there is not going to be one.** A man of thirty-four who buys training said at the door that he is not going to be the man in a lanyard and is not going to stop buying the training and both are his to decide, and put a card in the box in about nine seconds and was not thanked. **The four seconds of an earlier term are settled and are not re-opened. This is a third Friday and is not that Friday, and a second Friday happened a fortnight ago, and the three are not joined to one another by anybody and the only procedure in the entry is that they have not been joined.** The man of twenty-two was in the fourth row and did not write any of it down anywhere, which is the third such occasion in about a fortnight, and he has not given the three a name and nobody has noticed him not writing.

**315 — Monday of week 113 — entry 318 — the room is three hundred and fifteen, the card three hundred and nineteen, the twelve lines two hundred and thirty-five, the thirteen one hundred and eighty-six, the fourteen one hundred and fifty-one, the fifteen one hundred and thirty, the sixteen one hundred and five, the seventeen eighty-seven, the eighteen thirty-three, the nineteen eleven.**
A copy of the instrument was taped to the inside of a street door of a four-tenant building in the Lower Wards with two strips of tape from a roll that is not that building's. **Nobody in that building put it there, nobody has admitted to it, nobody in this case has asked anybody, and no form in this city records who puts a copy of an instrument on a door and none was created.** About nine people read it before eleven. The courier of twenty-one read it standing up at about half past eight and said one sentence to nobody, that he said in a room off a service road a fortnight ago that he would not sign anything, that he has not been asked to sign anything yet, and that he would like that noted before anybody hands him something. **Nobody handed him anything.** A woman of about thirty-one with a clipboard and a stack and a van said four correct sentences at about nine doors: this is a copy of a document that has been issued; you may read it; nobody has to sign anything; and I am recording who has read it and not who has agreed. Four signed, about four did not, one told her to go away. **She has to write a number at the top of her own sheet and the number is nine, and in a building she has never entered nine is going to be read as nine people in this city who agree, and there is no column for the four who did not, and she was not going to write one, and if she is not sure of that in about four years somebody else will be.** She said nobody thanks a courier either and had said it in a car park a fortnight earlier without knowing anybody had heard it. **Three people in this case were asked whether the order was right and all three answered and the three answers are not the same answer, and the reader may check all three:** a man of twenty-nine who works on lifts said the count is right, the name is not his to do anything with, he will not treat a page as a man in a room, two sentences and no third; a man of fifty-one with a tool bag said **it is**, one word, and added unprompted at about half past two that the document does not mention about nine lines in about nine buildings and one cook who has not been told, and that the document's silence is correct and he is not going to tell her; a woman of thirty-four who checks about nine hundred doors said **it is right and I am not going to help with it, and those are two different sentences and about four people are going to hear them as one.** About nine people who would have answered a telephone in the last fortnight were told what to do on Monday by a piece of paper, and every one of them has an ordinary job. A first-year of nineteen said in about nine seconds that one woman had to write nine at the top of a sheet and had to, and that she is the first person in this city who has had to make one.

**316 — Wednesday of week 113 — entry 319 — the room is three hundred and seventeen, the card three hundred and twenty-one, the twelve lines two hundred and thirty-seven, the thirteen one hundred and eighty-eight, the fourteen one hundred and fifty-three, the fifteen one hundred and thirty-two, the sixteen one hundred and seven, the seventeen eighty-nine, the eighteen thirty-five, the nineteen thirteen.**
A care-link in a first-floor flat in the Lower Wards, in place about two years, about nine pounds a month, a telephone answered at night by a person not in the house who comes if it is rung and cannot be rung after about eleven on a Sunday, rung about four times in two years and somebody came every time. **It runs, and it cannot be ended, and the machinery is not a villain and is four correct steps, and every one of the people in it is doing their job.** Step one: a cancellation is made by telephone on a Tuesday, and the first question back is whether she is safe at the moment, and she is standing in the room, and the answer has to be yes, and a yes turns a cancellation into a request for a review taking about four weeks. Step two: the review is about the person and not about the arrangement, nine questions asked by a person of about forty-four in the middle of a working day because that is when the reviewer is at that address, while the paying daughter is at work and the mother is at home alone with the door open. Step three: the record says continue, and the record is true, and it has said continue twice in about two years, and nobody has lied in it. Step four: to change anything a reason must be put in a box, and there are about four reasons and none of them is that the person is well. **The daughter named the cost out loud, unasked, in about nine words: nobody has refused me anything; everybody has been correct; and there is no route from a Wednesday morning to a Tuesday with no charge and nobody coming, and the reason there is no route is that the one person safe enough to make that call is the one person the call is about.** She also said she is not going to be a person who stopped looking after her mother, that nobody has said that to her, that it is not on the form, that it is in the four reasons and in her own head at about four in the morning about twice a month, and that she said it out loud in a clinic and not in a corridor because she was asked a question in a clinic. Her mother said she was not lonely and said the word three times and corrected herself never. **The daughter then said in about four seconds that she had found out what her mother intends to do this evening before she had found out how she is getting home, and that she has had that wrong for about two years.** Talia Venn asked one question in about nine seconds — what happens if you stop paying it — and it was the only question she asked them, and then wrote the whole of it down in about eleven minutes in the words of the mother and the daughter in the order it was said, did not summarise it, did not put it on the board on that clinic wall, and **put it in a clinical drawer that locks and that now holds three sheets and that is the single point of failure a man named in a workroom four miles away the previous week and that she has not conceded and has not told him has a second thing in it. Nobody authorised her to.** She did not telephone him and he was on a job and then in a van and she knew both without asking. She said one clause out loud to nobody about two women in an earlier winter who were right and were not thanked, and that it is not the same thing and she is not going to use them to make a point about a form, and **they are not a case and were not gone and got and the thread is unjoined and is not to be joined inside a fortnight.**

**317 — Friday of week 113 — entry 320 — the room is three hundred and nineteen, the card three hundred and twenty-three, the twelve lines two hundred and thirty-nine, the thirteen one hundred and ninety, the fourteen one hundred and fifty-five, the fifteen one hundred and thirty-four, the sixteen one hundred and nine, the seventeen ninety-one, the eighteen thirty-seven, the nineteen fifteen.**
The fourth Friday rota. **A second laminated sheet the same size as the company's own was taped over the face of it on the post at the back, four corners, with tape from a roll that is not the company's, and the mark in its corner is not the company's, and it was there before the man of thirty-four who buys training arrived at half past four, and no form in this city records who taped a second sheet over a company's own post, and none was created, and nobody has been asked.** Nine lines: a condition in nine plain words, a count of about nine sites in four districts, a named person as point of coordination, and one instruction which is the whole of the document — that the end of a practice of this kind is to be confirmed by a call from the office named and is not to be given in the room by an instructor. **Absent from it and not going to be added: what happens if the call does not come, any date, any question at the foot, any signature, any reference.** A woman of thirty-six with a lanyard read it out in full, standing up, in about two minutes, in the flat voice of a person reporting on a machine. **About four people then had to decide whether a piece of paper from a building they have never been inside outranks a word they have been given in person for about four years, and all four decided differently, and the reader is entitled to see all four:** a man of about twenty-seven on the list, that a word is a person and a paper is a record and only one of them can be asked about in about four years, so he will do what the sheet says; the instructor, that she has asked four times in four years for one word on that post and been refused four times, and that a sheet with somebody else's mark on it is not going to tell her when a practice ends, and she is not stopping, and **nobody asked her for a reason and she was not asked for the one she gave the last time and that one is not repeated here**; a man of thirty-four who buys training, that he said a week earlier he was not going to be the man in a lanyard and was not going to stop buying the training, that in about nine seconds he found out he was half right, and that he is complying because he pays for the room, and he is not going to pretend that is a different reason; a woman of about forty-one on no list, that she is not going to decide anything this week, that there is no way to do a thing on purpose that she did by accident, and that if nobody rings she will sit there. Then the last run went at about half past seven. **The telephone rang once at about a quarter past six about a man's daughter and did not ring at the end of the run.** About nine seconds passed, the woman of forty-one looked at the instructor, the instructor looked at the second sheet for about four seconds, about nine people waited, and the woman of forty-one did exactly what she had said and sat there, **and the instructor said the word at the point she says it every Friday, at the speed and in the voice she uses every Friday, and about four people did not expect that and about four did.** At the door the man of thirty-four said nobody rang, that this is not going to be in a record anywhere, that there is no column on any sheet in that building for a call that did not come, and that he is the only person in that room who knows it and is not going to be the person who says so twice. Nobody thanked anybody.

**318 — Monday of week 114 — entry 321 — the room is three hundred and twenty-two, the card three hundred and twenty-six, the twelve lines two hundred and forty-two, the thirteen one hundred and ninety-three, the fourteen one hundred and fifty-eight, the fifteen one hundred and thirty-seven, the sixteen one hundred and twelve, the seventeen ninety-four, the eighteen forty, the nineteen eighteen.**
**A man of fifty-eight with a bad hip was asked to go somewhere and did not, in one line, and the reason for the asking was better than the not going.** A call at about ten past nine in the morning from a person of about thirty-four with an ordinary job in a district office in the second of the four districts, reading off a piece of paper, which is correct. About four buildings in that district with a release not coming; nobody local knowing what to do; no local person who can be in a stairwell at eleven at night and decide whether a stranger may finish what they started; and the reason for the call — a statement made out loud in a room in another part of the city, in front of about nine people, about four buildings in a district he has never worked in, reported by a person of sixty who has never once lied to him, and **the fact that there is no list of anybody in this city and he was the only name available.** One line, in the corner, at about half past six: that it is his own sentence from the Thursday, that he gave it to a room and not to a district, and that he is not going to be one. He did not go. Nobody said anything, nobody asked him why, nobody improved on it, nobody thanked him. **He has said two things in about four years; the second was the question on the Thursday of week one hundred and eleven that a man of twenty-two said yes to; the third of the five things he has been asked about is one he refused in an earlier term and nobody has put it to him since and nobody is going to.** Later the same evening he made a third statement, about four years of being findable — not findable at all, then findable by about four people who knew him, then findable by anybody with a piece of paper — and said that every one of those has been a good thing for somebody and that the last one has cost a man of twenty-nine his afternoon, a woman of twenty-four her morning and him a telephone call at ten past nine, and that he is not saying which of them he would give back. Nobody improved on it and nobody thanked him and **it is not one of the two things and nobody is to count it.** And on the same evening a person of about twenty-six came forty minutes on a bus and asked that room what it thinks, which is the first time in about four years anybody has come back to it and asked a second time about anything. Three people answered and it was not one answer: what the room can do, being nine people who turn up and four folding chairs and a kettle; what the room cannot do, being that it holds no list and has refused every offer of one; and, from a first-year of nineteen in about nine seconds, the reason the room answered at all — **an unanswered question gets written on a form, and a form is a worse thing to hold than an answer.** Her words went into the visitor's own words, not summarised, read back before she left, about nine lines. **No form in this case exists for that answer; it is on a clipboard in a building this case has never entered; and about four people in that room are not in it, were not asked, and are not to be added to it.** The room now holds three things and the third is not a piece of paper and has no mark on it and is owed by nobody.

**319 — Wednesday of week 114 — entry 322 — the room is three hundred and twenty-four, the card three hundred and twenty-eight, the twelve lines two hundred and forty-four, the thirteen one hundred and ninety-five, the fourteen one hundred and sixty, the fifteen one hundred and thirty-nine, the sixteen one hundred and fourteen, the seventeen ninety-six, the eighteen forty-two, the nineteen twenty.**
A woman of about thirty-six with a shoulder bag and about nine documents went up the stairs of a building in the Lower Wards and gave one of them to a man of twenty-two on a second landing. **She does not know him and he does not know her and there is no list.** The sheet carries the same mark and the same unreadable reference, the same condition, the same count, the same name, and at the foot of it, below the name, a printed line ending in a space with a line beneath it reading *name*, and a second line reading *date*. **Not on it: any date by which it is to be signed, any consequence of not signing it, any statement that he has been asked, any statement that anybody will ask again.** She is authorised to give it, to record the name and whether it was signed, and to go to the next one; if it is not signed she writes that it was given and not signed and does not ask why and is not going to ask why, and the not-asking is not care. About four of the nine people she has given one to have signed, one with a date on it, and a place for a date on every sheet of which about four are filled and about five are not, **because the date is the date of the signing and not a date anybody is given.** He read it twice against a wall in about four minutes and said there is a place for my name and a place for a date and nothing on this that tells me what the date is for, and she said no, and said she is not authorised to know what it is for. **He did not sign it, and he did not say that he would not, and the two are not the same thing and about four people in this case have been confusing them for about four years.** He gave two reasons in about nine seconds: that he has nothing to sign it with, and that the second of those would be a thing said to a woman on a landing, of whom there are about nine and of whose destinations he knows about four. She said it was the most she had been told by anybody that morning and wrote none of it, being authorised to write two things, and she wrote the second of them. **The sheet went inside his coat, folded once, and it is on no surface in this case, and that is the second thing he has put in his head instead of on a surface; the first was a lift.** She named the cost on the stairs: a line in about four years that says a man did not sign a thing, no column anywhere in her work for the reason, about four people will read it as a man who was asked and said nothing, **and he did not say nothing.** A man of fifty-one with a tool bag said on his way past that he read the sheet over his shoulder, that it has a place for a date on it, and that in about nine years of work he cannot think of one job where the date was the important part. **Four miles away, on the same Wednesday, a woman of twenty-four had a document of her own with a place at the bottom of it from a district office, about nine lines, correct, the second such document that office has sent that clinic, and there is a rule there about acknowledging the first and not the second, and she put it in the drawer that now holds three sheets and is the single point of failure he named and she has not conceded and has not told him.** She did not telephone him and he did not telephone her and neither wrote, and there is no record anywhere in this case of their having spoken since the Monday of week one hundred and eleven. Neither has been wrong since. No scene, no declaration, no kiss, no milestone paid.

**320 — Thursday of week 114 — entry 323 — the room is three hundred and twenty-five, the card three hundred and twenty-nine, the twelve lines two hundred and forty-five, the thirteen one hundred and ninety-six, the fourteen one hundred and sixty-one, the fifteen one hundred and forty, the sixteen one hundred and fifteen, the seventeen ninety-seven, the eighteen forty-three, the nineteen twenty-one.**
He went into a building in Crown Terrace that he had never been inside, on a telephone call from a woman of about thirty-one whose job is to arrange appointments, who read the request off a piece of paper and moved the time about four days forward when he said it was wrong. **Nobody made him go. There is no form requiring him to go, no list on which he appears and no minute of the asking. He went because of a yes, said on a Thursday evening in a room with about nine people in it, in about four seconds, to a man with a bad hip.** A man of fifty-eight in a coat that has never been in a workshop did not stand up, said he had about nine minutes, and had a document in front of him that was not the instrument. He was shown a page in a file — not a form, not a minute, not a register, not a list, not a report, not a committee paper, not a leaflet, not a registration, not a constitution — carrying about nine words: that the person named in the instrument is appointed as the point of coordination, and that the appointment is for the duration of the condition, and nothing else. **No date of appointment, no date of review, no date at all, and the condition it runs for has no date either, being nine plain words with no beginning and no end, and no page in that building says when anybody will decide that it has finished.** He read it standing up in about four minutes, did not touch it, did not ask to, was not offered it, and said in about four seconds: there is no date on my appointment. The answer was: I know. Then, unasked, in about nine seconds, **a standard adopted as temporary with no date on it runs until somebody with a stamp puts a date on it** — the one sentence in this case nobody has ever improved on, said before in a room in Crown Terrace, not asked for again, and said now because four words had just been said that it is about. Nobody improved on it and nobody thanked him and nobody said it had already been said. Then the rest of it, and the difference, which is the finding of the entry: the sentence has been about a rule for about eleven years and everybody in this case knew it and nobody improved on it, and it is about a person now, and **a rule may be invoked by about four hundred competent people in this city and a person may be asked about himself by about four of them.** Then, asked for the rest of that: he has the stamp, is not going to use it, and is not going to give a reason, and the reason is that the day somebody with a stamp dates a thing the date is the date it was noticed and not the date it ended, and a date on a piece of paper is a date a person can be asked about. **The cost, named out loud by the man of twenty-two in about eleven seconds: he cannot make anybody do anything, has not been asked to and would not know how, and what he has instead is the sentence, pointed at him, true, the only true thing anybody in this case has said to him in a fortnight, which he is not going to improve on and is not going to be able to put anywhere.** Nobody said anything and nobody thanked him and he did not ask, and he was asked no question in about nine minutes and has never been asked one in that building, and eight questions were not asked and none of the eight is to be asked by anybody in this case. He went down the stairs and out at about ten past four and **did not write it on a service road and did not write it in his own book and did not telephone the woman of twenty-four four miles away, and he has not got a reason and has not tried, and that is the third thing he has put in his head instead of on a surface.** The hardboard is nineteen lines in thirteen hands and fourteen names, no line is to be added this week, the first line is a cook's and is still the first, the newest is eleven words and is the only promise on it, **and he did not go and put it up, and about four people in that room noticed that he did not and not one of them has said so.** Nobody thanked anybody. A cook of twenty-seven said nothing at all for about an hour, which she has done before and has not done for about a fortnight. A first-year of nineteen looked at the door when he came in and said nothing. A woman of about thirty was in the chair nearest the door and was not asked anything and was not thanked and was not apologised to. **At the close of this movement the Exchange's four figures, printed and not converted: forty-nine lines, sixty on the telephone, the count announced twenty-two times of which seventeen are the seventeen Wednesdays this book holds and five predate it, and nineteen years. And the appointment has a reference, a count, a reason and no date.**

---

# VOLUME 07 — MOVEMENT II (Chapters 311–320) — CHAPTER SUMMARIES AFTER THE REVIEW REPAIR PASS

**A review repair pass read the ten files and corrected ten findings. Three chapters were touched and seven were not. No chapter was restarted, no scene was rewritten, no planned beat moved, and no summary below contradicts its chapter. The full account is at section 11 of `workspace/volume-07/batch-0002/SUMMARY.md`.**

**THE THREE CHAPTERS THAT CHANGED, AND THE CHANGE IN EACH.**

- **318 — Monday of week 114, entry 321. The man of fifty-eight's one line had two times and now has one.** The body, the docket and the record of the morning call all place his one line at about half past six; two load-book paragraphs placed it at about ten past six. **Both are now about half past six, and his second statement stands at about four minutes to seven, about twenty-six minutes later.** Nothing else in the chapter moved: the call at about ten past nine in the morning, the four items read off a piece of paper, the about four buildings, the no, the corner, the not going, the no question asked, the no thanks, and the third statement that is not one of the two things he has said in about four years and is not to be counted. The visitor of about twenty-six who came forty minutes on a bus, the three answers that were not one answer, the first-year of nineteen's about nine seconds, the clipboard read back before she left, and the four people in that room who are not in it and are not to be added to it, are all untouched.

- **319 — Wednesday of week 114, entry 322. A load-book field label used a word this volume reserves, and the label is now `Brought by:`.** The record of the woman of about thirty-six who took one sheet up the stairs of a building in the Lower Wards opened on a reserved word in front of her description. **It now opens on `Brought by:`, which is what the record itself says happened two lines later, and the record is otherwise word for word what it was: ordinary job, ordinary coat, shoulder bag, about nine documents, four buildings in one district and two in another, about four minutes a building, correct, never thanked, given on a second landing at about eleven by a woman who does not know him and whom he does not know, and there is no list.** The chapter's own outcome is untouched: he read it twice against a wall in about four minutes, he did not sign it, he did not say he would not, the two are not the same thing, he gave two reasons in about nine seconds, she wrote the second of the two things she is authorised to write, the sheet went inside his coat folded once and is on no surface in this case, the man of fifty-one with a tool bag said the date was never the important part, and the woman of twenty-four's second document is in a drawer that locks.

- **320 — Thursday of week 114, entry 323. A list of eight was called nine, in the chapter and in three state files.** The record of the questions he was not asked in nine minutes in a building in Crown Terrace now reads **Eight were not asked** and **None of the eight**, and the eight items are the same eight. **The summary above in this file is corrected to match, and so are `state/character-state.md` and `state/continuity.md`.** Nothing else moved: the about nine minutes is a duration and is unchanged; the man of fifty-eight's I know; the sentence about a standard adopted as temporary with no date on it, said once and not improved on; the reason a date is a date a person can be asked about; the cost named in about eleven seconds; nobody saying anything; the third thing in his head instead of on a surface; the hardboard he did not go and put it on; the cook of twenty-seven silent for about an hour; the first-year of nineteen looking at the door; and the four figures at the close, forty-nine, sixty, twenty-two, nineteen years, with an appointment carrying a reference, a count, a reason and no date.

**THE SEVEN CHAPTERS THAT DID NOT CHANGE, AND WHY THAT MATTERS.** Chapters 311, 312, 313, 314, 315, 316 and 317 were read and returned no finding. **Four of the ten findings were about figures printed about those chapters rather than about the chapters themselves, and one was about a guardrail claim that covered all ten; only three chapters had a fault in them at all.** The measured figures for the movement are now walked on the finished files and stand at 33,896 words, 215 bold spans at 6.343 per thousand, worst chapter 7.45, eight-word overlap mean 97.7 from the per-chapter list 88, 38, 115, 81, 125, 85, 96, 101, 101 and 147, and an apparatus share of 48.18 per cent. **The superseded per-chapter lists are recorded at section 2 of the batch summary rather than deleted, and the superseded total of 33,895 is recorded beside them: the one word of difference is the replacement of a one-word field label by a two-word one in Chapter 319.**
