# VOLUME 07 — MOVEMENT III (Chapters 321–330), *The Broadcast* — CHAPTER SUMMARIES

**Dated after the Movement II block above and supersedes nothing. `outline/volume-07.md` governs. Load-book entries 324 to 333, one per chapter, continuous with the 323 at Chapter 320. The full dated account with the matchers beside every figure is `workspace/volume-07/batch-0003/SUMMARY.md`, and the measured figures for the ten files are 35,160 words, 254 bold spans at 7.224 per thousand, a worst chapter of 8.880 at Chapter 330, a true hedge of 802 at 22.81 per thousand, an eight-word overlap mean of 104.9 and an apparatus share of 41.79 per cent, and the superseded first-pass set is 304 spans at 8.524, a worst chapter of 9.96, 846 true at 23.72, an overlap mean of 143.0, quoted beside the correction at section 11 of the batch summary.**

- **321 — Monday of week 115, entry 324, *Four Rooms And One Box*.** Four district councils in four buildings in four districts, in four rooms of about nine people with the same furniture, find out they are competing for one relay unit. **Nobody had told them how the scarce thing is meant to be shared and that is nobody's fault and it is the whole problem.** Four one-sided letters went out on one morning from a building not named in the entry; each offers access on application in writing, asks for a count of premises, and says nothing about what a count is for. In the first district a woman of about thirty-eight does the correct thing in four minutes off her own record of about four hundred and eleven premises and it changes nothing — a man of about forty-one says out loud that that is what the letter is for, that you give them the number and they have the number and there is nothing on it that says what happens after the number. **The change comes from a piece of paper she had seen on another counter about nine days earlier, which is dated four days earlier: there is one of them, there are four of us, and the earliest of the four dates is the date of this one.** He answers that access is real and there is not any access to give. The other three rooms gave three other reasons: the second rests on its own highest count, the third's woman of about sixty asks who the fourth council is and is not answered, the fourth's woman of about twenty-nine observes they are not four councils competing but four rooms with four pieces of paper and no information. Marek is in the corridor outside the third room on a stairwell job, hears three words, writes none of them down, tells nobody, and is not the subject of the day. **The cook of twenty-seven, about seven o'clock: four rooms all got a letter about one thing and none told the other three, and about four people are going to be told this week that somebody else wants it too, and every one of them is going to believe it because they have all got the letter in front of them.**

- **322 — Wednesday of week 115, entry 325, *The Second Piece Of Paper*.** Four people with ordinary jobs each do the correct thing with a copy of a Continuity Office instrument in their own building, and all four are right, and three of the four get a second piece of paper back asking a question they are not authorised to answer. **The estates officer who logs it in her register in four minutes is sent a form saying *please confirm that the person named is aware of this appointment*; there is no second question, nothing to sign, and no name of a reader, and she is authorised to write what she is authorised to write and that is not it.** The duty supervisor who puts a copy in the folder for the next shift and reads it out twice is queried for a number per person, writes *not held* in the margin in four seconds, and gets the same question back a third time with nothing added. The woman of about twenty-eight who passes a document to the desk it concerns with a four-line covering note gets back, on the foot of her own note, *which of the two people in this document is to be asked the question at the foot of it* — and there are not two people in the document, and there is no procedure for telling a sender a question cannot go back, and it is in a tray on a desk in a building this case has never entered. **The fourth, a man of about fifty-four on a gate in District 3, pins a copy to a board and gets nothing back, because nobody knows he did it, and no form in that district records that a copy went on a board and nobody ever will.** The cost, named aloud by the first woman in about eleven seconds: she does not know, she is not going to ring him, and if she does then she has found him and about four people in this city can find a person who has been found once, so she will write down that she does not know, **and the true answer is the one that will be read by somebody as a refusal, and it is not a refusal, and there is no way on that paper to say that it is not.** What Marek is told is that a question of that shape is now on paper in four buildings, and he says nothing, has not repeated it, and has not written it down, and what he has is that there are now about four ways in this city for a piece of paper to arrive at his name and he was named on one of them about four weeks before anybody thought of the other three and cannot get any of the four back.

- **323 — Thursday of week 115, entry 326, *Quoted Back*.** A woman of about sixty whose job is to ask questions, in District 3 practice standards with about four hundred and eleven premises in her own file, puts a question to a Continuity Office man of about forty-one that was not one of his four prepared ones: if the condition is in my building and the point of coordination is a person, then I am not allowed to ask a caretaker to open a door because the condition is the reason he closed it, and I am not allowed to ring the person at the foot of your second side because he is on no list in this city and you are the only body in the room with his name on a piece of paper. They go and look at the door for about eleven minutes; he does not touch it, asks one question about a line of paint, writes nothing down. **Then she quotes the instrument back: the count is a count of sites, a site is a building, the number of buildings in her district with a release in some state this week is not nine and has never been nine, so the count is not a count of the number of decisions somebody will have to make — and if it is read that way this city is short by about nine hundred, which is not her figure and is a figure a woman in another part of this city has, and the two have never met. He concedes it, twice, the second time in the past tense about his own document.** She then uses the document for what a document is for: not for the person, for a route to him about one door, and he does it from his chair on his own telephone in about nine seconds. **The stairwell fire door on the ground floor of a four-storey building in District 3 goes back into service at about half past two; about nine people live in that building; the record that comes back is one line and is not a form, a minute, a notice, a report, a committee paper, a leaflet, a registration, a constitution or a hardboard.** Nobody thanked anybody. Her cost, out loud, in nine seconds, to nobody: she has had that file for about nine years and has never once used a document she was sent in order to get something, she did it this morning because it worked, and she would like somebody to write down that she did it because it worked and not because she has found anything out about anybody. **Nobody wrote it down.** In the room off the service road, the man of fifty-one with a tool bag: that is the first time in about four years I have heard of a piece of paper being used on a person rather than on a door, and I do not know yet whether that is better.

- **324 — Friday of week 115, entry 327, *Nine Voices*.** The fifth Friday rota of this volume, and a fifth shape, and not a third and not a fourth. **The post at the back of that room is not described in this entry, and the omission is recorded as a decision, because a writer who decides what is on one company's post is deciding a fact about one company.** The last run goes at about half past seven; the count completes; the woman of thirty-six with a lanyard, who has run that room for about four years on her own voice, does not say the word, and looks at the post at the back for about four seconds. **About a second later somebody at the front says it in his own voice; then somebody on the left, late, unsteady; then about four more over about four seconds, each in a different voice, none in anybody else's, one of them flat and fast and slightly too loud and not stopping. About nine voices in a room of about eleven.** She says one word: *Right.* The man of thirty-four who buys the training, in four seconds at the counter: **that is not my room's word, that is nine people.** And at the door, in nine seconds: four years of one voice and he was happy about it, nine seconds ago it was nine, he is not going to say what he thinks about that in a room, and let it be noted that he has not said it. A woman of about forty-one on no list says nothing and has not spoken in that room since. A man of about twenty-seven on the company's list **was the third voice and knows it and said nothing and is not to be asked to explain it.** A man of twenty-nine who works on lifts is in that room on no list and says nothing at all, again, and about four people in this case have guessed at why and none of them is right.

- **325 — Monday of week 116, entry 328, *The Broadcast*.** The namesake event of this movement, staged here and on no other of the ten days. **A voice, live, heard by about four million people, in four rooms in four districts and in a great many other places. Not a recording. Not delivered. No envelope, no courier, no clipboard, no letterhead, no addressee, and no character in this case received it privately or is named in it.** One claim, in about eleven seconds, and not repeated anywhere in this volume and not repeated by any character to any person in any room or in any load book: **that only one central anchor can end what is happening in this city.** She does not say who, or where, or how anybody would be allowed to reach it, and the word naming the public body is not in it. **`Iona Sorn` is named twice, once in the body prose and once in this chapter's own record of the broadcast, for that purpose and for no other: she is not met, is not a post, an office or a chair, and no name of hers is attached to a sequence, to the weather, to a door or to a crew, and no character has established any such link and this entry supplies none. She is right about the arithmetic, the check is available to a reader in Chapter 330, and it is deliberately not performed here.** The only thing said about a person hearing it is that a woman of about forty-four heard it at about seven in the morning at home and went to work at eight, and that is the whole of what she did about it. **The cost: three of the four councils took applications in writing and proved a need by reading out the names of people who had come to a counter in their own district in a fortnight and asked for a release to be looked at; about nine people in this city said a thing at a counter, gave their names because that is what a counter asks for, and went home, and their names were then evidence in four rooms. Nobody rang any of them and nobody has told any of them.** The counter clerk names the cost in about eleven seconds: nine people said a thing to me and went home and their names are now on a sheet in this building because I asked them for their names, and I have done that about four hundred times a week for about six years without once thinking about where the paper goes, and I have thought about it since ten o'clock and am not going to be able to stop. **The four rooms each give a reason for preferring the box to the anchor, and in two of the four the reason is that the box can be shown in nine seconds and the anchor cannot be shown at all.** And at about ten past eleven a man of about thirty-three in a corridor: four days on a box, and not one of the four of us can say who decides, and I asked Wednesday for a number per person and got the same question back three times and there is no column for it anywhere, and that is not a box, it is a question about who is allowed to ask what. **Four people in four buildings then stopped wanting the box and started asking the question, and the argument stopped being about a piece of hardware and has not been about one since.**

- **326 — Wednesday of week 116, entry 329, *The Eighteenth Sitting*.** The Exchange, **the second of this volume's five sittings and the first of the two at which the book does not open.** The book stays at forty-nine lines. **The reason is a person and not a room and no word of priority is used about it by anybody: a caller telephoned on the Tuesday, got about nine words out, could not finish, stayed on the line about four minutes, said they would ring again, and has not rung, and she did not write it, and there is no rule requiring her to and she has never needed one in nineteen years.** She then said, unasked: I have not written down a thing somebody could not finish in nineteen years and I have not had somebody stop in the middle of one in nineteen years, and I am not going to tell anybody in this room that those are the same thing. **The no-conversion rule was not asked for and nothing was given in its place — not said, not refused, not paraphrased, not alluded to — and a man of about forty-four who asked for it four weeks ago sat down at about a quarter to ten and did not ask, and about four people noticed that he had not asked and he was not asked about that either.** The count comes at about ten past ten unasked: forty-nine to sixty-three, three in four weeks to the day, the book up nothing, the telephone up three, the telephone was ahead before that and the direction and not the difference, and the count has been announced twenty-three times of which eighteen are the eighteen Wednesdays this book holds and five predate it on a sheet not shown to anybody in nineteen years. Nothing was converted by anybody. The last line went in four weeks ago and is twenty-eight days old and nobody gave an interval. The ninth chair was against the wall and not moved back, mover not named. **Two standing claims about a room being the reason a book was shut are on the page sixteen weeks apart in the same hand, and this movement supplied a third reason and claimed nothing; the owner of the repair of the other two is a later pass.**

- **327 — Friday of week 116, entry 330, *Four People In One Building*.** The sixth Friday rota, and a sixth thing, not joined to the other five. **The post at the back is not described in this entry either, for the second week running, and that is a decision.** The learning-to-wait injury, which was put on the page at a barrier in a gate and spread through a document in a training room, arrives in one building as about four named people and four behaviours and is not named by anybody: **a woman of thirty-six who cannot start, and who began three of the last four runs by looking at a man of about twenty-seven and waiting for him to move, and who said *are we going* before anybody had moved; a woman of about forty-one on no list who cannot leave, and who sits down after every run and stays about four minutes, and who on the fourth Friday did not go at nine until a man said her name; a man of about twenty-seven on the company's list who cannot answer without something in front of him to read from, and who told a visitor from another company that he would need to check a question there was nothing to check, and then asked the instructor to read him the count; and a man of twenty-nine who works on lifts who cannot begin a thing without knowing who will finish it, and who stood by a door for about nine minutes and who asked in a corridor whether anybody would be in the building on Saturday, was told about four would, said *right*, and said nothing else.** Nobody writes it down. **The man of thirty-four who buys the training has been asked four times in a week whether he is going to put it in a report and said no four times, and on the fourth said: I have run this room for about four years and I have never once written down anything about a person in it, and that is not because I am careful, it is because there has never been anything to write down, and there is now, and I am going to keep saying no, and I would like somebody to notice that I have not given a reason and that I am not going to. He gave no reason and none is supplied.** About four people in that room knew, on that night, that four people in that building had been altered in the last three weeks by the removal of a cue they had been taught to need, and nobody said it, and about four other people are going to be in that room on Friday and are not going to be told.

- **328 — Monday of week 117, entry 331, *There Is A Word And None Of Them Have It*.** The first word of the public body is available and is not used. **It is printed under a mark on the second side of one of the four letters about a box, and has been for about eleven days, and three of the four councils had that letter in their hands and had not read to the foot of the second side. On this Monday four people in four rooms find out that there is a word for the thing the four of them are in, and that it is one word, and that none of them has it — and not one of the four says it, none of the four learns it, the narrator does not print it, and the woman who holds a chair of forty-nine in a building in Crown Terrace is not told, is not asked, and does not say it.** The woman of thirty-eight read to the foot of the second side and said in four seconds: there is a name on this, it is not the body's name that sent it, it is the name of the thing that body is part of, and I have read that line about four times and could not tell you the word. The man of about forty-five read it on the Monday alone and said that it is a word for the whole city, that he has four hundred and something buildings in his own file, and that he has never once been asked what he am part of. The woman of sixty was asked for it by a visitor who had come forty minutes on a bus and said she has known there was a word since Friday, cannot supply it, and can supply that it is on that sheet and four of them have had it. The woman of about twenty-nine had worked it out on the Friday from about nine buildings and said there is a body that is not them and is not the Office and is not a company, it sits above all four, and none of them has ever been in a room with it. **The secondment is unchanged and is not explained, and the printed rule is named and restricted and does not contain the word and has never required it.** And at about half past three a man of about thirty-three telephones the other three rooms himself, on his own telephone, and asks not about the box but whether they will put the same question in the same words; three say yes and the fourth says neither yes nor no. **The question is four words long, is a question about who decides, and is not printed in this entry, because three of the four people who wrote it would like to see it asked cold.**

- **329 — Wednesday of week 117, entry 332, *Nobody Asked You Whether You Wanted It*.** The four-word question reaches a person of about thirty-one answering a telephone in a building in Crown Terrace for a body that is not the Office and is not a company. **She does not pretend to have an answer: she cannot say who decides, nobody has ever told her office, and she will not guess at nine in the morning on somebody's behalf. Then she gives four people from four buildings the same day and the same address, in about nine seconds each, out of a procedure that does not exist — a district, a building, a floor, and the day he is on the docket — and says it is more than she has ever given anybody for that document and that she had not had to think about it and has thought about it since about ten past nine. She did not put it in writing and there is no form for it and she did not create one.** No two of the four know about the other three. **Marek is on a landing in that building at about ten with a tool roll and listens to all four properly in about nine minutes, asks none of them one question, and says yes four times** — three requests and, for the fourth, the answer to whether he is the one named at the foot of the second side, which is the only time anybody in this case has asked him that in a building he was working in. Nobody thanked him. **And nobody in that building noticed that in about nine minutes four people in four buildings were told that a route exists, and not one of the four asked whether it was the only route, because not one of them knew it was a route and not a favour.** Talia Venn is in the ground floor of that building for the day because the district car park was full, and hears three of the four in full, and at about eleven says one sentence from the bottom of the stair, in about four seconds, to him and not to the man of about thirty-eight, with about nine people going past: **nobody asked you whether you wanted it and you said yes four times.** He did not answer it. She said nothing else and has not raised it since. **There was no scene, no declaration and no kiss, and nothing was resolved, and the resolution is held to a later volume.**

- **330 — Thursday of week 117, entry 333, *The Junction Holds*.** **THE MIDPOINT. A services junction at the end of a road four districts share, beside a hospital, over services belonging to four districts, about nine crews, opened by a diversion on the Monday and a supply shut on the Wednesday afternoon. He takes a telephone in a stairwell in the second of the four districts at about seven in the morning and is on it until about six in the evening — about eleven hours — with two gaps of about nine minutes in which he finishes two jobs on his docket. About four hundred decisions, none technical, none about the practice of anything. He taught, demonstrated, assessed and assisted nothing and was asked to do none of it. THE JUNCTION HELD. NOTHING CAUGHT FIRE, NOTHING WAS OFF FOR MORE THAN ABOUT AN HOUR AND A HALF AT A TIME, AND NOBODY DIED. He was right about nearly all of them, and the reason he was right is that he is the person other people tell things to, and that is about four years old and is not a technique and has never been called indispensable in this case.** He is the subject of this chapter, and the volume's own line that he is in all fifty entries and the subject of none was true of Movements I and II and is not true now. **The cost: at about one o'clock in the afternoon he works out that the questions have started getting easier because the askers have stopped trying. At about two a man of about thirty-four stops about eleven seconds short of asking him for the tenth time and asks. At about four a woman of about thirty-eight does not ask the tenth time at all and gets a gate wrong by about an hour and a half — the tenth was the one that mattered and it was not the one anybody would have named in advance. In the last two hours he tells two people to decide; one says what do you think and writes his answer down; one says you have it in front of you. About nine people in four buildings were not asked.** A woman of twenty-four is in that hospital's outpatients from nine until one doing work that gets paid for, sees nine calls in and nine out, describes it to nobody, and leaves at one without staying to see whether it held. **At about five, in a corridor, a man of twenty-nine who works on lifts — in the building since ten, van stuck, the only person in it that day on whom nothing had been done — says the true thing in nine words: ask a person twice and they stop asking you. Nobody improved on it. Four seconds. Not thanked.** **About four minutes later a woman of about thirty-four goes back to her own building, does not telephone him, rings a third district herself, and asks the question the fourth person had sat on; that answer had been held since Wednesday afternoon; the gate is held from about ten past five instead of about four. One decision, by a person, in a building, without him. It is the only one there was that day. He found out on the Friday and has said nothing and is not going to.** That is the first time in about four years anybody in this case has used a sentence somebody said out loud instead of improving on it. **Two counts are printed so that a later pass does not add them: this finding has now been said twice, and a separate count of three is carried in this case for true things said in about four years by that man, and they are different counts.** **A first-year of nineteen says one thing at the counter that evening, in about four seconds, about a drawer that locks, and it is not about any of this, and it is the only thing she has said in eleven days; she does not name the finding here in nine words and does not name it at all.** **Talia Venn is in that room from about half past six until ten and says nothing to him and he says nothing to her, and about four people notice and none of them says so, and her one thing was said on the Wednesday and is not repeated here.** The word naming the public body was not said, not printed and not in anybody's mouth. **The appointment still carries a reference, a count, a reason and no date, and the sentence about a standard adopted as temporary with no date on it was not said and was not needed.** Nobody thanked anybody. **The four figures at the close of this movement, printed and not converted: forty-nine lines, sixty-three on the telephone, the count announced twenty-three times, nineteen years — and the junction held, and nobody died.**

**THE THREE CHAPTERS OF THIS MOVEMENT THAT A LATER PASS SHOULD LOOK AT FIRST ARE 324, 327 AND 321, NAMED HERE BEFORE THE PASS AND NOT AFTER IT, WHICH IS THE THIRD MOVEMENT IN A ROW IN WHICH A SUMMARY HAS DONE THAT AND THE FIRST TWO OF THE THREE IN WHICH THE NAMED CHAPTERS CAME BACK CLEAN.** 324 is the lowest on the eight-word overlap in the batch at eighty-seven, 327 is the shortest chapter at two thousand nine hundred and five words, and 321 is the lowest on the apparatus share at thirty-eight and a third and a half.

---

# VOLUME 07 — MOVEMENT IV (Chapters 331–340), *The Blackout* — CHAPTER SUMMARIES

**Dated after the Movement III block above and supersedes nothing. `outline/volume-07.md` governs. Load-book entries 334 to 343, one per chapter, continuous with the 333 at Chapter 330. The full dated account with the matchers beside every figure is `workspace/volume-07/batch-0004/SUMMARY.md`, and **the measured figures for the ten files, after the review repair pass recorded at the foot of this file, are 32,140 words, 166 bold spans at 5.165 per thousand, a worst chapter of 6.484 at Chapter 333, a true hedge of 885 at 27.536 per thousand, an eight-word overlap mean of 68.4 and an apparatus share of 44.54 per cent. The superseded set is 32,228 words, 166 spans at 5.151, a worst chapter of 6.442 at Chapter 333, 886 true at 27.492, an overlap mean of 68.8 and an apparatus share of 44.68 per cent, and the set before that is 33,968 words, 231 spans at 6.801, a worst chapter of 8.000, 948 true at 27.909, an overlap mean of 189.6 and an apparatus share of 47.52 per cent, all three quoted beside each other at sections 2, 11 and 13 of the batch summary.**

- **331 — Monday of week 118, entry 334, *Four Hours, And A Reason*.** A Continuity Office man of fifty-eight with a bad hip writes, at about eleven in the morning, at a desk in a building in Crown Terrace, one printed instruction with a reference, a count of four hours, a reason, and his own name at the bottom of it, and sends four copies to four districts and keeps a fifth. **A count of hours has not existed in this city before, in this case, and about nine people can now see exactly where it lands.** The reason is that about nine sites in four districts have a condition in them this week and cannot all be carried at once, and four of the nine have to come off first. **There is no column on it for what a person is to do with an hour, no column for who decides, and no date on it, and the condition it is issued under carries no beginning and no end.** It is not a form, a minute, notice, report, committee paper, leaflet, registration or constitution, and **it is not a hardboard on two nails, and the difference between the last of those and the first is the whole of what the rest of the volume is about.** Four people in four buildings each do the whole of what the paper gives them, correctly, inside about four hours: a woman of about thirty-eight with a file of about four hundred and eleven premises, who put a copy in her own record and told about four people before about two; a man of about thirty-three with a desk and a gate and a shift, who read it out twice on a shift, pinned a copy to a board by the gate and telephoned about nine people by hand in about forty minutes through nobody; a woman of about sixty whose job is to ask questions, who read it in about four minutes, asked two correct questions and got answers to both and **neither answer was a reason**; and a woman of about twenty-nine, who put a copy in a drawer, said nothing until about half past four, and then said about four seconds of it to about four people who had not asked her. **Not one of the four can say out loud why their building and not the one next to it, and all four found that out on the same morning. A building cannot decline a piece of paper with a count on it and can do the whole of what the paper says, and is left holding about four hours it has not been given any way of carrying.** Nobody thanked the man who signed it and he has said he will not be thanked.

- **332 — Wednesday of week 118, entry 335, *The Hour Is Not A Person*.** Two days after the sheet went out the four hours are in four buildings and four people are holding them, and none of the four is holding a document; they are holding an amount of time, and **a count of hours is the first thing this case has produced that a person cannot put down, and nobody in this city has a practice for putting one down.** Four costs are named out loud in four buildings. **A man of about fifty-nine with four units**: that four hours on a Friday evening is not four hours, because about two hundred and forty people arrive in about four hours and about two hundred and forty leave in about four hours and the whole of his trade is the two of those; and then, unasked, that he has no way of saying it four times, is not going to stand in a doorway and say it to two hundred and forty people, and that the man who sent the sheet is right and he has not got a better idea and is not going to pretend otherwise. **A man of about thirty-three with a gate**: that it is not the two hundred and ninety, it is that he can hold a gate in the dark and cannot hold two hundred and ninety doors, and the sheet has one number on it and both of those are in the number; and then, unasked, **the other half, which is that the load is real, that the four hours are not a punishment, that the man who wrote it is not a fool, and that if the building does not come off then the doors come off and he knows which he would rather happened and so does the man who signed it.** **A woman of about sixty in the third of the four districts**: that two hundred doors and about nine people are not the same thing, that the two hundred go home at five, that nothing in that building can be done at night that was not done before seven, and that she has failed since Monday to find a way to say the four hours cost that building something and is not going to say a thing to be interesting; and the one reply in the whole day, from a woman of about twenty-eight, **that it is not that nothing happens, it is that about nine people do four hours of what they do on Fridays in a room with no light in it, and that is a thing that happens to them and not to a building.** **A woman of about twenty-nine**: that the four buildings on the sheet are the four with the most doors on a list, and that there are four buildings in this city where four hours in the dark would cost a person something, and none of them is on the sheet, and she has looked for a form and has not got one. **The reason all four are right is the load, and the reason all four are wrong is the load, because a load is measured in the thing it is made of and this one is made of hours; an hour is not a person; a count of hours is a correct answer to a question about a load and is no answer to any question about anybody. Every one of the four is carrying the hour correctly, and nobody in any of the four buildings is doing it badly.** He read the sheet on a counter on the Wednesday morning in about nine seconds, took it, asked nobody anything, and has not been told what any of the four said.

- **333 — Thursday of week 118, entry 336, *A Count Of Doors*.** **The movement's own engine, and its sentence kept whole: he is right about the load and wrong about four buildings.** The four buildings on the sheet carry about nine hundred doors between them — two hundred and forty, two hundred and ninety, two hundred, a hundred and seventy — and a woman of about twenty-nine establishes in about nine minutes, to about four people who had not asked her, off a list she has kept for about four years, that **those four are four of the four buildings on one company's maintenance list, that the count did not go wrong, that a count of sites is a count of doors, that a door is on a list because somebody maintains it and is maintained because there are many, and that no part of any of it is anybody's fault and none of it is a person.** A man of forty-one, `Oren Vey`, operations director of the company that holds the maintenance contract and prints the plates, comes to the fourth district at about eleven with a schedule of about four pages and about nine hundred lines, **brings no product and offers none and is not asked for one**, and says three correct things: that the contract is for maintaining a plate and covers no night, no person and nothing behind a door, and that he could not change what is on a plate in four buildings that morning if he were given the week; that he did not choose the four buildings, has never chosen them, and that they are the four with the most doors because they are the four where he has the most to lose, **asked to be recorded as something he said and not something he did**; and, in four seconds, to a person with an ordinary job and not to the room, that if you want to know why a city is darkest in the buildings where it is darkest for a person you will not find out from a list of doors, and he has looked, and he has four pages. **He left at about half past eleven and is not in this case again and nothing of his was used by anybody afterwards, and no name of his is attached to the four buildings or to any decision about them.** **Then, in a doorway in the second of the four districts at about half past twelve, unasked, a man of fifty-one with a tool bag names the four buildings where four hours in the dark would cost a person something — nine flats, about nine people; eleven doors, about eleven people; four doors and a room over a shop; nine doors and a woman who is not out before six in the evening — and then says the finding: those four have about thirty-three doors between them, the four on the sheet have about nine hundred, and a count cannot tell the difference between a door that is kept in repair and a person in the room behind it, and it is not going to learn.** He was right about all four, three checked by about four people over the following fortnight and the fourth checked by a man of twenty-nine who works on lifts who was standing in it on the Friday night. **Nobody thanked him, he has not asked, and that evening he put his bag down at the door of a room off a service road for the first time in this volume, stood at the back, said nothing, and was not asked about the doorway.** A woman of about thirty-eight adds the other half at about half past two: a premise is a word for a building, she has four hundred and eleven of them, nobody has ever asked her what a building is for, nobody who put one in her file has either, and a city counted this way for long enough is a city made entirely of things that were counted. **He has been told none of it. He is behind and does not know that he is behind, and about four people in four buildings know it and none of them is going to tell him.**

- **334 — Friday of week 118, entry 337, *The Seventh Friday*.** **The seventh Friday rota of this volume, and a seventh thing, and the six before it are not joined to one another by anybody and are not to be joined without a reason and a record.** The same company training room in Crown Terrace, about nine folding chairs, about eleven people, seven on the company's list and about four on no list. **The post at the back is not described, for the third Friday running, and that omission is this entry's only claim about that company.** The instructor, thirty-six, with a lanyard, who has run the room for about four years on her own voice, cannot begin the last run; she looks at a man of about twenty-seven, who does not know she looks at him, and about nine seconds pass in a room of about eleven. **Then the man of about twenty-seven turns a sheet of the company's own over, finds the line with the count on it, and reads the number out. Nothing else. The run goes.** Nobody copied the number; there is no sheet, form, report, register, note or column anywhere in that company for it; and **about nine people in that room have not said why a number will do what a voice will not, and one of them has been coming for about four years and is not going to say it either.** Four minutes later the same man says, unasked, in about nine seconds, that he has been in the third district's building about nine times, that it has about nine flats and about two hundred doors, and that the number on the door of the third-floor flat at the back belongs to a flat on the other side of the city and has been there about two years and he has read it nine times and said nothing nine times because it is not his plate and not his job and he is paid on Fridays; and then, in four seconds, that he is not saying it to get the four hours stopped, that he can say which four the count got and cannot say which four it should have got, and that he has been carrying the second one about a fortnight and has nowhere to put it down. **Nobody improved on it, nobody thanked him, the instructor said one word.** A man of twenty-nine who works on lifts, on no list, silent on all seven Fridays of this volume, stands by the door for about nine minutes, then asks in about nine seconds whether anybody will be in the city on Friday of next week past seven in the evening, is told that about nine people will be, in four buildings, in the dark, for four hours, and in writing, and says *right*, and nothing else; **that is the ninth such utterance of his in that room and the only thing he has said there in this volume, and nobody in that building has asked him what he intends to do on the night.** A woman of about forty-one on no list leaves at about nine in about four seconds without sitting down again, for the first time in about a month; one person noticed; she is not asked, in the room, in a corridor or at a door, and the reason is not in her mouth and is not to be asked for. **And the man of thirty-four who buys the training, who was asked four times in one week about a fortnight ago and said no four times and gave no reason, is not asked a fifth time. About four people noticed. Nobody stopped the asking. He said in about four seconds, at the counter, to nobody, that he had noticed as well, and gave no reason then either.**

- **335 — Monday of week 119, entry 338, *One Stairwell Stays Lit*.** **THE COST, WHICH LANDS ON PEOPLE WHO WERE NOT ASKED, IN A DISTRICT WHERE THE PRACTICES THAT KEEP PEOPLE WELL NEED LIGHT TO BE DONE HONESTLY, and the chapter in which the man of fifty-eight does the thing he is right about and is not yet at the point where he says the sentence.** A four-storey building in the third of the four districts has about nine flats, about two hundred doors, about nine people in the flats, and a table in its ground floor where, twice a week, a woman of twenty-four does work that gets paid for: about nine people, about eleven appointments in a morning, a chair on the other side, a piece of paper. **The work cannot be done in the dark, and not because it needs a machine: because a person is asked to say yes to something about themselves, and being asked and being watched are one act, and one without the other is not a smaller appointment but a different and cheaper one, and about four people in that building know it and nobody has written it down.** So the four hours land on about nine flats four days early. **The cost is named out loud, unasked, in about nine seconds, by a woman of about thirty-one who answers a telephone in a building in Crown Terrace for a body that is not the office and is not a company, who takes about four hundred calls a week and has no procedure for any of this: that in nineteen years she has never had a call at seven in the evening about a person, that on Friday she will take about nine and about nine of them will be about a person, and that she is going to be the person who decides which of them gets a lit stairwell, and nobody has told her how and nobody in this city has.** Then, unasked, in four seconds, the other half asked for: the sheet is right, its writer is right, four hours off a building with two hundred doors in it is load and not punishment, and four hours of asking has produced about nine names' worth of cost and no count at all, and the count is the part she has. **And the man of fifty-eight does the one thing he is right about: he writes a second instruction, in print, with a reference, a count of four hours, a reason — that one of the four buildings has about nine sleepers in it and one stair between them and the ground floor — and a person at the foot of it, and puts emergency light in that one stairwell for those four hours and nowhere else, sent to one district. It took about nine minutes and cost that office a crew, about nine hours of somebody's evening and a night's sleep, and he paid all three and has mentioned none of them. He changed no count, moved no building, moved no hour and put no date on anything.** The cost of the light lands on a person too: a woman of about twenty-eight works out in about nine minutes, unasked, that a lit stairwell is not a light but a person standing in a stairwell, and that the four hours of the table work still do not happen because a person holding a light cannot also ask anybody anything. At about half past two the woman of about thirty-one asks him on the telephone whether the count of four hours is a count of hours or a count of people, **and he says in about four seconds that he will look at it, and gives no date, and does not say he has an answer, and says that the reference on the sheet is the correct reference to quote, and that is the end of it.** Nobody told the man of twenty-two any of it; he finished all seven jobs; about four people knew something they were not going to tell him and he did not know there was a thing.

- **336 — Wednesday of week 119, entry 339, *One Sentence*.** **THE PIVOT.** A room in a building in the third of the four districts, about nine chairs, the same furniture as every other room in this case, no name on the door, nothing on the wall, not a meeting and no minute of it. Nine people, every one of them holding a piece of the four hours, including a man of twenty-two who is on no list and was not asked anything. A Continuity Office man of fifty-eight with a bad hip comes at about eleven, asked correctly, on his own authority, and did not have to. **They came expecting three things and got none of them: a date, a reason in words they could carry to about nine people in a building, and to be asked what their building could not lose. They got none of the three and the third was not asked. He was asked for a date three times in about eleven minutes and gave none on any of the three and gave no reason on any of the three, and the one sentence in this case that nobody has ever improved on was not said and is not printed here. Then he says, in about four seconds: an hour on that sheet is not a person, and he has written it as if it were, and that is the whole of what he has to say.** He sat down and did not explain it, did not take it back, did not add to it, did not say it again, and is not going to. **About nine seconds. Then the room does the thing rooms do: a man of about thirty-three starts about four words about a gate and stops, because a second thing said after that sentence would be a second thing; a man of about fifty-nine says one word, *no*, which is not a disagreement and which he has not explained and has not been asked to; a woman of about twenty-nine says nothing in the room and afterwards, in a corridor, in about nine seconds, that she disagrees with about two thirds of it and thinks the other third is the truest thing that office has said in about four years, and that both of those are hers and are not going to be improved on by anybody; and a woman of about sixty asks no question at all and is not to be asked why. Nobody thanked him. He has said he will not be thanked. About four people understood what it had cost him to say one thing to a room that did not want it, and not one of them said so, and he did not know that they knew.** **He has said two things in about four years and refused one, and this is not one of the two, and nobody counted it and nobody is to count it, and the count of two is not increased by this entry and is not to be increased by a later pass.** Marek Senn was in that room for the whole of it and said nothing, asked nothing, repeated nothing, improved nothing, wrote nothing down, told nobody in a room off a service road, and did not act on it. **He has about four days in front of him in which to act on it and has used none of them, and the reason is not that he does not think it is true, and nobody has asked him what the reason is, and about four people in four buildings know what it is and none of them is going to tell him.**

- **337 — Friday of week 119, entry 340, *Four Hours With The Lights Off*.** **THE NIGHT, AND THE VOLUME'S ONE PANEL, IN THE SAME CHAPTER. The plan's cast, exact: torches, voice, a door, a bell, a kettle, about nine people who have not all met, a man of fifty-one with a tool bag, a cook of twenty-seven, and a woman of thirty-four who checks about nine hundred doors. HE IS NOT IN THAT NIGHT, HE IS NOT ITS SUBJECT, AND HE DOES NOT APPEAR IN IT, AND THE CHAPTER IS BETTER FOR THAT.** The light goes at about five to seven from the top down, floor by floor, about nine seconds apart, on an old honest timer, and there is nothing anybody can do and nobody tries. One stairwell in that building has light in it for the four hours because a man of fifty-eight put it there in writing with a count on it, and about nine people knew which stair without being told. **THE PANEL IS HERE AND ONLY HERE, two plain lines under one marker, naming nobody, asking nothing, answering nothing, and agreeing with the sentence a man said out loud in Chapter 336: the count is a count of hours, and no hour on it has been cost in a person. The prose never refers to it.** **Nobody decided anything that night.** The nine: the man of fifty-one, who says three correct things in about nine seconds each, none of them an instruction, including that the buildings that go wrong in the dark are the ones where somebody decided to work it out, and puts a coin under the ground-floor door so that it stays open all night; **the woman of thirty-four, who comes in on her own account with her own list and reads about nine door numbers in the dark in about an hour and a half, saying nothing, and finds that seven are right, that one is wrong and has been for about two years and its holder does not know, and that one is right which she believed wrong, and tells one person in four seconds who does not act**; the man of twenty-nine who works on lifts, there because a lift stopped between two floors with no fault and there was nothing to be done, on the second-floor landing from about seven to about eleven with a torch he has not switched on, four periods of about nine minutes at the rail, **going twice when somebody came up the stair and not going twice when nobody came**, and four things, of which the landing gate twice, once by him and once by the woman of about forty-one, with nothing said by either then or since; the man of about twenty-seven who cannot read a number in the dark, says so in four seconds, can do about nine of about two hundred from memory, has about two hundred and no sheet, and goes up and down on his own timings; the woman of about forty-one, who came down at seven, sat on the bottom step with her back to the wall, and did not go out of a door that was open the whole four hours; the man of about thirty-eight from a gate, who came at ten to seven to pass a message to a man on that gate at night, stayed, and at about nine, unasked and without telling anybody, sat on the second-floor landing about a metre from the man at the rail for about nine minutes and said nothing at all; the cook of twenty-seven, whose kettle it is, who knew about a fever on the fourth floor at about seven, told nobody, was told four times by four people that everybody up there was all right, and put the kettle on at about ten and took it up four storeys in the dark in about two minutes; the woman of about twenty-eight, who wrote on the back of a hand twice and has not written it out; and the woman of about twenty-nine from the fourth district, who came on her own account and decided nothing. **A bell goes at about half past eight for about two minutes — the alarm on the car, on a four-year-old battery — and nobody in that building knows to this day whether it was the car, the wind in the stairwell or a plate moving on a landing; about nine people go down four storeys, in about ninety seconds, and come back up, in about eleven minutes all told; nobody counted them, because counting nine people in the dark with four torches is not a thing anybody does, and about four of the nine thought there were about eleven, and there were nine; nobody has said the alarm was nothing, because nobody knows.** The light came back at about eleven and about ten past, bottom up, about nine seconds a floor, and it was four hours and about ten minutes against a count of four. **Four torches, and none of them was given to anybody; nobody passed anything to anybody, nobody handed anything to anybody, nobody held a light for anybody, and nobody went up or came down with anybody; there was no list of who would do what, there is no list, and nobody coordinated anything, and if you asked any of the nine who was in charge of that stairwell they would tell you there was not anybody and they would be right.** **Nobody thanked anybody.** Four people went back up. A door stayed open all night that had been shut for about a fortnight, and a coin is still under it, and a kettle was washed, and two numbers on two doors are known to one person.

- **338 — Monday of week 120, entry 341, *Nobody Rung*.** A mobile clinic is in the ground floor of a building in the third of the four districts for the Monday and the Tuesday because the usual room is not free; about nine appointments across two mornings; about nine of the people who came had been in that building in the dark on the Friday night. **The list was made on the Thursday for a Friday with four hours of dark in it, the four hours moved about half a morning, about nine people on the list did not come because nobody told them the list had changed, and about four who came were not on it — and the two lists have never been put next to each other in any room, nobody has asked for that, and this entry does not do it.** At about eleven, at a table, in a room, a woman of twenty-four is asked a question about the list on the paper, looks at a clipboard for about four seconds, and says it once, in four words. **Nobody improved on it. It went onto the back of a hand that was washed. It is in her mouth and in no narrator's sentence, it is not in a document of this case, it is not on a surface, this chapter's own record does not carry its wording and does not refer to it by its subject, and the other nine records do not raise it — and that last half of the sentence was false until the review repair pass, because the body of the chapter used to carry a paraphrase of the sentence's subject in the narrator's voice, which is a worse place for it than a load book because a reader of prose believes it.** The rest of the Monday was not a thing anybody noticed. **A man of about thirty-three had a question he had rung about on each of the three previous Mondays and did not ring, and sat with a woman of about thirty-four in a different building for about nine minutes; it took about twenty minutes longer and the answer was the same. A woman of thirty-four did two buildings' worth of doors and rang nobody, and the reason is not that she did not need to and not that she forgot. A woman of about thirty-one was rung four times that morning on three subjects and not one of the four callers asked her what had happened in that building on the Friday night, and she has told nobody that she noticed.** **That is what the dependence looks like from the outside: three competent people in three buildings, each right not to ring, about nine hours of work about twenty minutes slower, and no resentment anywhere, and about four people in this city can see it and not one of them has said a word about it to anybody.** He attended eleven of eleven, finished eleven of eleven, **and is behind for the first time in this volume** — behind on the count of what happened in this city on Friday night. He does not know that he is behind. **He does not know about the coin under the door, or the bell, or the kettle, or nine people going down four storeys, or a table in a ground floor that did not happen, or nine numbers read in the dark, or a man of twenty-nine at a rail for four hours with a torch he had not switched on. Nobody told him; four people have not told him and are not going to; and he has about four days in which not to see it, at the end of which he is further behind.**

- **339 — Wednesday of week 120, entry 342, *The Nineteenth Sitting*.** **The Exchange, the nineteenth sitting, the third of this volume's five and the second of the three at which the book opens, and the book opens.** Nine chairs in a curve in a room above a line in Saltmarket, the ninth against the wall with its back to the room and not moved back, a tin that has never had a lock on it, a book with four columns and forty-nine lines in it when the morning starts. Nine people, including a man of about forty-four, ninth attendance, who asked for something four weeks ago and was given nine words that were not it, a young man of twenty-one who keeps a count he has never given her, and a young woman of twenty-one who has never been asked anything. **At about ten past ten the telephone rings and she answers in about two seconds and says her own name, and a person says one thing to her, and she does not ask them to say it again, does not ask them a question about it, does not write while they are talking and does not repeat it to anybody in the room. It takes about nine seconds, and then she says thank you, and then she is quiet for about four seconds, and then she writes a line in the book with a pen she takes out of a bag. The line is in the book and is not in this entry. Nobody in that room asked what it was or who it was. The book is at fifty lines because somebody said a thing to her face at about ten past ten, and there is no other way a line gets into that book and there has not been for nineteen years.** Then, unasked, as it comes: **fifty to sixty-six, one and three in four weeks to the day, the telephone was ahead before and is ahead now and the direction and not the difference, and twenty-four, of which nineteen are the nineteen Wednesdays this book holds and five predate it on a sheet she has not shown anybody in nineteen years. Nothing was converted; no call became a line and no line became a sitting; the telephone is sixteen ahead of the book where it was fourteen ahead four weeks ago and eleven ahead eight weeks ago, and the direction has not changed sign, and the difference is not a number of sittings.** **Nobody asked her for the rule and she gave nothing in its place — not said, not refused, not paraphrased, not alluded to — and the man of about forty-four, ninth attendance, sat down and did not ask, and about four people noticed that he had not asked and he was not asked about that either.** At about half past eleven she says one unasked thing about not having explained herself, and a young man of twenty-one says one word and is told that was not the same thing and says he knows. **Nobody in that room raised the seven days that had gone before it: the building that was dark for four hours on the Friday night, the count on a piece of paper in Crown Terrace, the man of fifty-eight's sentence a week earlier, about nine people who stopped telephoning a man of twenty-two, and the man of twenty-two. None of it was raised, none of it was asked about, nobody in that room knows about any of it, and this entry places the facts side by side and joins them in no way.**

- **340 — Thursday of week 120, entry 343, *Nobody Asked Him*.** **THE CLOSE. He takes a difficult share off a person who did not ask him to, and it is the worst thing he does in the volume, and it is because he is behind, and the being-behind is a consequence of the route through himself.** At about half past ten on the Thursday morning, which is not a thing he does, in a room off a service road, with about nine people, he asks for it — the only part of the four minutes anybody could defend — and in about four minutes gives the four buildings, and about nine hundred doors, to a man of about thirty-three. He says, in his order: that four buildings with about nine hundred doors have to be asked about about nine times a week once the first four hours are over; that he had done about four of the nine a week for a month and about four other people had done the rest; that it had not worked and had worked for about three weeks for everybody except him; that the man has a gate and a building and a shift, knows the staff of two of the four, and made four calls unaided to three other districts three weeks ago and got an answer in about nine minutes, and is the only person in this case who has done that without him; and that he cannot do four buildings and a gate and a shift and eleven jobs and is behind. **And then, in about nine seconds, unasked and to the room: that the four buildings are his, and that he is telling four people in a room instead of asking, because if he asked in front of them the man would say yes because they were in the room, and he would be right to say yes, and he would rather he said it to his face than to theirs. He did not ask him. Nobody has asked him about that sentence and nobody is going to.** The man of about thirty-three says yes in about four seconds, correctly: a gate and a building and a shift and four buildings is a Thursday afternoon and a Tuesday afternoon and about four evenings; he has the gate at night; he wants it in writing; and a man of twenty-two is the only person he knows who can put a thing in writing that anybody will keep. **Nobody thanked either of them. It is the correct thing to do, which is what makes it the worst thing: the share is real, the man can carry it, nobody said no, and a person who has spent four years refusing to be a burden handed a burden to a person who did not ask for it. He is not a villain and nobody in that room thought he was.** **And the reason is not a reason anybody could have argued with: he is behind because four districts made about four hundred small decisions through him for about eleven hours on a Thursday in an earlier fortnight, and what came out of it was not that the junction held but that about nine people worked out they did not have to work things out, and the working out has not come back. What he did on that Thursday morning is the thing four districts did to him. It took about four minutes, it was correct, and it is the same shape, and he has not said so and nobody in this case is going to say it to him, and this entry says it once and about him.** A first-year of nineteen, at the counter, says three words in about four seconds to nobody, and about four people in the room hear them, and they are right, and nobody improves on them, nobody thanks her, nobody repeats them, and they are recorded as not a finding, not a mechanism, and not to be written down by anybody. **And Talia Venn was four miles away at about half past ten in a workroom of her own with about four people in it, doing her own Thursday; she was not in that room, was not told and did not ring. She found out at about half past five from a person with an ordinary job who had come about forty minutes on a bus and had asked correctly and had been told correctly, and who then said one thing in about four seconds, unasked, to somebody who had not come for it. About four seconds. Six words. Said to the visitor and not to him; he is four miles away, does not know he said it, and is not going to be told. She has not rung him and is not going to ring him about it. It is not a declaration, not a refusal, not a resolution and not a scene. Nothing was resolved, and the relationship is colder at the close of this movement than at the start of it, and the resolution is held to a later volume.** Nobody thanked anybody. A man of about thirty-three took four buildings home on a Thursday afternoon and has them, and has not been asked whether he wanted them and did not need to be, and about nine people in this city now know that he has them, and one of them put them there.

**THE THREE CHAPTERS OF THIS MOVEMENT THAT A LATER PASS SHOULD LOOK AT FIRST ARE 332, 334 AND 338, NAMED HERE BEFORE THE PASS AND NOT AFTER IT, WHICH IS THE FOURTH MOVEMENT IN A ROW IN WHICH A SUMMARY HAS DONE THAT. THE CLAIM THAT THEY CAME BACK CLEAN WAS FALSE AND IS WITHDRAWN: the review repair pass found the batch's one blocking defect in 338, and one of its own new findings in 334, and 332 was clean.** 332 is the lowest on the eight-word overlap in the batch at forty, 334 is thirty-two, and 338 is the lowest on the apparatus share at forty-three and two tenths.

**AND THE ANCHORS AT THE CLOSE OF CHAPTER 340, WHICH A LATER PASS SHOULD CHECK AGAINST THE FILES AND NOT AGAINST THIS LINE.** The book is at fifty lines; the telephone is at sixty-six; the count has been announced twenty-four times; his appointment still carries a reference, a count, a reason and no date; the hardboard is unchanged at nineteen lines in thirteen hands and fourteen names, the first line is a cook's, and the newest is a man of twenty-nine's, is eleven words, is signed, is the only promise on it and is sixty-three days old; the panel has been spent exactly once and the marker once; the heating is on, costs more, four names on a lease cost more, and the fortnight a cook paid for is unpaid by anybody and is not resolved. **AND ONE ANCHOR THAT IS A CONFLICT AND NOT A FACT: the batch prompt for this movement states three times that the book stays at forty-nine lines at the nineteenth sitting and the calendar gives fifty, and the chapters carry fifty. The superseded figure is forty-nine and the owner of the reconciliation is the Volume 07 close.**

# VOLUME 07 — MOVEMENT IV (Chapters 331–340), *The Blackout* — CHAPTER SUMMARIES AFTER THE REVIEW REPAIR PASS

**Dated after the Movement IV block above and supersedes it for the four sentences in it that were false and for the figures. `outline/volume-07.md` governs. Load-book entries 334 to 343, one per chapter, continuous with the 333 at Chapter 330. The ten chapters were not restarted and no beat moved. The full account is section 13 of `workspace/volume-07/batch-0004/SUMMARY.md`; the review is `reviews/volume-07-batch-0004.md`.**

**THE MEASURED FIGURES AFTER THE PASS ARE 32,140 words, 166 bold spans at 5.165 per thousand, a worst chapter of 6.484 at Chapter 333, a true hedge of 885 at 27.536 per thousand, an eight-word overlap mean of 68.4 and an apparatus share of 44.54 per cent, and the two superseded sets are 32,228 / 5.151 / 6.442 / 886 at 27.492 / 68.8 / 44.68 and 33,968 / 6.801 / 8.000 / 948 at 27.909 / 189.6 / 47.52. A WRITER WHO QUOTES ANY OF THE THREE SHOULD QUOTE THE FIRST.**

**WHAT CHANGED IN EACH OF THE TEN CHAPTERS, AND NOTHING ELSE CHANGED IN ANY OF THEM.**

- **331.** Untouched.
- **332.** Untouched. It is the lowest on the eight-word overlap in the batch at forty and the review pass came back clean on it, which is one of the two of three named chapters that did.
- **333.** **The man of forty-one's visit is about half an hour in the record and was two hours; the body said eleven and half past eleven twice and did not change.** Two sentences that reported the record's own compliance with the plan are cut: that the plan of record requires him once and this is the once, and that a schedule of about nine hundred lines is not a message. Nothing else moved.
- **334.** **A doorway two days ago is the day before.** Two sentences cut: the record's statement that the post at the back of that room is not described in this entry for the third Friday running, and the record's statement that a report nobody wrote is not a message. The body of the chapter keeps the third-Friday beat because the plan requires it in prose and the rule is about documents. **Its overlap fell from thirty-six to thirty-two and it is still the second lowest in the batch.**
- **335.** **The table runs about eleven appointments in a morning in the body and in the record.** Cut: *the woman of twenty-four is absent from this record and has said nothing in it*, which was false in the record that said it and which named her two clauses earlier.
- **336.** **The four days are now in front of him and not behind him, in the body and in the record, and the two remaining working days are named; the Wednesday evening is no longer the fourth day of the rest of his week.** The sheet reached four buildings nine days before that morning and not about a fortnight.
- **337.** **The panel and the marker are untouched and are still here and only here.** The record of the nine hands now says that the man of about thirty-eight said nothing at all, which is what the body says and what the record did not. The paragraph that itemised, as a record of its own compliance, four things that did not happen is cut and replaced with what did happen. A clause placing a voice said live in another part of the city next to a lift that had stopped is cut, and it also carried an interval wrong by twenty-seven days. The cost record now says about eleven appointments in a morning. Two stray asterisks removed. **Nobody decided anything that night and the nine are still nine.**
- **338.** **THE BLOCKING ONE. The sentence is now in her mouth, once, on its own line, and the narrator's paraphrase of its subject is gone; the question she was asked is a question about the list on the paper and carries none of the sentence's subject.** The four days are up and the sentence still points to the end of the week. The doorway is about a fortnight old. The withheld calls are about six a week for about three weeks, which is Chapter 340's own arithmetic. The Monday of week one hundred and twenty is eighteen days after the midpoint Thursday and says three weeks and not a month.
- **339.** Untouched in every respect but four stray asterisks at the ends of four load-book paragraphs, which rendered as literal marks and which the published parity matcher cannot see.
- **340.** **The being-behind is the consequence of the Thursday three weeks before this one, and the phrase *four days into this volume's earlier fortnight* is gone because it was neither parseable nor true.** The four minutes and the record of them, and the arithmetic of being behind, all say three weeks where they said a fortnight. Her one document-shaped finding was spent about six weeks ago and is Chapter 318, and the chapter said three weeks in two places. The record now says that its three buildings and zero are one Monday inside its nine people and are not a tenth figure. **The share is still the worst thing he does in the volume, the man of about thirty-three still says yes in four seconds because it is correct, she is still four miles away and still not in the room, and nobody thanked anybody.**

**AND THE ANCHORS AT THE CLOSE OF CHAPTER 340, WHICH A LATER PASS SHOULD CHECK AGAINST THE FILES AND NOT AGAINST THIS LINE, ARE UNCHANGED BY THE PASS: the book is at fifty lines; the telephone is at sixty-six; the count has been announced twenty-four times; his appointment still carries a reference, a count, a reason and no date; the hardboard is unchanged at nineteen lines in thirteen hands and fourteen names, the first line is a cook's, and the newest is a man of twenty-nine's, is eleven words, is signed, is the only promise on it and is sixty-three days old; the panel has been spent exactly once and the marker once; the heating is on, costs more, four names on a lease cost more, and the fortnight a cook paid for is unpaid by anybody and is not resolved. THE ONE ANCHOR THAT IS A CONFLICT AND NOT A FACT IS UNCHANGED TOO: the batch prompt states three times that the book stays at forty-nine lines at the nineteenth sitting, the calendar gives fifty, the chapters carry fifty, the superseded figure is forty-nine, and the owner of the reconciliation is the Volume 07 close.**

**AND THE THREE CHAPTERS A LATER PASS SHOULD LOOK AT FIRST ARE 332, 334 AND 338, AND THE CLAIM THAT THEY CAME BACK CLEAN IS WITHDRAWN: the pass found the batch's one blocking defect in 338 and one of its own new findings in 334, and 332 was clean. One in two, for the fourth movement in a row, and the naming is still worth the line it costs.**
