# VOLUME 07 — MOVEMENT V (Chapters 341–350), *The Federated Hour* — CHAPTER SUMMARIES AT THE CLOSE OF THE VOLUME

**Dated after the Movement IV block above and supersedes it for nothing. `outline/volume-07.md` governs. Load-book entries 344 to 353, one per chapter, continuous with the 343 at Chapter 340, and the run that began at entry 304 in week one hundred and nine ends here. The full dated account with the matchers beside every figure is `workspace/volume-07/batch-0005/SUMMARY.md`, and the measured figures for the ten files are 31,338 words, 160 bold spans at 5.106 per thousand, a worst chapter of 6.122 at Chapter 347, a true hedge of 862 at 27.507, an eight-word overlap mean of 35.6 and an apparatus share of 44.32 per cent.** **This block was written after the review pass and had taken the corrected word count of 31,423 into a set whose hedge and apparatus share are the pre-pass figures of 862 at 27.507 and 44.32, so that no single set of files in this volume ever produced all five numbers on this line. The word count was the intruder and the figure of record in this block is 31,338, which is the set the addendum below quotes as superseded, and the set of record for the volume is 31,424 and stands in the addendum.**

- **341 — Monday of week 122, entry 344, *Four Hundred Small Decisions*.** A man of about thirty-three has held four buildings and about nine hundred doors for eleven days and has found out what four hundred small decisions cost, and he says so on the telephone in about four minutes, about himself and not about anybody. A woman of thirty-four who checks about nine hundred doors comes into a room off a service road instead of telephoning and asks a man of thirty-three for one of the four buildings in front of about nine people, and he is standing eleven feet away, and she does not apologise and does not explain. **And a man of about forty-six who signs for work that has been done, eleven years and about nine a day, stands at a counter and says the true thing about a decision in about nine seconds: nine were done, he could not tell you how one of them was done, and the second of the two ways through a decision was cheaper. He is on no list, is not in this case again, and nobody improved on it and nobody thanked him.** Marek is behind by thirty-two days and about nine people in this city can now see it.

- **342 — Wednesday of week 122, entry 345, *Nine People And No Order*.** The four buildings lose their light again for four hours, from the top down, on an old timer, and no stairwell is lit because the count of four hours expired nine days ago and no office has replaced it. **The question is not how to prevent it and is how a thing that was done by hand once is done by hand twice without anybody copying the first time, and the answer is that it got out of one stairwell and into about four people, at a counter, at a door and on a landing, in about four seconds each, and nobody called it anything.** Nine people are asked one at a time in a room off a service road and give nine answers and not one of the nine is a first step, and one of the nine did nothing and asked that it be recorded. **A man of fifty-one with a tool bag says the one thing he cannot do: he can report what he did and cannot report how, and has not done it the same way twice in about four hundred buildings, and cannot write it down, cannot show it, and the next one will not be the same.** Nobody is asked a second question. The man of about thirty-three says he will go on asking people *whether* they will do it again and not what to do, and has asked nobody yet.

- **343 — Thursday of week 122, entry 346, *Four Words*.** **He is asked whether he wants it, and the asking is four words long, and he answers with something true that is not yes.** In a corridor outside a workroom four miles away, at about eleven, in about four minutes. He has thought about it every day since the Thursday of week one hundred and seventeen, which is about five weeks and is longer than he has thought about anything, and what he arrives at is that she is the only person he has ever said a true thing to in a room. He says the sentence he has said to her six times in about four years and meant as a promise on every one of the six, and she does not react to the number. **She states a limit in about four seconds and does not ask him about it: if you decide something and tell me after, that is a Tuesday; if you decide something and tell me before, that is a decision you made twice and I will not be in the second one. He accepts it in about two seconds, and then, in about nine seconds, asks her whether a ground floor is free on the Friday morning, and she says that is a different question and he says he knows.** Nobody in that workroom was told anything. Nobody thanked anybody.

- **344 — Friday of week 122, entry 347, *The Ninth Friday*.** The ninth Friday in a company training room in Crown Terrace, about nine folding chairs and about eleven people, seven on the company's list and about four on none. **A woman of thirty-six who calls the run stands up at about half past seven and makes no sound for about nine seconds, and nobody reads a number out of a sheet, and nobody touches anybody's back, and nobody counts.** At about nine seconds the man of thirty-four who buys the training and has never called a run in his life says one word, and it is the wrong word, and about four people move, and nine people finish the floor in about nine minutes and about forty seconds. Nobody said why it went, nobody thanked him, nobody asked him anything, and he was not asked a fifth time. **About four buildings in this city were without light from about five until about nine that evening and the chapter does not join the two facts.** The injury is answered in a room, by about eleven people, without anybody naming it, and no sheet and no form and no line on any hardboard exists.

- **345 — Monday of week 123, entry 348, *The First Yes*.** **A woman of thirty-one with a key in her pocket, the only person in this volume ever asked to carry anything, is asked to be at her own door on the Friday night for about four hours, and she says yes in about four seconds.** She had the key out of her pocket for the whole of the four minutes. Her condition is not a condition and is the whole of it: if she says no at that door, nobody comes to find out why, not the asker and not anybody in the room, and she is not to be asked about it afterwards and will say nothing. There are about nine of them, asked separately in nine places over two days, none of whom knows what the other eight agreed to, and about four of whom have carried something in this case already. **He says the thing he says — *Then I'll take the middle* — and a man of about thirty-three says no, in one sentence, naming the Thursday of week one hundred and seventeen and about six weeks ago, and eighteen days ago a room in about four minutes, and nine people who said yes that week and none of whom said yes to him. He does not argue.** He asks what is needed and is told nothing. Nobody thanked anybody.

- **346 — Wednesday of week 123, entry 349, *Four Minutes, And Four Hours*.** **THE VOLUME CLIMAX, FIRST HALF.** A room off a service road has two ways in front of it. The first is about an hour and works, and requires about eleven people to be out of six buildings for about a week. The second is about four hours, is nine people and nine doors and a building at a time, and every one of the nine can stop, and might not work. **About eleven people lose their week either way, about four of the eleven are in that room, and all four of them say so out loud, and the four minutes are not about that and everybody in the room knows it and nobody says it first.** A woman of twenty-four, a man of twenty-one, a man of fifty-one with a tool bag and a cook of twenty-seven, with about four others. A man of twenty-two says the only difference between the two and does not choose and was not asked to vote and did not vote, and then sits down for about four hours. The room decides in about four minutes and there is no vote, no count of hands, no minute and no document.

- **347 — Friday of week 123, entry 350, *The First Knot*.** **THE VOLUME CLIMAX, SECOND HALF, AND THE TENTH FRIDAY ROTA.** About nine folding chairs and about eleven people in a company training room, and the woman of thirty-six begins the run herself after about nine seconds and it goes. Nine doors open at about nine o'clock. **A link at a door in the second of the four districts says no at about ten, on purpose, having said on the Monday that he would, explains it to nobody, and hands the door to the woman of thirty-one with the key, who takes it without being asked and does not ask why.** At about eleven he telephones a man of twenty-one, and the man on the other end of the knot agrees before he is asked — *You are going to ask me to stop* — and they cut the oldest arrangement either of them has, in about four minutes, and neither of them says a word about it since. He then walks about nine people two miles through the Lower Wards on his own feet, gets the count wrong twice, sits down on a step and cannot get up the first time, and is told in four words that he is the slowest thing in the group. **A second door is shut on purpose at about half past eleven and is handed to a man of about twenty-seven who lives in that building, who cannot read the plate in the dark and does not need to. The two people who put the night back together have not been in the same room.** The load comes down between about half past twelve and about one. Nobody knows the exact moment. No name is attached to any of it.

- **348 — Monday of week 124, entry 351, *What Was Lost, On A Counter*.** **A piece of paper about the size of both hands together, in a woman of twenty-four's handwriting, with eleven lines on it, each line about a week, four of them in four other hands. It is not a form and it is not a minute and it has no reference, no date, no heading, no print, no box, no signature, no second copy, no drawer and no system, and a person can pick it up and put it back and nobody can find it again.** About four of the eleven are in the room and about seven are elsewhere in the city with a week gone and nothing on paper. **A Continuity Office man of fifty-eight with a bad hip comes unasked, says in about nine seconds that he can put a reference, a count and a reason on it and that not one of the three is a week, says that he has looked since the Saturday and has not found anybody who can put a week back, asks for the paper correctly with the reason and the holding time given, is refused, does not ask again, makes no remark about a pity or a compromise, and writes nothing down in a job in which he writes things down.** At about seven one of the nine is asked to do it again and says no in about four seconds and nobody says anything and nobody stops her.

- **349 — Wednesday of week 124, entry 352, *The Twentieth Sitting*.** **THE EXCHANGE, THE TWENTIETH SITTING, THE FOURTH OF THE VOLUME'S FIVE AND THE SECOND OF THE TWO AT WHICH THE BOOK IS SHUT, FOUR WEEKS BEFORE THE CLOSE.** A person telephoned on the Tuesday about a room in which about nine people had arranged to be at nine o'clock on the Friday night and had each been asked, and the caller got about nine seconds out and stopped. **She does not write it, and she says why in about four seconds, and then says one more unasked thing in about nine seconds: that in nineteen years she has written down about nine people and has written down a room, and that she is not going to say anything else about that this morning.** One of the nine begins a sentence and does not finish it, and about four people noticed, and what he was going to say is not in this entry and is not on any surface. The book is at fifty lines before and after. The telephone moves from sixty-six to sixty-nine and the count from twenty-four to twenty-five, the direction is given and the difference is not, and nobody asks her for the rule and she gives nothing. The ninth chair is against the wall.

- **350 — Wednesday of week 128, entry 353, *The Federated Hour*.** **THE CLOSE. Four weeks after a book stayed shut.** In a room in Crown Terrace, about nine chairs, a table, a clerk of forty-four, a woman of sixty whose job is to ask questions and who asks none, and a chair of forty-nine. **The condition on his share is quoted whole and word for word, once, in her mouth, and she says that it is the reason the only thing anybody in this city could ask him for was a decision. The name of the public body is said out loud, once, in a mouth, and it is the first time in this manuscript.** The instrument is withdrawn; the stamp is used once and then pushed to the far side of the table; the withdrawal is a fact about who may ask and is not enforceable against anybody who did not write it down and the chair says she has not looked and is not going to. **The review is nine people, in about nine rooms, in about four districts, with a list, and it is not a committee and no minute of it is taken by anybody including the person whose job is to ask questions.** At about ten past ten the twenty-first sitting opens: a caller says one thing to the holder's face in about nine seconds, the book moves from fifty to fifty-one, the telephone from sixty-nine to seventy-two, the count from twenty-five to twenty-six, nothing is converted, the rule is not asked for and is not given, and the ninth chair is against the wall. **And in the back room of a converted shop unit off a service road, with the door shut, in about four minutes, she says she is not going to be the person he comes to and is not going to say when, and the reason is not that he is wrong: he said *I can carry it* and meant it as a promise six times in about four years, and she has watched him mean it that way six times, and every one of the six was about a thing nobody had asked him to.** He says *I understand* and she says *you understood in nine seconds last month and then you asked me about a table.* No scene, no kiss, nobody thanked anybody, no date on the condition, no return path promised. The hardboard is nineteen lines in thirteen hands and fourteen names and the newest is one hundred and eighteen days old and is still the only promise on it.

**THE THREE CHAPTERS OF THIS MOVEMENT THAT A LATER PASS SHOULD LOOK AT FIRST ARE 349, 350 AND 347, NAMED HERE BEFORE THE PASS AND NOT AFTER IT, WHICH IS THE FIFTH MOVEMENT IN A ROW IN WHICH A SUMMARY HAS DONE THAT.** 349 is the highest in the batch on the eight-word overlap at ninety-three, 350 is the second highest at sixty-four and the longest at four thousand three hundred and thirty words, and 347 is the highest in the batch on the bold figure at six point one two two and the second highest on the hedge at thirty-two point six five three. **The claim that the named chapters came back clean is NOT made, and in the four previous movements in which the naming was done before the pass it produced a result three times in four, one in two, one in two and one in two. The honest statement is that naming them is worth the line it costs and that this file does not know what the pass will find.**

**AND THE ANCHORS AT THE CLOSE OF CHAPTER 350, WHICH A LATER PASS SHOULD CHECK AGAINST THE FILES AND NOT AGAINST THIS LINE.** The book is at fifty-one lines; the telephone is at seventy-two; the count has been announced twenty-six times, of which twenty-one correspond to the twenty-one Wednesdays this book holds and five predate it; nineteen years; his appointment still carries a reference, a count, a reason and no date; the hardboard is unchanged at nineteen lines in thirteen hands and fourteen names, the first line is a cook's, and the newest is a man of twenty-nine's, is eleven words, is signed, is one hundred and eighteen days old and is still the only promise on it; the heating is on and costs more and the fortnight a cook paid for is unpaid and unresolved; the ninth chair is against the wall and was not moved back; the panel and the marker were spent once in this volume, in Chapter 337, and not here; nine keys are needed and two exist and the first cannot be asked back by anybody; and the word naming the public body was said once, by the woman who holds the chair of forty-nine, in a mouth, in Chapter 350, and by nobody before it.

**ADDENDUM TO THE BLOCK ABOVE, DATED AT THE VOLUME CLOSE, AND IT CORRECTS ONE WORD OF IT.** The figures paragraph in the Movement V block above said the apparatus share is "under Movement I's 44.75 by forty-three hundredths of a point." **That comparison is between two different quantities and is withdrawn. The volume close re-measured all fifty files of this volume and published both quantities for all five movements, and the finding is that Movements I and II published the mean of their ten per-chapter apparatus shares and Movements III, IV and V published the aggregate.** Movement I's aggregate is 44.796 and its mean is 44.748; Movement II's aggregate is 48.100 and its mean is 48.178; Movement III's aggregate is 41.789 and its mean is 41.820; Movement IV's aggregate is 44.540 and its mean is 44.721; **Movement V's aggregate is 44.317 and its mean is 44.234, and it is the lowest in the volume on both.** **Movement V is below Movement I by forty-eight hundredths of a point on the aggregate and by fifty-one on the mean, and it is below Movement IV by twenty-two hundredths on the aggregate, and all three margins are small and are published as small.** The superseded margin in the paragraph above is forty-three hundredths of a point and it is withdrawn, and the corrected pairings are at section 14.2 of `workspace/volume-07/batch-0005/SUMMARY.md`. **No chapter of this volume is affected, because no chapter has ever printed this figure; it has lived in state files and batch summaries and nowhere else, and every one of the five state files carries this correction.** Owner: the case, and a Volume 08 writer should use the aggregate and say which quantity it is using.


**AND A CLOSING ADDENDUM TO THE MOVEMENT V BLOCK ABOVE, DATED AFTER THE REVIEW PASS, WHICH CORRECTS THREE FIGURES AND TWO CLAIMS IN IT AND ADD ONE FINDING.** The review pass over `workspace/volume-07/batch-0005/` returned twenty-six findings, two of them blocking, and this block carries three of the corrections. **FIRST, the announced count's decomposition: nineteen and five is twenty-four, and the pattern is that the announced count is the number of sittings the book holds plus five, so it is twenty and five at the twentieth sitting and twenty-one and five at the twenty-first, and the nineteen was a carry-forward from the nineteenth sitting and appears in this block and in the prompt of record and is superseded.** **SECOND, the interval to a man's ask is twelve weeks at Chapter 349 and sixteen weeks at Chapter 350, and the eight weeks that stood here is the true figure at the nineteenth sitting and not at either of these.** **THIRD, the four figures after the review pass are 31,424 words, 160 spans at 5.092, 863 true at 27.463, an overlap mean of 35.6 and an apparatus share of 44.24, and the set this block printed before the pass — 31,338 words, 5.106, 862 at 27.507 and 44.32 — is superseded and stands beside it.** **AND THE FINDING: a review pass found eight more interval defects in the ten chapters this phase did write, and every one of the eight was a figure that had been supplied in the prompt or derived from a figure that had been supplied, and the two that were not derived from a supplied figure were both in a mouth and both were the holder's own arithmetic. Twelve interval defects in this volume's last movement, of which this phase's own walk found six and a review pass found eight, and not one of the fourteen was found by reading the ten chapters against the day map without the calendar in front of it.** The whole account is section 16 of `workspace/volume-07/batch-0005/SUMMARY.md`, and the volume close's calendar repair now runs to two rows, the nineteenth-line running age and the thirty-day interval, both repaired in place with the superseded text quoted beside each.

# VOLUME 08 — BATCH 0001 — NO CHAPTERS WERE WRITTEN, AND THIS BLOCK SAYS WHY IN ITS FIRST LINE

**`outline/volume-08.md` does not exist, is not in this repository, and the Volume 07 close did not write one and was not asked to. A batch with no plan of record and no day map would have to compute every interval figure, and an interval that has to be computed is an interval that will be wrong, so this phase wrote the hand-off and no chapters.** The dated account is `workspace/volume-08/batch-0001/SUMMARY.md`. **There is no chapter summary in this block because there is no chapter, and the honest entry for a phase that wrote nothing is the reason.**

**WHAT THE PHASE DID INSTEAD, ALL OF IT MEASURED ON VOLUME 07'S FIFTY FINISHED FILES AND NOT TAKEN FROM A SUMMARY: it re-ran every match this repository has and reproduced all four volume-wide figures to the digit — 163,618 words, 1,005 bold spans at 6.142, 3,942 true hedge at 24.093, apparatus share 44.678 aggregate and 44.728 as the mean of the fifty per-chapter shares — and all five movement rows on all five published quantities.** Load-book entries run 304 to 353, continuous, no duplicate, no gap; **Volume 08 opens at entry 354.** The walk of the day map returned all fifty rows reproducing, all twelve anchors reproducing, and the card-minus-room invariant holding at exactly four on every one of the fifty chapters. The superseded volume-wide set is 163,532, 6.146, 3,941 at 24.093 and 44.692 and is not to be reintroduced.

**THE NEXT PHASE IS `workspace/volume-08/batch-0002/PROMPT.md`, AN OUTLINE PHASE THAT ALSO WRITES NO CHAPTERS.** It owns `outline/volume-08.md` and `workspace/volume-08/ARITHMETIC-AND-CALENDAR.md`, and it must produce both before Chapter 351 exists. The first writing batch of Volume 08, Chapters 351 to 360, may not be created until it has.

# VOLUME 08 — BATCH 0001 — THE REVIEW REPAIR PASS, AND NO CHAPTER SUMMARY IS ADDED, CORRECTED OR REMOVED

**This pass wrote no chapter, so there is no chapter summary to add, and it is recorded here because a reader of this file counts chapters and not passes and should not find a gap in the count.**

**All fifty Volume 07 chapter entries above stand exactly as written. Two findings of the review touched this volume's files and neither was a chapter: the bare digit 3 is inside District 3 twenty-four times and not seventeen, the fourteen of them in Chapter 323, and the frame-heading walk found the alternate closing heading in three files and not two, with Chapter 326 carrying both headings.** Chapter 326 is therefore the one file of the fifty that a walk on either heading alone would misdescribe, and the three Exchange chapters 326, 339 and 349 are the three whose sittings differ in outcome: “one book that did not open,” “one book that opened,” “a book that stayed shut.” **No chapter was reopened, no entry number moved, and the load-book run is still continuous from 304 at Chapter 301 to 353 at Chapter 350 with no duplicate and no gap.**
